# This is NOT the Next.js you know

This version has breaking changes — APIs, conventions, and file structure may all differ from your training data. Read the relevant guide in `node_modules/next/dist/docs/` (resolved from this file's directory; in monorepos the `next` package may not be visible from the repo root) before writing any code. Heed deprecation notices.

This block is written and re-added by `next dev` — verify at `node_modules/next/dist/server/lib/generate-agent-files.js`. Removing it from a diff only re-creates the uncommitted change; committing it with your work keeps the tree clean.

<!-- END:nextjs-agent-rules -->

# Design system

Material 3. The tokens live in `app/globals.css`; the primitives live in `components/`.
**Never re-type either at a call site** — that is how the app ended up with three card
radii, five scrollbar appearances and 29 hand-copied primary buttons.

## Use the primitive, not the class string

| Need | Use | Never |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Button | `components/Button.tsx` — `type="button"` unless the call site says `submit` | a `<button>` with `bg-primary text-on-primary …`; a command inside a form left at the browser's default, which submits it |
| Text input / textarea | `Input` / `Textarea` / `Field` from `components/Input.tsx` | a bare `<input>` with border + focus-ring classes |
| An arbitrary colour field | `ColorSwatch` (`components/Input.tsx`) — a badge's own colour, in /admin | a bare `<input type="color">` |
| Choosing a theme colour | `PaletteSwatches` — one call site, /settings, each theme a borderless tile of itself in miniature (`PaletteTileFace`); the eleventh tile's own two dialogs are `ColorPicker` and `ImagePalettePicker` | a `Select` of colour *names*; `ColorSwatch` for any of the eleven, since the OS dialog speaks RGB where this system speaks HCT and shows a colour where a *theme* is being chosen; a disc per theme — a disc is the shape of a colour |
| Radio button | `components/Radio.tsx` | a bare `<input type="radio">` |
| Slider / range | `components/Slider.tsx` | `<input type="range">` plus a global class |
| A one-time code | `components/CodeInput.tsx` | six `<input maxLength={1}>` and a ref array |
| On/off setting | `components/ToggleSwitch.tsx` — `layout="row"` in a list | a `justify-between` div with a bare switch in it |
| Card / section surface | `components/Card.tsx` — `interactive` makes it a `<button>` | `bg-surface-container-… rounded-… p-4` |
| Dropdown (picks a value) | `components/Select.tsx` — `label` makes it a form slot (M3's outlined exposed dropdown) | a hand-rolled absolutely-positioned menu; in a form, a filled `Select` under a separate caption |
| Suggestions under a text field | `useCombobox` (`lib/useCombobox.ts`) with a `Popover` | a hand-rolled keydown handler; a first row pre-selected, so Enter picks it instead of submitting |
| A screen that needs an account, signed out | `components/SignInRequired.tsx` | opening the login dialog on arrival |
| A tab only an account has, on a public screen | leave it out of `Tabs` and `TabPanes` signed out — /messages is 公告 and 系统 (decision 22); a link naming it lands on the nearest tab offered, its address corrected, and a sign-in adds it in place, opening it if the link asked | a `SignInRequired` pane in each personal tab |
| Hiding the engine's focus outline | `focus-visible:outline-hidden` beside the ring | `outline-none` — it also removes the outline forced colors needs |
| Menu (runs a command) | `components/Menu.tsx` | a `role="menu"` div with no keyboard support |
| Any other floating panel | `components/Popover.tsx` | a fifth recipe for corner + elevation + border |
| Dialog | `components/Modal.tsx` | a hand-rolled scrim + panel |
| A dialog's action row | `Modal`'s `footer` prop | your own flex row — two `fullWidth` buttons in one cannot fit, since `buttonClasses` always emits `shrink-0`, and a row inside the body scrolls away with the content |
| A tick in a selection control | `components/CheckGlyph.tsx` | `MdCheck`, or a second copy of the same path |
| "Are you sure?" | `useConfirm` (`components/ConfirmDialog.tsx`) | `window.confirm`, or a `Modal` + 4 useStates |
| Asking for one value | `usePrompt` (`components/ConfirmDialog.tsx`) — `rows: 1` is an `Input`, where Enter confirms | `window.prompt`; a resizable textarea for a one-line name |
| Copying to the clipboard | `copyText` (`lib/utils.ts`) | `navigator.clipboard.writeText` with no fallback |
| A random id | `randomId` (`lib/utils.ts`) | `crypto.randomUUID` — it exists only in a secure context, and the site is also served over plain HTTP, where it threw |
| A share link | `createShareLink` / `trackShare` (`lib/api/share.ts`) — a short link that falls back to the long one, and the share counted | a `fetch` to `share.php` at the call site |
| Reading the session | `useSession` for rendering (`ready` gates login prompts); `readUserInfo` / `readToken` for event-time reads; `updateUserInfo` for token-checked response writes (`lib/hooks.ts`) | reading localStorage in a render initializer, or overwriting a session from a stale response |
| Formatting a date | `lib/format.ts` — every shape the app prints (full, short, date, month-day, clock, compact, day label, relative, last online), read and printed as Beijing time with `zh-CN` fixed — except three columns the backend writes in UTC, on the original front end's evidence: 浏览历史's `view_time` / `last_view_time`, 词库编辑历史's `created_at` and the maintenance password's `updated_at`, read by `parseBackendUtcTime`; a backend epoch whose unit is not pinned is read by magnitude (`epochStamp`, `lib/adminSiteTools/model.ts`: ≥ 1e11 is milliseconds); `useNow()` (`lib/hooks.ts`) for anything relative | `toLocaleString` with an option object at the call site, or `new Date()` on a backend string — it carries no offset, so the browser read it in its own zone |
| Grouping rows by day | `beijingDateKey` (`lib/format.ts`) — the Beijing calendar date is a day group's identity; its printed label is only its label | a group keyed on its label: a page whose days were not contiguous had two 「今天」 sections under one key, and React duplicated rows on every delete |
| A count printed exactly (coins, quota, a ledger) | `formatExactCount` / `formatSignedCount` (`lib/format.ts`) | `toLocaleString()` at the call site, or `formatCount`'s 万 rounding where the number is the point |
| A figure an admin sets (price, stock, balance, order) | `figureText` / `figureField` (`components/admin/figures.ts`) — absent prints `—` and opens empty | `Number(x) || 0` |
| A failure's sentence, and whether to offer 重试 | `apiErrorMessage` / `isRetryable` / `isNotFound` (`lib/api/errors.ts`) | `error.message` straight out of a `catch` — `Failed to fetch` reached the UI that way |
| An admin write whose answer was lost | `CatalogOutcomeUnknown` (`lib/api/adminCatalogTools.ts`; site-tool writes throw its subclass `WriteOutcomeUnknown`): no answer, a 5xx other than 501, or a 2xx without a boolean `success` — held by `useCatalogMutation` + `MutationResult` as 操作结果待确认 until 我已核对记录, announced and with focus kept — and, once acknowledged, given back where it was when the outcome arrived (`origin`, or the caller's `returnFocus`) — never retried | a refusal sentence for a dropped connection, or a press left free to mint a second invite |
| Gender / race options and labels | `lib/profileFields.ts` | a second option list — three vocabularies once wrote one column |
| A PicPony asset URL | `getAssetUrl` / `getAvatarUrl` (`lib/utils.ts`) | `` `https://picpony.top/${path}` `` — three join semantics were in use |
| Bounding a number | `clamp` / `clamp01` (`lib/utils.ts`) | `Math.max(a, Math.min(b, v))` |
| An admin API call | `import * as adminApi from '@/lib/api/admin'` | `api.adminXxx` — `api` is a runtime spread, so it cannot tree-shake |
| Bottom sheet | `components/Sheet.tsx` — `onExited` for work that must wait until it has left | a `Modal` on a phone |
| Icon-only control | `components/IconButton.tsx` — `variant="media"` on a photo | `p-2.5 rounded-full` around a glyph |
| Icon control on the app bar | `IconButton` `variant="on-primary"` | a 48px box repeating `focus-ring-on-primary` |
| A close / dismiss control | `IconButton` `dismiss` | hand-writing the quarter-turn hover |
| A hover/focus label | it is automatic — `IconButton` shows its own | `title=` on an icon-only control |
| A label on anything else | `useTooltip` (`components/Tooltip.tsx`) | `title=` where a real description is meant |
| A caption or secondary control revealed on hover | `hover-reveal` under its `group` target | `sm:opacity-0` — wide touch screens have no hover |
| An icon's size | `ICON` (`lib/icons.ts`) — `size={ICON.standard}` | a number picked against the glyph beside it |
| An icon inside a Button | nothing — the primitive sizes its own slot | a `size` on the icon; author CSS beats the svg's `width` attribute, so it is inert |
| An icon inside a Badge | nothing — `Badge` sizes it at 14 (`sm`) / 16 (`md`) | `ICON.dense`; 18 makes the badge 2px taller than one without an icon |
| Heading above a card or list | `components/SectionHeading.tsx` — `level="group"` for a run of rows within a list (a day of /history, of the coin ledger) or a well of chips in a form (`TagWell`, the pending library), its count in `aside`; the region it heads is named by it — `aria-labelledby` its id (a heading with `actions` takes the id on a span in its children, since its own `id` lands on the wrapper); an inline editor's panel is the named region and the form inside it is not; a standalone form is named by its heading | an `<h2 class="text-title-m-…">` written out; an `aria-label` restating the heading under it |
| Tag / status pill | `components/Chip.tsx` | — |
| Mark beside a name | `components/Badge.tsx` — `tone="media"` on a photo | an inline `<span>` with a container pair |
| A user's earned badges: the mark, the wall, equipping | `UserBadge` for the mark; `BadgeWall` (each opens its description) and `BadgeEquipDialog` on a profile; `lib/userBadges.ts` reads both wire spellings | a second `equipped_badges` parser, or a `Badge` with a click handler |
| A thumbnail's corner mark | `MediaBadge` (`components/Badge.tsx`) — `corner` owns inset and concentric shape | a `Badge` with a hand-written corner recipe |
| An image collection, including profile uploads and favourites | `MasonryGrid` / `ImageGridSkeleton`; `MasonryGrid selection` for batch mode, with selection controls beside the image link | square thumbnail grids with their own links or click handlers |
| Unread count | `CountBadge` (`components/Badge.tsx`) | a hand-clamped `99+` pill |
| Role beside a username | `components/RoleBadge.tsx` | `roleInfo(x).chip` on your own `<span>` |
| Nothing here / it failed | `EmptyState` / `ErrorRetry`; `entrance={false}` where the caller fades the swap that brought it (a claim's result after its press); `statusViewBox(size)` for a placeholder in a status block's shape | a centred `<p>` in a `<div>`; a skeleton re-typing the block's floor and padding |
| A route-level `loading.tsx` | `PageFallback` (`components/ImageDetailSlot.tsx`) around its markup | a page-shaped fallback that can render in the `@imageDetail` slot — Next gives every slot of a layout that segment's `loading.tsx` |
| A progress meter | `components/ProgressBar.tsx` | a `h-2` div with an animated `width` |
| A table of records (the admin console) | `components/DataTable.tsx` — real columns when wide (one grid, every row a subgrid of it), labelled 名称：值 pairs when narrow, chosen by its own width; row-shaped skeletons, `errorDetail` beneath the failure title, `className` on every column including `primary`; rows come and go with presence — a leaver stays in the flow (a subgrid cannot leave it), its footprint collapsed, held and fading while the rows after it and around the table glide; pass `listKey` (`usePagedRows`, `useShownListKey`) so a page turn or filter replaces the rows outright; a leaver that held the widest cell of an `auto` track leaves the track held at that width (a floor) until the list is replaced outright | a header row of labels over rows that wrap on their own, aligned with nothing |
| A file drop target | `components/DropZone.tsx` — the target is a sibling button under the zone's content; `filled` is a quiet surface (`surface-container-high`, solid `outline-variant` edge), the brand tone is for `dragging` only | a `role="button"` wrapped around content that holds its own controls |
| A tab row | `components/Tabs.tsx` — `variant` picks the shape | a button row with `role="tab"` and no arrow keys |
| Tabbed panes | `TabPanes` / `TabPane` (`components/TabPanes.tsx`) | `{active === 'x' && …}`, or a `key` on the panel |
| Rows that come and go in a list (a delete, its undo, a day emptied) | `PresenceList` (`components/PresenceList.tsx`; `DataTable` has its own, for its subgrid rows) — a leaver keeps its place while it fades, the rows after it glide up, an arrival fades in; a press lands any glide in flight. A single block that leaves as a list fills (its empty state) is `PresenceBlock` | a row unmounted in the frame it was removed, with everything below it jumping up; an empty state cut out as the first row arrives |
| A box whose height follows what it holds (a card around a changing list, a dialog whose body swaps, a heading that loses a line) | `FollowHeight` (`components/FollowHeight.tsx`) — `watch` when the parent re-renders for reasons that cannot change the height; a box that swaps what it shows for something else is `SizeSwap` (`app/upload/SizeSwap.tsx`) | a `transition` on `height`; a box that snaps to its content in the commit |
| The fade at the edge of a horizontally scrolled row | `useScrollFade` (`lib/useScrollFade.ts`) | a copy per screen |
| Chat message | `components/ChatBubble.tsx` | a radius picked by eye |
| Sharing a picture, a favourites folder or a privacy space with somebody | `useShareToContact` (`components/ShareToContactDialog.tsx`) — `{ share, shareDialog }`, render `shareDialog` once | a second contact picker, or an `[image_share:…]` string written at the call site |
| Looking people up by name | `useUserSearch` (`components/ShareToContactDialog.tsx`) | a debounce of your own around `search_users` |
| Following a tag (订阅 / 已订阅) | `SubscribeButton` (`components/subscriptions/SubscribeButton.tsx`) — the live Derpibooru count first, an alias followed to its tag, the list corrected in place; a subscription's address is `subscriptionHref` | an `add_tag_subscription` at the call site, or a count of 0 recorded for an alias |
| Overlay behaviour | `lib/overlay.ts` hooks | a second copy of the focus trap / scroll lock |
| Back closing a surface | built into `Modal` and `Sheet` (`closeOnBack`); `useHistoryLayer` (`lib/historyLayers.ts`) for anything else — a modal drawer, the lightbox | a `popstate` listener of your own, or a `pushState` that is not a layer entry |
| A transient status report | `showToast` (`components/Toast.tsx`); `{ action }` for the one tappable part | a toast that takes pointer events, or an `alert` |
| Press feedback | `data-ripple` + `state-layer` | an active-scale utility |
| Component motion | `spring-*` / `spring()` / `springTiming()`; anchored menus use `MENU_TRANSITION` and `menu-enter` / `menu-exit` | a duration and a curve chosen independently |
| Entrance for a block | `components/Reveal.tsx` — plays on mount; see note below | a hand-rolled fade, or a scroll trigger |
| Scrolling to an element | `scrollAppToElement` (`lib/scrollTo.ts`) | `el.scrollIntoView({ behavior: 'smooth' })` |

**There is one tab control and it is `Tabs`.** The app once had four tab controls: three declared no ARIA roles at all, and the one that declared `role="tab"` implemented none of the contract that role promises — no arrow keys, no roving tab stop, no `aria-controls`. /tasks, which copied the structure (`useSlidingIndicator`, an absolutely-positioned indicator, a `data-tab` per button) before the primitive was reachable, reintroduced the defect `TabBar`'s own comment recorded as fixed: an active tab distinguished by colour with no weight contrast. `variant` covers all three shapes (`underline`, `pill`, `rail`) and `tone` covers /tasks' amber indicator. `activation="manual"` is for a row whose panels each start a read when shown (the admin rail): arrows move focus only, Enter or Space selects. A row that scrolls keeps the selected and the focused tab in view (its own scroll, never the page's) and fades at whichever edge hides more tabs; the underline indicator sits on the divider, covering it. The roving stop is rendered once — the server's markup and the hydrated tree have it on the selected tab — and moved afterwards in the layout effect that scrolls the selected tab into view, after that effect's read. Written by React during a switch, `tabindex` on the tab that had just taken the focus recalculated style synchronously in the middle of the commit's mutations (5–41 ms at 4× CPU on a development build). `scripts/testPresenceFocus.mjs` holds it to zero React writes per switch.

**A tooltip is not `title`, and an icon button no longer needs to ask.** `title` gives the OS font at the OS size with no token in it, a delay the page cannot set, nothing reachable on touch — and no `aria-describedby`, because `title` is a last-resort accessible *name* rather than a description, so a screen reader either read it in place of the label or ignored it. `IconButton` renders an M3 plain tooltip from its own `aria-label` (or `title`, or an explicit `tooltip`) and drops the native attribute, so there is only one bubble; anything else that wants one takes `useTooltip`. On touch a long press (500ms, cancelled by travel or a scroll) shows it, it stays Compose's 1.5s after the finger lifts, and the click that ends the long press is swallowed — except on a link, whose long press is already the platform's link menu; under a pointer the bubble is hoverable (WCAG 1.4.13) — leaving the control grants a short grace to move onto it.

`useTooltip` is a hook rather than a wrapper component on purpose: a wrapper must either clone its child — which breaks on any component that does not forward a ref — or introduce a box of its own, which changes layout. A control already owns its element and ref, so handing it props is simpler and layout-neutral.

The tooltip's first portal mount uses `@starting-style` for opacity zero. Its ordinary FastEffects transition then handles both directions from the current opacity; a transition without an initial style would animate dismissal but hard-cut every first appearance.

`title` is still right for one thing: a hint on content that *already shows its text* — a truncated tag name, a relative timestamp's absolute value — where the attribute supplements rather than names.

**`Modal`'s `footer` is one flex row, and it takes a leading member through `mr-auto`.** The row is `flex flex-wrap justify-end gap-3`, so the ordinary case is "spell the buttons in order and they land at the trailing edge". A member that belongs at the *leading* edge — /settings' 重新发送 beside 稍后 and 验证 — takes `mr-auto`, not a nested flex wrapper: an auto margin in a `justify-end` row absorbs the free space to its right. The row wraps as a guard — a fourth action or a longer label then takes a second line instead of being clipped by the panel, which is how the /block-groups row failed. A dialog with two branches gives `footer` a conditional rather than moving the row back into the body.

**Under a header, the 16dp between the headline and the content is 8 + 8** — the header's bottom padding and the body's top padding — not 12 + 4. A labelled field's floating label sits 6px above the field's box, and with 4px of scroller above it the first field of every editor dialog lost the top of its label's glyphs. The total did not move.

**Equal-width actions only grow on the row axis.** In a `flex-col sm:flex-row` action group, use `sm:flex-1` on the buttons and their skeletons. Unqualified `flex-1` changes the column's height basis to zero: Derpi's 56dp buttons collapsed to their 24px text line, while the empty skeletons collapsed to 0px. The column's default stretch already supplies full width. Derpi's actual pair uses `@lg:flex-row` / `@lg:flex-1` inside a query container: its two labels need 32rem of content width, and the tablet sidebar means viewport width cannot promise that.

**An overlay reverses from its current pose.** `Modal` commits its in-flight WAAPI pose before changing direction; `Sheet` initializes below the viewport only on its first placement, and a drag cancels the settling tween before taking over. Separate entrance/exit keyframes restarted a half-open panel from its full endpoint. A modal's scrim and its panel are siblings, each fading on the one clock, and the panel alone takes the transform: nested, their opacities multiplied and the content lagged behind its scrim. Standard travel is 16px, reduced travel 8px; the existing 400ms enter / 200ms exit pairing still scales with the user's speed. **There is one scrim at a time**: a dialog that opens while another is still leaving (确认 answered, and the run it started opening its progress dialog) takes the leaving one's scrim over at its current opacity, and the leaving panel finishes its exit one step above it — two scrims crossing for 130ms read as a flash of darker page.

**`Card interactive` renders a `<button>`.** A `<div>` with a cursor, a state layer and a ripple is a control no keyboard can reach and no screen reader can name — a prop that produces an inaccessible control is worse than an absent one.

**`Card as="a"` exists** so card-shaped `<Link>`s need not hand-roll the whole recipe: an anchor takes the same block/left-align/focus treatment a button does, it just never takes `type` or `disabled`.

`Avatar` has the matching escape hatch: `unoptimized` renders a native `<img referrerPolicy="no-referrer">` for a host that is neither in `next.config.ts`'s whitelist nor willing to serve a request carrying a `Referer` — do not grow a second avatar component for it. `priority` is for the one avatar that is a page's largest paint (a profile header): eager at high fetch priority, which is what Next 16 replaced its own `priority` with.

Its `size` is a union — `32 | 40 | 48 | 56 | 'hero'` — rather than `number | string`, so the ladder is a constraint. `'hero'` is for the one avatar that changes size at a breakpoint and cannot be sized inline at all (an inline `width` beats any class); the 96 → 128 pair lives in the component.

**Four primitives stand a default down when the call site names its own** — `Skeleton` for a radius, `Badge` for `max-w`, `ProgressBar` and `Field` for `w-full`. `Field` only stands down for an unconditional `w-*`; a breakpoint width such as `sm:w-32` keeps the full-width mobile default. The pattern matters because `cn` is a plain join: emitting both leaves Tailwind's output order to pick, which is not a decision anyone made.

**`Select`'s trigger is as wide as its widest option, not its current one** — a combobox that resizes when you pick a value re-lays-out the row under the pointer that just chose it (/settings' content filter, options 完全安全 (Safe) and 中等限制 (Spoilers), did exactly that). Every label is rendered into one grid cell with all but the selected one `invisible` and `aria-hidden`, so the browser's own intrinsic sizing takes the max and it stays true when the options change. No measurement, no `min-w` to remember. With a `label` it is a form slot — the outlined `.m3-field` shell a labelled `Input` wears, the label floating into the notch at 56dp — so a form with a dropdown in it is one label system rather than a notched field beside a captioned filled box.

**`ToggleSwitch`'s press comes from pointer events, not `:active`.** On a touch screen the browser owns `:active` — Chrome delays applying it and holds it for a minimum period afterwards, so the handle could still sit at its pressed width after finishing its travel. Off a real `pointerup` the shrink and the travel start in the same frame and, both being `FastSpatial`, land together; `pointercancel` and `pointerleave` are part of it, or a press that turns into a scroll leaves the handle swollen. Under a finger the press waits for the tap timeout like every other press (below), so scrolling a settings list past a switch neither swells its handle nor pulses it.

`Spinner`'s colour is one `tone` axis — `primary` / `on-primary` / `inherit`. `primary` resolves to **primary-ink**, a foreground over a secondary-container track, not the pale primary fill. Built-in foregrounds now clear 4.5:1 on that track; exact custom themes retain their established ink policy.

Press feedback is `data-ripple` plus the `state-layer` utility; there is no `usePressable` hook — nothing in the repo defines one. Press lives in `spawnRipple` (`lib/ripple.ts`) and the `state-layer` utility in `globals.css`, and `Button`/`IconButton` already carry both. **A finger's press waits for the tap timeout**: `trackPress` holds a touch's wave for Android's 100ms, plays it at once on a quick tap, and lets it go when the touch travels past the slop or becomes a scroll — a wave on every `pointerdown` flashed under every flick of a list; mouse and pen stay immediate. A control whose hit area is larger than what it paints carries `data-ripple="inner"` and paints its wave into a `[data-ripple-host]` first child, so its own box does not clip its target (see the touch-target note below).

**There is no scroll-driven reveal, and that is a decision rather than a gap.** `useScrollReveal` in `lib/motion.ts` is gone: it never had a call site, because an entrance cascade is for *picture* content — the gallery already has one in `useStaggerGridOn`, ordered by visual position rather than DOM order — while on text it makes rows the reader came for behave like an animation. It was also the only ScrollTrigger consumer, and `lib/motion.ts` registers its plugins at module scope, so the plugin shipped in the root chunk of all twenty routes to serve nothing.

`<Reveal>` is therefore the app's one general entrance helper, and it plays on mount — a *client* mount: an instance hydrating server HTML stays still, since that block has been on screen since the first paint and an entrance would hide it and fade it back (the maintenance screen is the case). A below-the-fold reveal, if ever genuinely wanted, does not need ScrollTrigger — an `IntersectionObserver` rooted on the app scroller is a dozen lines, and `PicDetail` already has two. Four constraints carry over:

- **Never above a gallery card** — it parks targets at a `y` offset and the hero flight reads `getBoundingClientRect` on press.
- **Never inside a leaning pane** (`TabPanes lean` — the home route, /policy, /messages, /settings, /favorites, /tasks and the profile tabs) — a block on screen there takes a transform animation of its own for the run, and a reveal would move the same node on a second clock.
- **Never inside any tab pane whose content swaps** — /user/[id], /messages, /favorites, the home route: the pane transition and the reveal would animate the same nodes on two different clocks. `StatusView` asks `useInTabPane()` (`components/TabPanes.tsx`) and knows on its first render; it used to find the pane in a layout effect, after a `Reveal` had already mounted and started.
- **Never over content that already cascades on mount** — a second entrance on top of an existing cascade is the "动画重叠" failure, not extra polish. /tasks uses `TabPanes lean` with no separate row cascade. /admin's panels instead use plain `TabPanes`, with no extra entrance on their contents.

**An interactive element may not be nested inside another one.** A `<button>` inside an `<a>` is invalid HTML, and it fails invisibly: the gallery card's spoiler cover was a click-handled `<div>` inside the card's `<Link>`, so the only focusable node was the link — Tab landed on it and Enter navigated to the picture the cover exists to hide. Lift the second control out to be a *sibling* of the link and position it over the top. Where the enclosing element is genuinely the convenience target (the image detail's media box, which zooms on click), keep the container's handler as a pointer affordance and put a real control inside it, so the pointer path and the keyboard path both exist. `DropZone` is built that way: its click and keyboard target is a sibling button laid under the zone's content, which passes the pointer through except on controls of its own — so /upload's 移除文件 is a separate, reachable button rather than a flattened child of a `role="button"`. A drop is checked against its `accept` — a mismatch goes to `onReject`, or, without one, on to `onFile` so the caller's own validation says why rather than the file vanishing — and a drag of a known wrong type shows as refused instead of inviting the drop.

**One hidden control is pointer-only on purpose: /about's wordmark.** Ten quick presses open the developer guide (`DeveloperGuideModal`); there is no keyboard twin and no visible entry. The secret is the developer password, not the door, and a keyboard user reaches developer mode through an administrator (`admin_enable_developer`); a hidden global key handler would be a shortcut nobody could discover either, and a visible entry would publish a mode that relaxes the content filter (G4-029).

For an overlay that has to stay mounted through its own fade-out, `inert` (React 19) is the one attribute that removes it from the tab order and the accessibility tree together; `aria-hidden` alone leaves a focusable element inside a hidden subtree. The same attribute is how an open modal hides the page: `lib/overlay.ts` sets `inert` on `[data-app-root]` while any modal layer is open — `aria-modal` alone is a hint some screen readers ignore.

**The image detail over a list is modal to the list, not to the app.** It fills the content area and nothing else, so the app bar and a docked drawer stay live beside it — exactly as they are on the detail's own page, which a direct visit always had. Only the list under it goes `inert` (once the picture has landed); making the chrome inert as well left a drawer and an app bar on screen that no click reached. So its layer is `containFocus: false`: focus enters on open and returns to the card on close, but Tab may leave for the chrome and nothing pulls it back, and a dialog opened from the chrome (登录) returns focus there. The section is a non-modal dialog — no `aria-modal`, which would fence a screen reader out of chrome a pointer can use. The skip link lands in the detail while it is up. A drawer row for another page navigates from the detail as a link inside it does (Back returns to the picture); the row of the very page the detail covers — and the wordmark over the home gallery — is its 返回 instead, as re-selecting the current destination is in a native navigation bar: the picture flies home and history keeps no second copy of the page (`onReselect`, reaching the overlay's own close through `requestImageDetailClose` in the hero runtime).

**A detail without a hero flight still owns its original background.** `lib/detailBackground.ts` records it after the router commits. Back from another route must recover that picture entry's source, not the page just left: otherwise reselecting the sidebar's incorrectly highlighted destination closes the picture. Native history-entry keys distinguish repeated image URLs; without the Navigation API, a read-only identity of Next 16's route segments and parallel children keeps the query-bearing page segments and ignores mutable refresh hints. It writes no history state or entries. An open detail retains its origin while a temporary drawer/dialog entry is above it. The shell and browser regressions cover repeated opens of one image from two searches and Back with the Navigation API unavailable.

**Back closes the topmost overlay, and nothing under it** (owner decision 1). Every `Modal`, `Sheet`, confirm/prompt and the lightbox owns one *same-URL* history entry while open — a copy of Next's own state plus a `__picponyLayer` marker — so the system gesture, the browser's button and `history.back()` peel one layer. Four rules make it safe, and each is a bug the first version had: **a pop between a layer entry and the entry beneath it is consumed** (same URL, same tree: Next's `ACTION_RESTORE` for it would discard a pending navigation, the R10-001 hazard), by a capture listener and by the hero driver's own first line; **closing any other way steps back off the entry**, so history never keeps a dead entry that makes the next Back do nothing; **a navigation from inside a layer supersedes the layers** — the write is held, the entries unwound, the layers asked to close, and the write replayed on the page's entry, so "confirm, then navigate" leaves `[page][new page]` with no call-site code; and **a stale layer entry** (reload, restored tab, Forward onto a closed dialog) is stepped off silently. The image-detail overlay is **not** a layer — the hero ladder owns its entries and reads straight through a layer above its guard; anything that traverses history itself awaits `settleHistoryLayers()` first (the hero's queue and the home tab pill both do). A surface that refuses to be dismissed (Esc off, an upload running) swallows Back rather than obeying it.

**A gesture never outlives the surface's state.** The phone drawer's swipe (`useDrawerSwipe`) writes inline styles for as long as a finger or its release owns the panel, and every ending hands them back: a release; a change made elsewhere meanwhile — Back (on a phone the edge Back gesture reaches the page as a drag on the open drawer before the system takes it), Esc, a row, a route change — after which the panel springs from where it is to React's state and the release finds nothing to do; `enabled` flipping and unmount, whose cleanup runs after `context.revert()` and clears what the revert leaves (GSAP's resting `translate3d(0,0,0)` with `translate: none` outranks the class). Before, a Back during the drag was undone by the release (the drawer reopened and pushed its entry again), and a revert left a closed drawer standing on screen, inert. `npm run test:drawer` holds every ending in Edge.

`EmptyState` and `ErrorRetry` are two presets over one `StatusView`, so a list that is empty and a list that failed have one silhouette. Both take `size="page" | "pane" | "inline"` — match the enclosure, because a half-viewport block inside a 120px well makes the well scroll.

**`inline` is one sentence and no glyph.** The default tray came to ~155px in wells that cap at 120 — the empty state itself made them scroll; at this size the words *are* the empty state, and a call site with something better to show can pass `icon`. `StatusView`'s outer wrapper is `w-full` for the same family of reason: in a flex enclosure it was a shrink-to-fit item pinned at the start of the row ("暂无标签" against the left edge of a `flex flex-wrap` well), and the obvious call-site fix cannot work — `className` lands on the inner shell, whose 100% resolves against the collapsed wrapper.

**Every** "nothing here" / "that failed" goes through them, including the ones that do not look like a list: the 404, the route error boundary, the image detail's failure, an admin table's empty body.

**`fill` is for the screens whose entire content is the block** — the 404, the route error boundary, a profile that does not exist or failed to load (both profiles), a forum thread that does not exist or failed to load, and the image detail's failure in both of its presentations. `page`'s own floor is half the viewport, right under a page header and wrong when there is nothing else on screen; a list's empty state must not set it, or the page grows past the viewport. A `fill` block's title is the page's `<h1>` — it is the whole page.

**`fill` drops `page`'s `min-h-[50dvh]` floor**, and that is not tidying — a floor beside `flex-1` is a competitor, not a backstop. `[data-page-content]` has two in-flow children, the content wrapper *and* the footer (≈190–240px), so `flex-1` divides the space **above the footer**; once that space falls under 50dvh the floor overflows the column and the block lands at the top of the scroller. The block still centres in the region it owns rather than in the viewport, so with a footer present it reads slightly high by design.

The image detail needs threading rather than a prop: `fill` is `flex-1`, so every box between the block and the scroller has to be a flex column, and in the overlay presentation that chain runs through `.image-detail-overlay-content` and the container transform's own fit target — `renderDetailShell` takes a flag for it. It is `flex-1`, not a percentage height, and that is not a style choice: a percentage `min-height` only resolves against a *definite* parent height, and every box between here and the scroller gets its height from flex distribution, which Chrome treats as indefinite — `min-h-full` computed to `auto` and centred nothing. It also needs `[data-page-content]`'s inner wrapper to be a flex column, which it is.

**"Complete" is not "loaded".** `HTMLImageElement.complete` is true for an image that has **failed**, so a `complete` check alone marks a dead image as ready — `FadeInImage` removed its shimmer and left a blank card. The predicate is `complete && naturalWidth > 0`.

And it has to be **assigned, not raised**: the check runs on every change of the layered loader's `displaySrc` and must clear as well as set, or a card that showed anything once never shimmers again and returning to a page whose images had already failed once brings the cards back bare. `npm run perf:pageturn` asserts that count stays zero — every card in view must be showing a shimmer or the give-up plate.

**Loading is the destination's own shape, not a spinner.** A list loads as `Skeleton` rows in the row's own geometry, a grid as `ImageGridSkeleton`, a card as a card. A `Spinner` is for an action in flight — a submit button, an upload, an inline "querying…" — not for a page: a centred dot says "something is happening somewhere" and then reflows the whole screen when the content lands. The test is whether there is a destination shape *yet*, and the shape has to actually match: a placeholder whose spacing or thumbnail differs from the card it stands in re-spaces and shifts the list the moment content lands — the one thing a skeleton exists to prevent. `.skeleton` sits in `@layer components` in globals.css, and that is load-bearing: unlayered, it beat every utility, so a call site's `absolute inset-0` never applied and every positioned shimmer in the app — each gallery card, the banner, the detail's media well — measured 0×0.

**The route fallback is the page column's, never the picture slot's.** Next gives every parallel slot of a layout that segment's `loading.tsx` (the loading data is stored on the parent segment and applied to each of its slots), so `app/loading.tsx` is also the `@imageDetail` slot's fallback — and that slot is rendered in the content host beside the scroller, not in the page column. Whenever the slot waited on a Back or Forward the router cache no longer held (on the dev server, after any saved file), the page silhouette was laid out at the host's top-left, gutterless, its positioned bars painted over the page already on screen — "the skeleton left behind". `AppLayout` wraps the slot in `ImageDetailSlot`, and `app/loading.tsx` wraps its markup in `PageFallback`, which renders nothing inside it: the slot stands for nothing on every route but a picture, and the picture brings its own loading state. A per-slot `loading.tsx` would not do it — the boundary that catches is the slot's first segment's, which takes the root's loading data.

**A name still being asked for is a placeholder in the name's line, not the raw value renamed a moment later.** `useTagNames` answers a name, `null` (show the tag itself) or `undefined` (still asking, at most 1.2s); a caller holds the slot with a `Skeleton` in that line's type role and fades the answer in (`NameFade`), never on mount. Answers accumulate for the screen's life, so a row on its way out keeps the name it showed. A write that is about to show its own row may ask beside the write and wait briefly (the subscribe's 500ms) so the row arrives named. A placeholder for content of variable length takes the commonest shape, measured — the tag dialog's entry is one line of description because that is the plurality of live entries — and every other shape moves by its difference on `FollowHeight`.

**A removal decides where its focus goes, in the commit that removes the entry, on every tier — and `PresenceList` does it.** It is read before the commit, while the leaving entries are still in the DOM (under 关闭 the commit removes them outright). A focus inside a leaving entry goes to the same control of the entry now in its place, else of the one before it, else to `fallbackFocus()` (a control is focused; a heading is a landing). The control is counted among the entry's controls whether or not they are available, so a switch the row disables while its delete is out does not shift the count. A focus already lost — on the document, or inside the confirmation that is leaving, because the dialog's own return a frame later races the list's commit — is placed the same way only for a removal the call site claimed: `presence.current?.claimFocus([key])` immediately before the request or the write that removes it, and its returned release if the removal does not happen. A live focus is never moved. A removal nobody asked for (a re-read) moves only a focus that was inside the entry it took away. The dialog's later return finds the focus placed and keeps it (`lib/overlay.ts`). `PresenceBlock` takes `fallbackFocus` too, for a block that leaves with the focus inside (the cart's last line leaves in its list's block). An empty state that replaces the last row is the press's answer — `entrance={false}` and `animate-page-transition` — or `Reveal`'s staggered start holds the heading the focus lands on `visibility: hidden`. The same holds when content leaves without a dialog (a docked cart's 结算 after a purchase). /history keeps its empty state inside the list as a `PresenceBlock`, so the last row leaves where it stood and the outer list's `fallbackFocus` finds the empty heading in the same commit; 清空记录 (a reset, which `claimFocus` cannot cover) lands there in the commit the empty state arrives in; and 撤销, pressed in a toast that then leaves with the focus, hands it to the first restored row in the restore's own commit (`useHistoryDeletes`' `onRestored`).

**A profile's pictures are the same gallery.** `MasonryGrid` owns the natural aspect ratios, column distribution, gaps and `ImageCard` hero links. The columns are placed by CSS, not counted in JS: `lib/masonry.ts` computes each card's slot for all three counts (2 / 3 / 4) as custom properties and a media query picks one, so the server's HTML is already the phone's two columns and the desktop's four, and the cards are one flat list keyed on the image id that a breakpoint change moves rather than remounts. A JS count was four columns on every server render — the largest layout shift in the app on a phone. A profile's uploads (its bound Derpibooru account's `uploader_id:` search — PicPony has no uploads action), the Derpibooru profile's and the favourites screens use it directly, each paged list handing it a `createPagedSequence` source so 上一张 / 下一张 cross its pages and the return flight turns the list to the picture's page; a profile's 收藏夹 tab lists its public folders instead, each opening its own page. In a swapping profile tab, pass `entrance={false}` to the grid and its `ImageGridSkeleton`; the pane already moves, and this does not disable opening an image. A profile's tab reads when first selected and **keeps its key after** — gated on "selected" alone, the pane sliding out turned into its skeleton. The skeleton's `count` is the request's page size, and its aspect mix is a real page's (mean 0.93 height over width, measured over 250 uploads; it was 1.17, so a grid lost a quarter of its length the moment its pictures replaced the placeholder). A list with nothing yet still has that length: `PagedImages` lays its placeholder out at once and shows it after the usual deferral — at zero length for those 180ms the page shrank under the viewer, and a switch to a view still loading (本站讨论) measured the arriving pane at 0px, so the tab machinery clamped the page to it and pulled the viewer up 360px at 1440 and to the top on a phone. A list may provide only media geometry: `ImagePreview` carries that contract without invented vote counts, and missing metadata waits for the detail response.

**Favourites select pictures in the same grid.** `MasonryGrid selection` supplies active state, selected ids, toggle and long-press callbacks without changing card geometry or the hero path. Space toggles, Shift extends, and Escape or Back leaves the selection history layer. `/favorites` offers 收藏夹, Derpibooru and optional 隐私空间 through `TabPanes lean`, with marked skeletons and no nested Reveal. Its folder, public shared-folder and signed-in shared-privacy routes use `PageBack`: `/favorites/folder/[id]`, `/favorites/shared/[username]/[folderId]` and `/favorites/privacy/[ownerId]`. A public shared folder needs no account.

**A page's floating bar is one object: `SelectionBar` and the shop's `CartBar`.** Always mounted and `inert` while hidden; it rises on `spring-default-spatial` and fades on `spring-fast-effects`, leaving on effects only; while it leaves it shows what it last showed. Its 80px of room is a spacer of its own *after* a zero-height sticky slot, and the bar hangs from the slot, so the room can open on `defaultEffects` without carrying the bar — at the page end the footer glides down under the arriving bar instead of dropping 80px — and the room is held after the bar leaves until a scroll puts it below the viewport (M1-004's clamp). Its layer is `z-page-chrome`.

**Every grid is a list, a sequence and a page of counts, without asking.** `MasonryGrid` renders a `list` of `listitem`s with `aria-posinset`/`aria-setsize` (DOM order is feed order); hands each card the list 上一张 / 下一张 walk (`sequence`, or its own page when a caller passes none — `lib/imageSequence.ts`), which the card names with `openFromSequence` on every activation before the hero launches; fetches PicPony's comment counts for its page once (`siteCommentCounts`, shown as `+n`, never waited for — a caller that has them passes `siteComments`); and renders a new page in a deferred pass (`useDeferredValue`), so fifty cards never land in one task. A card is named by its tags (`describeImage`), never by `name`, which is the upload's file name. Its spoiler cover is opaque, names the tags, reveals on one press with focus moved to the link first, and keeps the link `inert` while it covers it. The server paints only the first eight cards' pictures and none of the animated ones (whose rendition depends on the device); the rest mount after hydration. A dead picture ends in a plate saying 图片加载失败 with a 重试 beside the link, never inside it.

**A page turn is deferred; a change of list is not.** `MasonryGrid` — and the home lists' `PagedImages` above it — render a new page of the *same* list in the background (deferred, then mounted a chunk at a time), but the rows of a *different* list (`listKey`, else `sequence.key`: a settings change, a cached Back to another search) at once. Deferred, the previous list's rows stood under the new caption for a render — rows the user may just have asked not to see — and a restored offset was clamped against their height. *At once* is not *whole*: fifty cards in one commit was one long task, so the new list mounts its first chunk, is checked against the viewport before the first paint (`slotsInBand`, from the placement's own numbers, after every layout effect of the commit so a parent's restored offset counts) and mounts there and then any slot on screen the chunk lacks — in place of the chunk when the viewport is deep in the list — and the rest follows a chunk a frame. A grid mounted on the client is handled the same way.

**Scroll anchoring cannot be relied on over the masonry.** Its cards are absolutely placed, and the browser's anchor selection among them is unreliable: swept at 1440 in 100px steps, a block inserted above the grid moved the cards under the viewer at 30–44 of 50 offsets — a different set on every run, with the slots contained or not; only near the grid's top did it hold every time. Content that can appear above a mounted grid late has to hold the offset itself, and correct only the movement that *happened*, since where the browser did anchor a blind correction doubles it. The failed page turn's inline error does both (`components/FailedTurnHold.tsx`, rendered after the rows by both profiles' upload grids; `PagedImages` still carries the first copy): the grid's offset is read before the commit (`getSnapshotBeforeUpdate`, hence a class) and after it — never from a scroll listener, which read layout on every frame of the glide and forced a layout per frame — and while the list's top is on screen the message reveals itself instead, since anchoring there *does* hold the rows and would put it out of sight above them.

**A card lays out itself, not the page.** Each `.masonry-item` carries its own height — `--mr`, the card's aspect, which the placement already stacks by — and size and layout containment, and `.masonry-grid` is contained the same way at the placement's height, so a picture decoding, a plate, a spoiler cover or the next chunk of a page lays out one slot or the grid and stops there. Uncontained, each such change dirtied the path to the document and every layout walked the whole page: a page turn on a phone at 4× CPU spent 1.5–2.0s in about fifty layouts of ~1,800 objects each (dev, R12-010). Two consequences to keep: a card is exactly its media box — anything added under the picture has to fit inside the aspect or it overflows onto the card below — and a slot has no *paint* containment, because the entrance cascade moves a card a few pixels outside it (`.image-card` itself keeps its own).

**A shimmer nobody can see does not run.** The compositor declines an animation with no visible effect, so an off-screen sweep ticks on the main thread instead — every frame restyles each such placeholder of a page still loading, forty-odd on a phone. `FadeInImage` pauses its sweep until the picture is within reach of the viewport (the same observer that arms its load timeout, one per scroll root and shared by every picture under it) and stops it once the picture is in. The placeholder itself is still drawn while paused, so a card in view is never blank.

**A background profile owns its route parameters.** Both the profile and the intercepted image route use `[id]`; `useParams()` follows the foreground and briefly turned `/user/1` into a read for user 3012 while `/pic/3012` opened. The replacement skeleton clamped the gallery's scroll before the flight finished. The local server page passes `id` to `ProfileContent`; the Derpi client page reads its own `params` prop with React `use`. Returning also selects a thumbnail in the active tab, since uploads and favourites may retain the same image in two mounted panes.

**Delete is the outlined bin** (`MdDeleteOutline`) in the destructive ink, in a row and in a menu alike — the forum thread's, the group rows', /history's and the favourites' menus. The filled bin was a heavier glyph for the same action on some screens and a different shape on others. A delete repeated down a list, one per row (/history), is `IconButton variant="danger-quiet"`: neutral ink at rest, the destructive ink while its row is hovered — when `hover-reveal` shows it, so a pointer sees what it always did — or while it has the keyboard's focus. Under a finger a page of rows was a column of red bins, and a removal 撤销 takes back is a trailing action, not a warning (OD-5).

**A selection control's label slot is `min-w-0 flex-1`** (`Checkbox`, `Radio`): in a row that spans its container the label owns the rest of it, so a long name truncates inside the dialog and a trailing part (a folder's count) lands on the row's trailing edge — the four folder pickers ran their names out of the panel with the counts beside them. In a control sized to its content it changes nothing.

`Badge` is a *mark* and `Chip` is a *control*. If it has no click handler and no dismiss cross, it is a `Badge`. A `Chip` with only `onRemove` (an input chip) renders its label as text and its cross as the one control, named `移除 {label}` by default — a row of crosses all called 移除 told a screen reader nothing.

**There are two text fields, and the label decides which.** They share the tone vocabulary, 56dp form height and `.m3-field` shell. The outlined field keeps its 4dp corner; the filled field takes an 8dp corner, matching `Select`'s default field shape. The search bar is the separate pill shape. The softer filled boundary is a deliberate project refinement, not a new M3 token claim.

A **labelled** field is a slot in a form: it has a name that has to survive being filled, and it gets M3's *outlined* field with the label floating into the outline. That is the whole point of the pattern: an empty field and a filled one stop being different objects — with a stacked label, a form of six empty fields is six blank boxes with captions floating between them, the caption belonging to the box below as close as the one above.

An **unlabelled** field is not a form slot — a search box, an admin filter, a chat composer, a thing you type into and act on immediately, whose placeholder is its whole identity. It gets the *filled* treatment: `surface-container-highest`, no border, no shadow; dressed as an outlined field it read as a form control whose label had failed to load, and it put the heaviest boundary around the least ceremonial thing on screen. The tone matches `Select`'s trigger and the component container table below. An unselected filter chip takes `surface-container-high`; it is a different control role, not an alternative field tone.

The notch is a real `<fieldset>`/`<legend>` pair, not a label painted over the border with a matching background: an M3 outlined field has no fill, so there is no colour to paint with, and a `<legend>` is the only thing in CSS that removes a section of a border. Two elements therefore carry the same words — the `<label>` the user reads and an invisible copy inside the `<legend>` whose only job is to be the right width. They stay in step because the legend's font-size is exactly 0.75x the label's and the label scales to 0.75 as it floats, 0.75 of `body-l` being `body-s`. All of that lives in `.m3-field` in globals.css, because it turns on `:focus-within` and `:placeholder-shown` matching against a *sibling*.

The label and legend explicitly own their font weight and tracking as well as their size; the legend's tracking is also 0.75x, so surrounding emphasized text cannot change the notch. Label travel and notch opening share FastSpatial; their ink, the field boundary, button materials and selection-control colours use FastEffects. The general transition utility remains the 200ms standard fallback, and the state layer keeps its own 150ms response.

The `<fieldset>`'s negative top inset exists to cancel the UA drawing the top border through the vertical centre of the `<legend>`, so it must be zeroed when there is no legend — left in, an unlabelled 44dp field painted its line 6px above its own box. When a field's geometry looks *slightly* wrong, measure the painted line, not the element's rect.

**Focus follows the field's boundary.** The outlined field has no outer ring: its own outline thickens to 2px in `primary-ink`, avoiding two nested boundaries. The filled field has no outline to nest inside, so it takes the ordinary `focus` ring. Both key off `:focus-within`, because the element wearing the indicator is the container and it is reporting on the control inside it. An invalid outlined field retains its error outline and label; on a filled field, the focus ring takes precedence over the error ring.

**An unlabelled field can carry its own actions.** `trailing` puts controls inside the box — /search's clear control (while there is text), 以图搜图 and the submit live there. It is a flow item, not an overlay: one button, two, or a button with a word in it all fit with no width reserved at the call site, and the control shrinks by exactly the slot's width because the slot refuses to shrink and the control's `min-width: 0` lets it.

**One inset, all the way round the slot: 8dp** — `(56 - 40) / 2`, the field's height minus the control's, so it is not a chosen number, it is the only value that centres the control. Two different insets around one object is what reads as "the button is not centred"; it also makes the concentric-corner rule checkable, since `inner = outer - gap` needs a single `gap`. And only **one** control in the slot carries a container — the primary action: a field's trailing icon is a glyph at `on-surface-variant` (`TrailingIconColor`), so 以图搜图 and the clear control are `standard` icon buttons (below `sm` the submit is its glyph alone, `responsiveLabel`); two filled containers inside one pill gave the secondary action the primary one's voice.

Judge it per field, though: a search box is one field and one action pressed once, so collapsing them into a single object helps; a composer is the thing you live in while typing, and /messages briefly burying send and emoji inside the field was worse — three plain targets became one crowded box.

`Input` has **one height for a form slot and one for chrome**. Both the labelled and the unlabelled field are M3's 56dp, and `size="lg"` is M3's *search bar* — also 56dp, but a pill — for the single field on /search that is the whole point of its page. Boundary and corner follow the field's role; height stays shared. A labelled field with nothing to suggest is given a single-space placeholder, because `:placeholder-shown` is what tells the label whether the field is empty and it only matches while a placeholder exists.

`size="sm"` is the other axis — 40dp with `body-m`, for a field that is *chrome*: an admin filter, a control in an `.m3-row`. It shares the 40dp height, 8dp corner and 16dp text inset with `Select size="sm"`; the dropdown uses `label-l` because its value is a control label, while typed text stays `body-m`. Both are 14px. M3 offers no 40dp field, so this is a **stated divergence**, argued by the enclosure. It is unlabelled-only, because the notch's legend width, float distance and negative fieldset inset are all derived from 56dp, and a labelled field lives in a form column anyway. Its `trailing` slot holds **one 32dp control** — the inset is the same formula as every size, `(40 − 32) / 2` = 4px, so "one inset for every field size" stays true; a 40dp control has no room there. `SearchInput` bakes the size in rather than offering it, since every call site of that component is a filter bar, and uses that slot for its own clear button (the engine's search cross, and its number stepper, are hidden inside every field). **Under a finger its typed text is 16px** (`body-l`, still a 28px line in the 40dp box): iOS zooms the page into any focused field set smaller, and the fix is the type size, never a viewport `maximum-scale`. A `type="password"` field brings its own visibility toggle in the trailing slot (`revealable`), and Edge's native eye is hidden, so there is one, the same everywhere.

A `Textarea` follows the same rule through its block padding rather than a fixed height, since it grows. Get the number by measuring: this app's `body-l` line box is 28px rather than the scale's 24, because the body line-heights run looser for Han glyphs (a documented divergence several sections down), so symmetric **14px** is what lands a one-row unlabelled textarea on `BARE_HEIGHT`'s 56dp. A derived number needs a line in the docstring saying what it was derived from, or the next scale change orphans it — 10px was correct while the unlabelled field was 48dp, and when 48 left the control-height scale the textarea was quietly left behind, ending up *shorter than the two buttons flanking it* in /messages' composer.

Both `Badge` and `Chip` are easy to write out by hand without noticing, because the class string is short and looks harmless: `rounded-full px-2 py-0.5 text-label-m` plus a container/ink pair was pasted at fifteen sites, in three type roles, each naming both halves of its colour. The tell is `rounded-full` on something holding text that is not a button — for a dismissible tag that is the wrong shape twice over, since a chip is 8dp.

`Button` variants, so a semantic action never has to be hand-rolled: `filled` · `tonal` · `surface` · `accent` · `text` · `danger` · `danger-text` · `success` · `warning`. The four semantic ones take the scheme-independent `*-fill` pair rather than the `error`/`success`/`warning` *text* roles, which flip between schemes — a filled confirm button wearing a text role visibly swapped shade with the theme. `danger-text` exists because `variant="text"` plus a `className="text-error"` emits two colour utilities and lets Tailwind's output order decide which wins; `cn` is a plain join and resolves nothing.

**A field toolbar is one material.** `Button variant="surface"` uses `surface-container-highest` / `on-surface`, matching `Select` and the filled input. Use it for neutral commands beside fields, as in search's sort direction and advanced filters. These controls use the same 40dp step; a 32dp chip, a 40dp field and a pink tonal button are not one coherent toolbar. An expansion is a disclosure, not a selected filter: use `trailingIcon` with a rotating arrow and `aria-expanded` / `aria-controls`, rather than a `Chip` whose selection replaces the arrow with a tick.

`Select shape="pill"` is for a choice in that toolbar; the default `field` uses the filled field's 8dp corner. Search's toolbar sits **between the field and the results**, never under them — under fifty pictures and the pager it was found only by scrolling past everything it reorders — and it stays whatever the search came back with, empty and failed included, since the condition that emptied a list is the one to loosen. It has no enclosing border or separator rules. The sort choice and direction form one group; advanced filters and reset form the other. These groups wrap together, keeping a reset from becoming a lone button on a new row. Expanded conditions sit on a quieter tonal surface, with spacing separating the two numeric conditions from the three attribute choices. The disclosure arrow shares the panel's opening `default-spatial` and closing `fast-effects` responses. **The conditions are the query's, not a snippet appended to it** (`parseSearchFilters` / `composeQuery`, `lib/searchQuery.ts`): the panel opens on what the committed query holds, 应用并搜索 writes them back in place, and each active one is also an input chip under the toolbar that removes it.

**Chinese and natural-language words go through the backend's semantic parse** (`semanticQuery`, `lib/api/semantic.ts`) when the status document says it is on and the words are Chinese with no query syntax (the original front end's two tests, `isSemanticText`). The URL keeps the words as typed in `q`; once the parse answers, its tags are pinned beside them (`tags=`, a replace), so a reload or a shared link runs the same search at once rather than waiting for — or depending on the mood of — the model. While the parse runs, the result area is its own skeleton under one 40dp row with a determinate bar over the backend's measured estimate, then an indeterminate one (`SemanticLine`); the same row then shows the tags as filter chips (leave one out and the search refines) and 搜索原文 (`raw=1`), or why the words were searched as typed (超时 / 暂时不可用). One height for every state, so the row changing its mind never moves the grid. 反馈本次搜索结果 appears only when a model produced the tags, behind the sign-in captcha.

**Busy is active, disabled is unavailable.** A loading `Button` / `IconButton` keeps its material, ink and exact footprint while its spinner (or `busyIcon`, e.g. `LoadMoreButton`'s dots) reports activity — and keeps its focus: it is `aria-disabled` + `aria-busy` with activation blocked (`blockActivation`: no default action, so a busy submit does not submit again, and no bubbling), **not** native `disabled`, which drops a focused element's focus onto the body (measured in Edge — a keyboard user's next Enter then did nothing). Native `disabled` is for an actually unavailable control, and only that takes the 0.38 treatment. A control a caller marks both `disabled` and `loading` is busy: the primitives emit native `disabled` only for `disabled && !loading`, so a submit that disables itself while its request runs keeps its focus. Icons and text must not shift when busy begins, and an inherited spinner takes the control's own ink, including on media.

**There is no outlined button**, and M3 does specify one. All four of its uses were a secondary action beside a filled primary one (取消 next to 保存, 重置 next to 检索), and at that job a 1dp keyline reads as a button that lost its fill rather than as a quieter button; `tonal` is the step M3 puts directly below `filled` for exactly this pairing, and it separates from the surface the way everything else here does, by a container tone rather than by an edge. Removed rather than left unused, because a variant that exists gets reached for. The same reasoning retired the filled field's border and the filter chip's: this app separates by tone.

`IconButton` keeps its `outlined` variant, and that is not an inconsistency — it is the one "switch that is currently off", which is what M3's outlined icon button means. It also owns the shape axis: `shape="square"` is the back affordance's 12dp corner. `toggle` declares a two-state button: `aria-pressed` follows `selected` both ways, true *and* false, under a label that stays the same (收藏, not 收藏 / 取消收藏); without it `selected` is visual only, which is right for a popup trigger that already reports `aria-expanded`.

Its selected circle-to-square change uses `transition-icon-button`: FastSpatial for the corner and dismiss rotation, FastEffects for the material. The circular endpoint is 50%, rather than an oversized length whose radius stays visually clamped through most of the interpolation. This is selection feedback, not a press morph; the fixed square shape stays fixed. `Select`'s arrow instead uses `menu-enter` / `menu-exit`, the CSS twins of its popover's `MENU_TRANSITION`.

## Colour

Only `--md-sys-color-*` tokens, via their Tailwind utilities (`bg-surface`, `text-on-surface-variant`, `border-outline-variant`, …). No raw hex, no `rgb()`, no Tailwind palette classes (`bg-slate-800`, `text-red-500`).

A `dark:` variant is almost always a bug: the token already flips. The legitimate uses are swapping a whole asset (`Logo.tsx`) and nothing else. Reach for the container/on-container pair instead of hand-picking a second tint.

Pair only within a role — `primary`/`on-primary`, `surface-container`/`on-surface`. Dividers use `outline-variant`; text-field borders use `outline`.

**`scripts/palette.mjs` generates every tonal value, and it is the authority.** Run `npm run colors` for the diff, the contrast table and the ten-theme matrix, `npm run colors:write` to write. Three products: the **default** theme's declarations are substituted in place in `globals.css` — it only touches `--md-sys-color-<token>: #hex;` lines, so the ~40 paragraphs of reasoning between them survive — while the nine character themes are written wholesale to `app/theme-palettes.css` and their brand colours to `lib/generated/themeColors.ts`. Both generated files are also *checked* on a plain run, so a stale one is a failure rather than a silent disagreement. It is **idempotent** against the current file, and it exits non-zero rather than writing if a tone lands off target, the neutral-variant palette's chroma moves, or any theme drifts from the default on any measured pair.

The basis is **HCT**, because that is the space M3 quotes its own numbers in: "neutral chroma 6" means 6 in HCT, and no single OKLCH chroma means the same thing at every lightness. Role → tone is verbatim from AOSP's `ColorLightTokens.kt` / `ColorDarkTokens.kt` (v0_210). `accent-*` stays in OKLCH and that is not an inconsistency — an even hue sweep at fixed lightness and chroma is the entire point of that scale, and OKLCH is where that relationship is expressible.

### The palette axis: eleven themes, two 配色方案, and `primary` is decided with the artwork beside it

`data-palette` on `<html>` selects one of ten built-in themes or the user's own; `data-palette-hues='mono'` selects **单色**, and its absence is **多色**, the default (decision 27). A built-in theme's `primary` is **the character's own hue from the MLP-VectorClub colour guide, at a tone and a chroma chosen for the largest area on screen** — the refinement in `fix/K1/DIRECTIONS.md` §2, recorded per theme with its guide row in `THEMES` in `scripts/palette.mjs`. Everything else is shared: the role→tone map, the harmony (M3's own, see below), the semantic ramps, and the three things still solved from the two fills.

**Direction A governs built-in fills and new image recommendations (decisions 33–34).**

- Keep the character's established hue and choose its appropriate register. The light tones are Applejack 86, Fluttershy 92, Lyra 88, Rainbow 84, Twilight/Pinkie 82; Chrysalis 42, Luna 34 and Rarity 39 stay deep. The brand is held to the same rule: the mascot's mane hue stepped 9.7° toward coral (H5.5) at the warm cap T86, `#fdcad3 / #fdaebe` — at its own hue (355.7) every in-register tone fell within ΔE 4.7 of 碧琪.
- `lib/clearColor.ts` is the shared chroma rule: light = min(C36, 0.96 × available gamut); deep = min(C48, 0.72 × available gamut). A pale peach's modest absolute chroma is not a defect. Built-in dark fills use the same hue/register rule seven tones lower.
- Role harmony remains the character's existing secondary/accent hues, bounded chroma and M3 neutral ladders. Semantic colours and tag categories do not follow the theme. 多色 / 单色, borderless theme tiles and one custom companion hue remain the contract.
- Built-in foregrounds are solved for text contrast. Exact manual and saved custom themes retain their full existing role derivation; recommendation generation is never run on saved values.

#### What is still derived

| Role | Built-ins | Exact custom / saved values |
| --- | --- | --- |
| primary ramp | light fill's hue at `rampChroma` | unchanged |
| on-primary | white if both fills clear 4.5:1, otherwise lower the ramp's dark ink until both do | original white-at-3:1 / P20 rule |
| primary-ink | target 4.6:1 against N90 light / N30 dark, assert ≥4.5:1 against actual surface/highest/selected containers | original page-only 2.9:1 light / 3:1 dark calculation |
| on-primary-variant | the bar's icons and wordmark: the ramp tone from on-primary toward the fill at 0.75 of on-primary's contrast, floored at 4.5:1, never above on-primary; coming down from white, a tint held to C22 (ASSERTION 19) | same rule; equals on-primary where that is under 4.5:1 |
| all remaining roles | existing role→tone map and character harmony | unchanged |

The stronger foreground policy is explicitly selected by the generator; `deriveCustomTheme` retains `exact-custom`. It is not a user style switch.

#### The harmony is M3's, verbatim — that is 单色

```
secondaryPalette:      hue,        chroma 16
neutralPalette:        hue,        chroma  6
neutralVariantPalette: hue,        chroma  8
```

单色 is `SchemeTonalSpot` with one change: **its tertiary roles read the secondary palette**, so no second hue appears anywhere (TonalSpot's own tertiary, hue + 60° at chroma 24, is not used). Before 多色 nothing read tertiary, so 单色 renders as the app always did; the three accent moments (below) read the secondary pair under it.

These numbers were once offsets reverse-engineered out of globals.css — `#755360`, `#894d1f`, `#7f7378` — kept because changing them would move the default theme, not because they were correct: measured against the spec they were secondary chroma **19.4** (21% high), tertiary **+57.31° chroma 37.1** (55% high) and neutralVariant **−9.48° chroma 6.7**, with only `neutral` already exact. Switching cost **18 declarations** in the default theme (`outline`, `on-surface-variant` and `outline-variant` among them, the two most repeated non-brand colours in the app); the measured pairs did not move (`outline`/`surface` is still 4.27:1 light and 5.83:1 dark). Paid once.

**The chroma stays flat even though `primary`'s is not.** M3 pairs a constant secondary 16 with a constant primary 36 — a ratio of 0.44 — while this app's primary ramp runs **45.7–56.8**, so the ratio here lands between **0.282** (the brand and four characters, 56.8) and **0.350** (邪茧, 45.7). Making secondary proportional would restore the ratio and would *not* be M3: the spec's number is a constant, and a focus ring that gets louder on the more saturated themes is a focus ring answering a question about the brand rather than about the keyboard.

**ASSERTION 8 is what makes any of this checkable.** ASSERTION 2 measures emitted chroma against *this repo's own* constant, so it is self-referential — a dependency bump that retuned TonalSpot would have passed every check in the file. ASSERTION 8 builds a real `SchemeTonalSpot` per theme and compares the three palettes 单色 reads at every tone the role map uses, and asserts that 单色's tertiary, focus and chip roles are its secondary ones; `primary` is excluded, because the library pins it flat at 36 and this app's is `rampChroma`. It passes today for all ten, and it fires on a **one-unit** chroma drift.

#### 多色: the character's second colour and accent

多色 fills two slots M3 leaves to the scheme with the character's own colours, both read off the same guide rows as the primary (every one cited in `THEMES`; the default's off the mascot renders):

| role family | 多色 | read by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `secondary*` | the **second colour**, chroma 14–18: broad and quiet | every selection container — the drawer's current row, the tab pill and rail, a selected or tonal icon button, a tonal button, the current contact, an unread row, your own chat bubble, the listbox's chosen option — and every track (progress, slider, spinner). They follow for free |
| `tertiary*` | the **accent**, chroma 36–48: a mark | three **accent moments** only: a favourite or like that is on (`IconButton selectedTone="tertiary"`: the tertiary container pair), the level meter (`ProgressBar tone="tertiary"`: tertiary on its own container), an empty state's glyph (`EmptyState`, through `StatusView`'s `iconTone`) |
| neutrals | the default theme only: **the mascot's coat** — cream in light (H48), periwinkle-lavender in dark (H285), M3's chroma 6 / 8 | every surface |
| `focus`, `chip-selected` | **TonalSpot's secondary, in both 配色方案** | the focus ring answers "where is the keyboard", and switching the scheme must not move it; a selected filter chip keeps the single-hue pair because the tag categories own chip-shaped colour at tone 90 — **an accent never wears a chip** |

The default's pair is the mascot's own: her mane pink (`#e06c9f`) is the brand's ramp seed, her coat shading periwinkle-lavender (the second colour, H272), her bows periwinkle (the accent, H268 — about 1% of her pixels, which is what an accent is), and her coat cream the page. The nine: 苹果嘉儿 leaf, 小蝶 rose and butterfly teal, 天琴 apricot and eye orange (her harp gold is the warning hue), 邪茧 mane blue and eye lime, 云宝 mane violet and orange, 露娜 eye teal and mane violet, 瑞瑞 diamond blue, 暮光 mane indigo and cutie-mark magenta, 碧琪 balloon blue.

**Second colours may coincide; a theme is told apart by its primary and its accent.** 默认 and 暮光 share the mascot's periwinkle by construction, 邪茧's mane blue sits beside 瑞瑞's diamond blue, and 碧琪's balloon blue is near both. Each is cited from its guide row, and moving one off its source to separate a pair visible only side by side would trade a documented colour for a guessed one (D1-023).

Four rules keep it from being noise, each an assertion. **The second colour is ≥ 40° from the primary** (ASSERTION 10): at tone 90 a hue under 40° away does not show at all — the mascot's mauve and lilac, 16° and 39° from her pink, rendered exactly as the pink did. **The accent never passes for a severity** (13): the built-ins' hues keep out of the guarded bands, and the accent mark and every selection container stay ΔE ≥ 6 from every severity role, fill and container (measured 9.3 and 6.9 — 单色 has 苹果嘉儿's selection container at 2.5 from warning-container, which 多色 fixes). **Its pairs clear their bars everywhere** (11): the accent on the page 4.24:1, on the highest card 3.47, the level meter against its own track 3.45, the focus ring beside a selected row 4.97. **And nothing new is disliked** (12). The accent's light tone is **50, a mark tone**, not M3's 40, which turns every warm hue brown — `primary-ink` solves the same problem — so `on-tertiary` on `tertiary` is not a text pair; nothing uses it as one.

**A dark container is a shade, not a greyed tone** (ASSERTION 17, D1-010). A second colour is declared once, as the tint the light scheme's tone-90 containers wear (chroma 14–18), and M3 reuses that chroma at tone 30 in dark, where the warm and violet hues' gamut is two to four times as wide — so the 16 that is all of a tint's purity is a third of a shade's, and the rest is grey: 天琴 `#594234`, 苹果嘉儿 `#414a32`, 小蝶 `#5b3f4b`, 云宝 `#504158` and 默认/暮光's `#40465c` held 0.37–0.51 of their hue's deep register, where the four that read clean (邪茧, 露娜, 瑞瑞, 碧琪) held 0.68–0.81. So for the ten built-ins the dark 多色 secondary palette takes `deepSecondChroma` (`lib/paletteRule.ts`): the declared chroma or 65% of `clearChromaAt(hue, 30, 'deep')`, whichever is more, capped at C24 — two thirds of the accent's quiet end, so a broad selection stays quieter than a mark. That gives 天琴 `#613f2a`, 苹果嘉儿 `#3f4b2c`, 小蝶 `#613b4d`, 云宝 `#533f5f`, 默认/暮光 `#3c4665`; the clean four, the light scheme and 单色 (TonalSpot's, ASSERTION 8) do not move, and a saved custom theme keeps its derivation (the exact-custom contract), so the custom sweep is not held to it. It cannot make a dark orange not brown — tone 30 at H50 is a chestnut at any chroma; what it removes is the grey.

**Where 多色 deliberately does not reach**: the unread count (M3's error role, the fixed fill), the 近日推荐 badge (on a photograph, where only a non-flipping `primary` applies), the level badge Lv (beside role badges, it would read as a tag), links, switches and checkboxes (the brand's own marks).

**`error` deliberately does not follow M3, and that is not an oversight.** The spec pins it at hue 25 / chroma 84; this one is hue 22 / chroma 67.2 — the three degrees are worth nothing either way. What matters is that `success` (chroma 43.9) and `warning` (chroma 39.0) have **no M3 equivalent at all** (the spec has no such roles), and taking 84 for the one ramp that has a spec would make the error toast visibly louder than the success toast beside it — a severity ordering nobody chose. The three are tuned as a set.
