**`rampChroma` is measured at `CHROMA_REFERENCE_TONE` (61), not at the fill's own tone, and that is the one non-obvious line in `lib/paletteRule.ts`.** A pale fill is pale because it sits where the gamut is *narrow* — `#FAF5AB` is chroma 30 at tone 95 where only 62 is available, while at tone 61 the same hue holds 67. Reading the chroma at the fill's tone built the entire primary palette at chroma 20 and made `primary-ink` — the colour of every glyph, tab indicator, checkbox and focused field outline in the app — come out `#a69166`, a khaki-grey; measured at tone 61 小蝶's ink is `#ac8e00`, a clean gold, and across the ten the inks run chroma 40–57 with no muddy member. ASSERTION 6 is that floor.

The consequence to carry: **a theme's fill and its ink are allowed to be very different colours** — 小蝶's bar is a butter yellow and her ink is a gold. That is correct (one answers a question about a surface and the other about a mark) and it is what lets a pale-coated character have a usable theme at all; every character's ink differs from its fill, because a decided fill sits off its own ramp.

| id | 标签 | primary 浅 / 深 | primary-ink 浅 | on-primary | 多色 第二色 / 点缀 | 出处 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | 默认 | `#fdcad3` / `#fdaebe` | `#a93d5c` | `#65032a` | H272 / H268 | 吉祥物鬃毛 #E06C9F（H355.7）向珊瑚偏 9.7° 至 H5.4：A 的暖色上沿 T86；原色相在 T82–T86 与碧琪仅差 ΔE 0.9–4.7，偏转后 6.9 / 6.1 |
| `applejack` | 苹果嘉儿 | `#fecf95` / `#f2b96d` | `#8a5900` | `#462b00` | H130 / H130 | 毛色 Fill #FABA62（H73）：A 的暖金浅色下沿 T86，保留苹果嘉儿的金橙色相 |
| `fluttershy` | 小蝶 | `#fee79d` / `#eed37a` | `#776100` | `#3b2f00` | H352 / H192 | 毛色 Fill #FAF5AB（T95.4），保留已定 H95 黄油色：A 的黄色上沿 T92 |
| `lyra` | 天琴 | `#9feccf` / `#8cd8bc` | `#007157` | `#00382a` | H50 / H44 | 毛色 Fill #8CFFDB（H174 T92.7）：A 的薄荷上沿 T88，大面积彩度上限 C36 |
| `chrysalis` | 邪茧 | `#386c67` / `#2e5a56` | `#007069` | `#ffffff` | H231 / H130 | 甲壳中段 #1E837F（H193 T49.6），保留深青甲壳身份：A 的深色上沿 T42 |
| `rainbow` | 云宝黛西 | `#96dbfb` / `#82c7e6` | `#006c8a` | `#003546` | H315 / H42 | 毛色 Fill #9BDBF5（H225）与 Shadow Fill #8CC7E7（H232）之间 |
| `luna` | 露娜 | `#3d489a` / `#2c3788` | `#3645ad` | `#ffffff` | H184 / H303 | 毛色 Fill #363E7A（H280 T28.5）：A 的深色下沿 T34，保留夜空深蓝 |
| `rarity` | 瑞瑞 | `#5e4fa4` / `#4d3e92` | `#5e4ab5` | `#ffffff` | H232 / H228 | 鬃毛 Fill #5E50A0（H294 T39）：保留紫鬃毛的自然深度，与露娜靠色相及明度区分 |
| `twilight` | 暮光闪闪 | `#e9bcf9` / `#d5a9e5` | `#8946a8` | `#500670` | H272 / H359 | 毛色 Fill #CC9CDF（H319 T70.8）：A 的浅紫下沿 T82，仍配靛蓝鬃毛与品红星标 |
| `pinkie` | 碧琪 | `#fdb9d5` / `#efa2c3` | `#a43d73` | `#63013e` | H226 / H226 | 毛色 Fill #F5B7D0（H353 T80.7）：A 的浅粉下沿 T82，与品牌粉靠明度区分 |
| `custom` | 自定义 | displayed/user hex / existing derived dark fill | existing rule | existing rule | one 副色相 | manual or image recommendation |

Two characters have no usable coat and take the next feature the guide gives them, which is also what ASSERTION 4 guards. **Cite the Fill row, not the Outline** — the Outline is the dark line drawn around a shape rather than the colour a character reads as. 瑞瑞's coat **Fill** is `#EAEEF0` at chroma 5.3 (her Outline `#BDC1C2` is 4.9 — a white pony either way) and 邪茧's is `#2A2A2A` at chroma 1.0, so they take a mane and a carapace. 碧琪 takes her coat Fill, at hue 353 and T82 — her mane's raspberry could only become a quiet large area in the middle band neither ink reads on. The brand, once it came under the same rule, had to make room: at its mane hue 355.7 every in-register tone landed within ΔE 4.7 of her, so it takes H5.5 at T86, held apart by hue and tone together (ΔE 6.9 light / 6.1 dark; the pair stays in ASSERTION 5's exemption list, its hue gap being 12.4°).

#### Measured limits and deliberate choices

The pale character fills have low boundary contrast against light pages; Luna/Rarity/Chrysalis dark bars have 1.77 / 2.13 / 2.39:1 against their dark page. Their text pairs still pass. These are reported fill-edge values, not a claim that every paint boundary reaches 3:1.

The two close-hue exemptions remain Luna/Rarity and Pinkie/default, held apart by measured fill distance. Character accent-container proximity to error-container remains about ΔE 4.9–5.3 for Lyra/Rainbow/Twilight in light, confined to the small filled favourite/like glyph; selection containers keep their stronger semantic-distance guard. The light tertiary remains the mark tone 50, not a normal-text pair. With the brand at H5.5 its primary family sits nearer the error hue: light `primary-container` (`#ffd9df`, the profile banner placeholder, a missing avatar, a drop zone while dragging) is ΔE 3.7 from `error-container`, and the dark link and `primary-ink` are ΔE 7.3 from `error`; the primary family is not held to ASSERTION 13.

Custom themes keep known contrast limits because the chosen colours and their existing derivation are not silently replaced. A focused outlined-field label therefore uses on-surface (invalid: error), not primary-ink; retain that shared field fix.

#### Nineteen assertions

`npm run colors` checks tone/chroma fidelity, contrast floors/spread, readable source hue and hue separation, primary-ink chroma, disliked colours, exact TonalSpot mono ramps, built-in text pairs and direction-A chroma ceilings, multi-hue/semantic separation, accent/focus/chip pairs, the 99-seed custom sweep, tile/page separation, the dark selection container's clarity (17), the brand's literals (18) and the app bar's quieter ink (19). Assertion 9 now holds the brand's fill like the nine — decided under direction A — under readable dark ink; pale fills are bounded by C36 rather than an obsolete 0.80 gamut-share ceiling.

A plain invocation verifies generated output; `--write` installs it. `--output-dir=PATH` exports the same asserted output for review without installing shared files. The normal verifier still rejects stale globals or generated palettes. Dedicated `scripts/testImageRecommendations.mjs` covers direction A and the quantizer population regression.

#### The eleventh palette: the user's own colour

One displayed hex is installed exactly. 选择颜色 names an exact colour; 从图片取色 recommends a colour after adjusting the image family's tone/chroma for the interface. Confirmation never transforms the displayed value again. Legacy single-seed cookies, manual values and saved themes retain their original derivation; no migration is performed.

**Under 多色 it has one 副色相, shared by the second colour and the accent** (decision 27 — the ten get two hues from an artist; one chosen by hand is the one least likely to clash). 自动 is a **right angle**: the seed's hue −90°, else +90°, never inside a guarded band (the three severities, each spanning its role and its fill, and the olive shoulder of `DislikeAnalyzer`'s band) — on the mascot's mane pink (`#e06c9f`, the brand's ramp seed) it lands on her own bows, 266° against 268°. Three presets are the same idea at other turns (邻近 −30°, 三分 −120°, 互补 180°, each with the same mirror and guard), and 自选 names a hue on a rail. **A relation tapers with the seed's confidence in its own hue** — a grey gets accents as grey as it is, as `rampChroma` gives it a near-grey ramp — while **a hue the user named is taken at its word**, guards included: the rule the primary already follows. The second colour is chroma 18 and the accent 36, the quiet end of the built-ins' ranges. 从图片取色 pairs each seed with the picture's own partner (below).

Nothing about it is generated, so it is **not** in `lib/generated/themeColors.ts`'s `PALETTES` array; that file carries only the id, because `PaletteId` is what every consumer validates against and a union that cannot express the palette the user chose is a union that silently downgrades them to the default.

| Concern | Where |
| --- | --- |
| The recipe | `lib/paletteRule.ts` — no `'use client'`, so all three consumers can import it |
| The stored form | `lib/paletteSpec.ts` — plain and import-free, so the client modules can parse it without the recipe |
| Naming a colour and its 副色相 | `components/ColorPicker.tsx` — a `Modal`, and the app's own rather than the OS's |
| Finding a pair in a picture | `components/ImagePalettePicker.tsx` — its own dialog, not a section of the other |
| What either one shows | `components/PalettePreview.tsx`, shared, so the two cannot disagree |
| The spec | `LS_KEYS.paletteCustom` and `COOKIE_KEYS.paletteCustom`: the seed, then `/` and the 副色相 unless 自动 — `#e06c9f`, `#e06c9f/triadic`, `#e06c9f/272`; a bare seed, what earlier builds wrote, reads as 自动 |
| First paint | `app/layout.tsx` derives it server-side into `<style id="palette-custom">`, both 配色方案 |
| Re-deriving | `lib/paletteLazy.ts`, a dynamic-import seam like `lib/motionLazy.tsx` |
| Installing | `applyCustomPalette` in `lib/appearance.ts` — replaces that same `<style>` |
| The wipe | `changeCustomPalette`, which guards on the **spec** (seed and 副色相) rather than the id |
| The tile | an eleventh radio in `PaletteSwatches`: a tonal placeholder with a palette glyph, disabled, until a colour exists |
| The tile's face | `components/PaletteTileFace.tsx`, the theme in miniature — shared by the ten, the eleventh, 从图片取色's options and `ColorPicker`'s 副色相 tiles (compact); the ten's colours are `lib/generated/themeFaces.ts`, the eleventh's `data-palette-tones` |
| Image extraction | `lib/imageRecommendations.ts` + `imageOptions` in `lib/paletteLazy.ts`, loaded when reading an image |

Nine things about it are load-bearing:

- **The cookie carries the spec, not the declarations.** A stored *output* goes stale the moment the rule moves — exactly what happened to the ten twice — and re-deriving costs one HCT run, memoised by `deriveCustomThemeCached`.
- **The server derives it.** `lib/paletteRule.ts` is a plain module, so `app/layout.tsx` ships every declaration, 多色 and 单色, in the first byte. No client equivalent: the pre-paint script runs before any stylesheet resolves and cannot carry HCT, and the lazy chunk lands after several paints.
- **A `<style>`, never inline properties on `<html>`.** Inline beats every selector including `html.dark[data-palette='custom']`, so a scheme flip would keep painting the light values. As a stylesheet the specificity is (0,1,1)/(0,2,1) — exactly as the generated file — so `applyScheme` needs no knowledge of it, and the rules come from the same `paletteBlocksCss` as `theme-palettes.css`: they cannot diverge.
- **The pre-paint script's `C` object is the validation list**, gaining `custom` only when *this request's* cookie carried a usable spec; a stored `custom` with nothing rendered for it falls back to `default` rather than a palette no stylesheet answers to (one line, malformed and cleared-cookie alike), and the shell then re-derives it from storage (`recoverCustomPalette`, which also catches a cookie naming another spec than storage).
- **The tile cannot read the tokens** — they are always the *active* theme's, so the first chip turned 露娜's blue the moment you selected 露娜. `applyCustomPalette` parks fourteen hexes on `<html>` as `data-palette-tones` — `primary`/`on-primary` per scheme, then per scheme the face's page, selection and accent and 单色's page and selection, packed by `packTones` (`lib/paletteSpec.ts`), which `app/layout.tsx` uses at SSR too — with the seed and 副色相 beside them, left there when you switch away.
- **A `dynamic()` dialog needs `loading`, and its absence is a page-level bug.** Both dialogs are `dynamic()` — together a 32-cell gamut-aware grid, a hue rail and deterministic area-ranked image recommendation analysis — and without `loading` the component **suspends** to the *route's* boundary: the first press of 选择颜色 replaced all of /settings with its route skeleton. `loading: () => null` moves the boundary onto the dialog, whose own skeleton covers the gap. Each also holds a one-way "has ever opened" latch, or `dynamic()` fetches at mount and a user who only names a colour still pays for the image-analysis chunk; each chunk is instead fetched on *intent* — a pointer arriving on, pressing, or focus reaching the button that opens it. A confirmed colour is installed from `Modal`'s `onExited`, not at the confirm: the wipe snapshots the screen, and a dialog still leaving was frozen into the old theme's picture and dragged away with it. The wipe grows from the button that opened that dialog.
- **The same picture gives the same colours.** The library's TypeScript `QuantizerCelebi` starts its k-means by putting every pixel in a `Math.random` cluster, and its first pass prunes from that start, so one picture offered different colours on every read (the mascot's second render: 8 distinct answers in 20). `lib/quantize.ts` is the port with that one line replaced — the starts drawn from `java.util.Random` seeded `0x42688`, as AOSP's Java quantizer draws them — and `scripts/testPalette.mjs` holds both the generator's reference values and the repeat-read invariant. Never go back to the library's quantizer for this.
- **Area ranks recommendations.** `lib/imageRecommendations.ts` groups C≥8 deterministic clusters around fixed 22° hue anchors, sums covered opaque pixels, then ranks families by area. Chroma does not give a small vivid detail priority. Tone/chroma use approved A: pale T82–88 (warm red/peach up to 86; yellow up to 92); a cool H165–325 family with ≥60% below T50 may recommend T34–42 first. Each family gets a place before supported light/deep alternatives, up to eight.
- **Every opaque sample is counted.** Final Lab centres that round to the same RGB key have their populations added. Assignments and centres are preserved; there is no nearest-centre recount. The 92-pixel negative control formerly returned only 71.
- **No invented fallback hue.** A gray image keeps its own populous tones, spread 12 tones apart. An all-transparent image gives no recommendations; an unreadable file is a separate decoding error. The old Monet helpers remain pure compatibility/reference utilities and do not drive the product picker.

- **The downsample is an area, not an edge**: `WallpaperColors.java` caps `MAX_WALLPAPER_EXTRACTION_AREA = 112 × 112` and rescales by `sqrt(cap / area)` ("aspect ratio independent"); it was a 128px longest edge. One deliberate divergence: `imageSmoothingQuality: 'high'` where AOSP scales with `filter = false` (nearest neighbour) — at a 30× reduction it samples one pixel in nine hundred and can delete the small saturated subject that then trips the cutoff above.

**Recommendation and exact selection are separate operations.** Direction A acts only while extracting recommendations. It retains the input family's hue and chooses a clear light/deep register before showing the theme; no style setting is stored. Manual colours and already saved specs do not pass through it.

**An image companion comes from an eligible family, otherwise it is honestly automatic.** Candidates need ≥0.2% of opaque pixels, readable chroma, a ≥45° hue gap and the existing automatic hue guards. Area leads; among populations within 10% of the largest eligible one, separation nearest 90° breaks the tie. One editable companion hue serves secondary and tertiary; mono keeps it for a later switch while showing its one-hue theme. The dialog explains recommendation adjustments and automatic fallback.

- **Both dialogs open on the consequence, not on a swatch.** `<input type="color">` carries none of this app's tokens, speaks RGB or HSV where the whole system speaks HCT, and shows a *colour* where the thing being chosen is a *theme*. `PalettePreview` is the shared answer: the bar with its own ink, a selected row and a favourite that is on — the second colour and the accent under 多色, the one hue under 单色 — the `primary-ink` every non-bar role will take, and what the dark scheme becomes.
- **The picker opens on the consequence too: a tile is the theme in miniature** (`PaletteTileFace`) — a band of its `primary`, its app bar, over its own page, and on the page a pill of what a selection wears there and, under 多色, a dot of the accent: each colour where the theme puts it, at the size it takes there, so 单色 shows one hue and 多色 its two more without a word. It replaced a 40dp disc quartered into primary, second colour and accent, which read as a muddy pie — a pale second colour greyed it — because a disc is the shape of *a colour* and what is chosen here is *a theme*. 72 × 56 at the card's corner, and **borderless**: it separates by tone, like everything in this app, and the keyline the disc needed for its pale coats went with it. Its page is the theme's `surface-container-lowest`, the ladder's far end in the page's own direction and the one step clear of every surface a tile is drawn on — the theme's own `surface` sat 1.8 in L* from /settings' row in light (ASSERTION 16). Selected: a check badge at the top-right, a disc of that theme's `primary` with its `on-primary` tick, concentric with the corner (on the bar the disc is the bar, and the tick is what shows), and the label in the emphasized role — no ring; the focus ring is the standard one, following the corner. Under forced colours each tile draws the system edge and the badge takes the system's selection pair. The arrows move the choice through the eleven, Home and End jump to the ends; a wipe commits a frame after it is asked for, so `changePalette` guards on the palette a wipe is heading to, not the one `<html>` still names — a burst of arrows used to leave the selection a tile behind focus.
- **`ColorPicker`'s 副色相 group shows the theme per way of choosing**: five tiles (自动 / 邻近 / 三分 / 互补 / 自选), each with the theme it gives as a compact tile face, so the choice is made by looking rather than by knowing what 三分 means — and at the size the accent really takes, a mark, where the old half-disc gave it half of everything; 自选 opens a rail of 24 hues, each the accent's own palette at tone 62 (`accentSwatch`), starting where 自动 is. The group is not shown under 单色, where nothing would answer to it, and the stored choice is kept.
- **In `ColorPicker`, every cell is a real `Hct.from()` result, not a gradient**: sRGB's gamut in HCT is an irregular solid, so a gradient offers values the browser then clips and a user can aim at one colour and land on another. Chroma is a *fraction of what is available* at that hue and tone (at tone 95 a hue may hold a third of what it holds at 60). One `Pick` object holds the hex, hue and selected grid cell, updated atomically. The stored hue and cell are authoritative for selection: grey has no recoverable hue, and HCT rounding at light tones can map several cells to the same hex. Re-deriving the cell from that hex would move the check or select several cells; separate synchronising effects are unnecessary.
- **Two dialogs rather than one.** One dialog with two entry points held two models at once (a `picked` option *and* a hex, each clearing the other) and a confirm button ambiguous about which it was sending. They ask different questions: *what colour* (rail and grid) vs *which of these* (a short list nothing can be typed into).

HCT is behind that dynamic import for a reason and must stay there: `npm run perf:weight` plus a grep of the built chunks must keep showing the HCT chunk in **no** route's document. `warmPalette()` fetches it from `PaletteSwatches`' own mount — /settings is the only screen that pays for it, resident long before either dialog is opened.

**`primary` is a fill role; `primary-ink` is the brand as a mark.** Same hue, at whichever tone can be read on the page. Where the brand is ink rather than a container, read it: `text-primary-ink` — a selected glyph, the checkbox's box, the tab indicator, the slider's fill, the focused field's outline, a quoted block's rule. Use `primary` where the thing is a container with `on-primary` over it: the app bar, a `filled` button, the switch track, the active pagination pill. The switch's tick is the same pair the other way round — `primary` on the `on-primary` handle; `primary-ink` there measured 2.0–2.9:1 once the light fills' `on-primary` became a dark ink. Rule of thumb: could you put a label inside it? (An earlier role of the same name served links *and* active states and did neither well; this one has a single job and cannot invert — never lighter than the brand in light, never darker in dark.)

**The app bar's ink is `on-primary-variant`, a step quieter than `on-primary`** (the owner: 「header里的图标（含logo）能不能稍微浅一点点」). The bar carries icons and the wordmark and no text, so its ink may give up some contrast: three quarters of `on-primary`'s against the bar, floored at 4.5:1 so it would still carry text, along the same ramp — a dark ink rises toward a pale bar, keeping its own chroma, and white comes down toward a deep one as a tint of the ramp's hue held to C22 (`ON_PRIMARY_VARIANT_TINT_CHROMA`). The ramp's own tones near white carry as much chroma as the gamut allows there, and cyan allows a lot: 邪茧's came out a saturated aqua (`#7bf6eb`, C45.7), a new colour on the bar, where 露娜's and 瑞瑞's were pale tints (`#dbddff`, `#e3dbff`, about C21); held to the tint it is `#bde8e2`. Only the header's text class and `IconButton variant="on-primary"` take it; filled-button labels, the checkbox tick, the pagination pill, selection discs, the banner badge, the spinner and the palette previews keep `on-primary`, and the bar's focus ring keeps `focus-ring-on-primary`. One constant (`ON_PRIMARY_VARIANT_SHARE`) moves it; ASSERTION 19 holds it for the ten and the custom sweep.

**What follows the theme and what does not.** A theme re-skins the *system*; it is not a licence to recolour everything:

| Follows | Fixed in all eleven |
| --- | --- |
| `primary` / `primary-ink` / `secondary` / `tertiary` families — the latter two with 配色方案 too | `error` / `success` / `warning` — a severity that changes colour with a theme is not a severity |
| every `surface` step, `on-surface`, `inverse-*` — and, for the default under 多色, the mascot's coat | the four `*-fill` + `on-fill`, for the same reason and because they already do not flip between schemes |
| `on-surface-variant`, `outline`, `outline-variant` | `accent-*` — a categorical scale; a 分级 chip that changed hue per theme would change meaning |
| `focus` and `chip-selected` — per theme, but **TonalSpot's secondary in both 配色方案** (a literal per theme, not the `secondary` token, which 多色 moves); `focus-on-primary` / `focus-on-media` through their `var()`s | `glass-body` / `glass-body-b` / `glass-sheen` — the /about plate's own material: the two ends of its field and its specular. Their 21-code separation is the plate's most load-bearing number, not a tint — the reference measures 12.3 code values of span at chroma 3.8 and reads as white silk |
| `link` / `link-hover` — the brand hue at P40 / P80 | `media-*`, `on-media*`, `scrim` — things sitting on a photograph |
| the /about plate's *ink*, which reads `primary` at run time — the one role that follows the palette without a token of its own, because a shader uniform is where a `var()` cannot reach | the wordmark. `.logo-keyline` is `currentColor` and the Lottie roses are hand-drawn pink; a brand mark does not recolour with a user preference |

**Links follow the palette, and the underline is what pays for it.** The old fixed blue left every link ignoring the theme (blue is a link's affordance, but the argument left the source URL under a picture off-theme too). They are the brand hue at a text tone now (6.12–6.19:1 on light `surface`, 10.81–10.94:1 dark, vs the old blue's 6.34 / 10.09 — a hue swap with no legibility change), and prose links carry a **rest-state underline** so the affordance no longer rests on hue. The underline is what pays for the swap: under the old blue a link sat 22.6° from 云宝黛西's brand and 23.0° from 瑞瑞's in OKLab hue — close enough to read as one colour — and now it *is* that hue (0.2°–2.1° from `primary-ink` across the ten), so the line is the only thing left distinguishing a link from an emphasis mark.

The selectors carry `html` (`html[data-palette='x']`, `html.dark[data-palette='x']`) and that is load-bearing: `@import` has to precede every rule, so the generated blocks land *above* `:root`, and `:root` and `[data-palette=x]` are both specificity (0,1,0). With the element name they are (0,1,1)/(0,2,1) and beat `:root` and `.dark` whatever the order. 单色 adds one attribute to each — (0,2,1)/(0,3,1) — and carries only the roles it changes (eight per character; the default's twenty-two, its coat included), declared as one set in both halves of the pair. **globals.css holds the default theme's 多色**, so a visitor with no cookie needs no selector at all; 多色 is the attribute's *absence*. The two blocks per scheme cost about 0.7 KB brotli over the ten.

To **add** a theme: one entry in `THEMES` in `scripts/palette.mjs` — an id, a label, the fill's decision (guide hue, tone, share of the gamut) with the guide row it came from, and 多色's second colour and accent with theirs — then `npm run colors:write`; the CSS, the tiles' colours (`lib/generated/themeColors.ts` for the bar, `themeFaces.ts` for the rest) and the `<meta name="theme-color">` values are all products of that run, and ASSERTIONS 9–13 say whether the choices hold. To **move the brand**: edit the default row's `fill` decision in `THEMES` and run it — ASSERTION 18 prints the two literals to paste into `BRAND_SEED`/`BRAND_SEED_DARK` in `lib/paletteSpec.ts` (the import-free module, so a client that needs only the colour reads the one literal; `lib/paletteRule.ts` re-exports both) — then re-check `.logo-keyline` against the Lottie artwork. `BRAND_RAMP_SEED` beside them is the mascot's mane, `#e06c9f`, and does not move with the fill: `BRAND_CHROMA`, the floor under every theme's ramp, is read off it, and a tint's chroma would take all eleven `primary-ink`s down (ASSERTION 18 holds it to chroma ≥ 35). A new role goes into globals.css by hand once, in both blocks (the run names it and its value); after that `--write` keeps it. The hand-copy step (two literals in `viewport.themeColor`) is gone: that export carries no `themeColor` at all, because a static array cannot express eleven palettes (one per-user) and mutating Next's own tag does not survive a client navigation — the tag is rendered from the cookie in `app/layout.tsx`.

**Container steps follow the named M3 tones rather than a tint chosen per call site.** The elevation column records this app's shadow policy: ordinary cards keep their tonal variant without a decorative shadow, while detached overlays retain their shared elevation level.

| Component            | Container                     | Elevation |
| -------------------- | ----------------------------- | --------- |
| Filled card          | `surface-container-highest`    | 0         |
| `Card variant="elevated"` | `surface-container-low`    | 0         |
| Outlined card        | `surface` + `outline-variant`  | 0         |
| Dialog               | `surface-container-high`       | 3         |
| Modal bottom sheet   | `surface-container-low`        | 1         |
| Menu / popover       | `surface-container`            | 2         |
| Search suggestions   | `surface-container-highest`    | 1         |
| Plain tooltip        | `inverse-surface`              | 0         |
| Navigation drawer    | `surface-container-low`        | 1 (modal) |
| Filled text field    | `surface-container-highest`    | 0         |
| Progress track, slider track | `secondary-container`  | —         |

A row inside a dialog steps one tone up from the dialog's `surface-container-high`: `surface-container-highest` (DerpiFilterImport, the glossary's history and Derpibooru search). A quote inside a `highest` card takes the default `high`: at the top of the ladder the one step is down.

The progress row had drifted furthest. `LinearProgressIndicatorTokens` puts `Height`, `TrackThickness` and `ActiveThickness` all at **4dp** — no size axis; the track is `secondary-container`; the indicator is `secondary` or a `*-fill`; the fill is a full-width rounded bar translated out of the clipping track (`translateX` of the value less 100%), not an animated `width`, because `width` is a layout property and one instance runs live inside the image overlay while a flight is landing. It was `scaleX`, which scaled the fill's rounded cap with it: at 10% the leading end came out square beside a round track end. Six hand-rolled tracks carried three heights (4 / 8 / 10, the last off the 4dp grid), three track tones, two drive mechanisms and **no `role="progressbar"` at all** — no progress was announced, including the 词库 sync, the one place a user waits. `ProgressBar` owns all of it. `TrackActiveSpace` (a 4dp gap between indicator and remaining track) is knowingly not implemented: it needs the remaining track's leading edge to follow the value, the layout work the transform exists to avoid. **`StopSize` is not implemented either — a divergence, not an omission.** Both attempts read as a detached mark on a meter that is mostly not full: a 4dp square in the fill's colour at `right-0`, clipped into a nub by the track's `rounded-full`; then a round dot concentric with the cap in the track's on-container (`on-secondary-container` measures 13.32:1 light / 7.24:1 dark against the track, vs the fill colour's 5.00:1) hidden above 98% — and `experience % 100` is 0–99, so it can never cover the dot.

M3's rule for the dot is conditional and this app meets it: required when the track's contrast against the container behind it falls under 3:1, and `secondary-container` measures 1.23:1 light / 1.99:1 dark against `surface` and **1.00:1** against `surface-container-highest` — the empty half of the track is invisible on that step. If that ever needs fixing, give the *track* contrast rather than a mark at the end of an invisible one. `Slider` keeps its own stop indicator and should: its track is 16dp with an 8dp cap, so a 4dp dot inset on the inactive side reads as part of the track, not a fragment of a fill.

The dialog was `surface-container-lowest`, the *flattest* step: the one surface meant to read as lifted off everything was painted lighter than the page behind it and relied entirely on its shadow. Tone first, shadow second is the whole M3 depth recipe; that had it backwards.

The tooltip is the one place `inverse-surface` is right — no tension with the snackbar's note below. `inverse-surface` flips between schemes, which is wrong for a **severity**: the same message must not arrive as a dark chip in one theme and a light one in the other. A tooltip carries no severity; its whole job is to contrast with whatever surface it is over, and flipping is how it keeps doing that.

**An alpha on a token is a bug.** `bg-primary/10`, `border-error/40`, `text-on-media/60` — each eyeballed once per scheme, each drifting, because nothing makes the next call site pick the same number; the tint that looked right on light is nearly invisible over dark. Use the tone scale (`surface-container-*`) or the container/on-container pair. If you need a weight no token has, add the token.

**There is now exactly one legitimate alpha at a call site**, and it is not a colour: the `--md-sys-state-*` opacities, M3's own `StateTokens` numbers, reaching call sites through `state-layer` rather than by hand. Two former entries were listed "legitimate" and were wrong:

- `bg-scrim/50`, "one value, three call sites": the value is **0.32** (`ScrimTokens.ContainerOpacity`), so all three were 56% too heavy — and a fourth, at 62%, hid inside an arbitrary `shadow-[…]` in `ImageCropper`. The dim is `bg-scrim-veil` now and the crop mask is `--md-sys-color-crop-mask`: two different objects, two tokens, no number at a call site. A crop mask being heavier than a dialog dim is a real distinction — it has to make "outside the crop" inactive while staying legible enough to aim with.
- `bg-on-surface-variant/40` on the sheet's drag handle was not an M3 number at all: `SheetBottomTokens.DockedDragHandleColor` is `OnSurfaceVariant`, full strength, and `SheetDefaults` passes it unmodified.

The fix has the same shape every time: need a weight no token has → **add the token**; and if the thing you are weighting turns out to be a different object from the one the existing token serves, that is why the number kept drifting.

**An element `opacity-NN` used as an ink weight is the same bug.** The rule above only named alphas *on tokens*, so the identical drift grew back: a quoted reply preview at 70% in two components that each picked the number alone, an English tag name at 50%, a footer wordmark at 60%, a banner icon at 80% — none could agree, because there was nothing to agree with. Reach for a *type role* first (a supporting line is `body-s` under a `body-m`, not a faded copy of it), then `on-surface-variant`, then add a token. Element opacity is legitimate for motion (a fade, a hover reveal, a collapsing `grid-rows-[0fr]`), for the paging dim over a grid whose next page is on its way (one value, 50%, at every paged grid), and for `disabled-content`.

**Disabled content is `disabled-content` (38%), and nothing else.** M3 specifies 38%; the app used five weights — 40 on `IconButton` and `Pagination`'s arrows, 50 on `Button`, `Chip`, `Checkbox`, `ToggleSwitch`, `Select`'s trigger and `DropZone`, 60 on `Input`, 70 on the captcha handle, a lone 38 inside `Select`'s menu — with two files disagreeing with *themselves* (`Pagination`'s arrows vs its numbers; `Select`'s trigger vs the menu it opens). It is a `@utility` in globals.css rather than a colour so a control's border, glyph, label and container fade together in one declaration, and it composes with the variant: `disabled:disabled-content`.

**Text and marks on photography** have their own roles, mirroring `on-surface` / `on-surface-variant` / `outline-variant` — a picture is not a surface, and none of the surface roles apply over one:

| On media                            | Role                 |
| ----------------------------------- | -------------------- |
| Primary ink — a title, a tag chip   | `text-on-media`      |
| Secondary ink — caption, meta, +N   | `text-on-media-variant` |
| A rule or track — crop guides, bars | `bg-media-outline`   |
| The plate ink sits on               | `bg-media-plate`     |

`bg-media-plate` is the one answer for a format badge, a score pill, a caption bar or a hover veil. It replaced `bg-scrim/40`, `/50`, `/55` and `/60` — four weights for one object, the two closest on screen the furthest apart.

**Focus** is one ring for the whole app: `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-ring`, or `focus-ring-on-primary` for chrome on the app bar. Over a **photograph** it is `focus-visible:inset-ring-2 focus-ring-on-media`, and the inward direction is not optional: that ring resolves to `on-media` (white), and an outset white ring puts its outer edge on the picture where a bright subject takes it to 1:1; inward it sits on the control's own `bg-media-plate`, where the worst case — a pure-white subject under a 55% black plate — still measures 4.8:1. `IconButton variant="media"` supplies that plate and switches the ring width itself; anything else reaching for this needs one too. Never tint it per variant — a focus ring answers "where is the keyboard", so it must look identical on every control — and it is solid, not tinted: `primary/40` composites to about 1.4:1 against a light surface, under the 3:1 WCAG 2.4.11 asks of a focus indicator.

**The ring is `secondary`** — M3's own name: `FocusIndicatorColor` is `Secondary` in `MenuTokens`, `SearchBarTokens`, the card tokens and the chip tokens alike. It was `primary`: on a filled button that was `primary` on `primary`, **1:1** — the app's most prominent control had no visible focus state — and against the light surfaces 2.94:1 / 2.39:1, both under the 3:1 bar; `secondary` measures 6.17:1 / 5.01:1 light and 10.90:1 / 7.26:1 dark. The two exceptions exist because neither role has any guaranteed contrast on the brand bar or over a picture. **It is TonalSpot's secondary in both 配色方案** — a literal per theme rather than the `secondary` token, which under 多色 is the theme's second colour: the ring answers where the keyboard is, and choosing a scheme must not change that answer (ASSERTION 14).

**An outset ring is drawn on the surface *behind* the control, not on the control** — do not "fix" `Button variant="filled"` to use `focus-ring-on-primary`. `ring-2` is a `box-shadow` painted just outside the border box: on a filled button those pixels are the page, where `secondary` measures 6.15:1. The 2.09:1 figure in globals.css's table is for a ring drawn *on* a primary-coloured surface — the app bar, and nowhere else — which is what `focus-ring-on-primary` is for; `IconButton variant="on-primary"` already carries it.

The third form is `focus-visible:inset-ring-2 focus-visible:focus-ring-inset` — the same ring painted inward, not a second style. A ring is a `box-shadow`, so an ancestor that paint-contains throws it away entirely, and the app has two kinds that do: a gallery card (`.image-card` is `contain: layout paint style`) and any media box clipping its corners with `overflow-hidden rounded-*`. A full-bleed control inside one of those — the spoiler cover, a zoom target — rendered a focus indicator that was then discarded. Reach for it only when the enclosure clips; everywhere else the ring goes outside, where it does not eat 2px of the control.

The fourth form is for a control that floats over whatever the page shows and has no plate of its own — the mascot and the ponies: `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-offset-2 focus-visible:ring-offset-surface focus-ring`, the same ring with a 2px band of the page's `surface` inside it. Over a picture the band gives the ring the edge it has everywhere else (6.17:1 light, 10.90:1 dark). A media plate would not do: a pony's sprite is drawn in the frame beneath its control, so a plate on the control darkens the pony (G1-022).

**Forced colors (Windows High Contrast) drop every ring**, because a ring is a `box-shadow`, and flatten every container tone. One rule beside the focus rings in globals.css draws a 2px `Highlight` outline on `:focus-visible` instead — inset where the control's ring is inset, on the field's own boundary for a field, and on the keyboard's roving cursor, which has no focus of its own. The primitives hide the engine's outline with `outline-hidden`, which Tailwind keeps as a transparent outline under forced colors (so the control is right even without that rule), never `outline-none`, which removes it there too. Three utilities say what a part becomes in that mode: `forced-boundary` (a 1px system-text edge for anything that separated itself by tone), `forced-selected` (a selected container takes the system's selection pair) and `forced-mark` (a mark that exists only as paint — a tab indicator, a switch track, a radio dot, a meter's fill — takes `Highlight`, or it vanishes and takes the state with it). Their colours are `!important` inside the media query because a utility's place in the stylesheet follows its first property, which sorted them ahead of the container utilities they have to beat: measured, the selected rail tab kept its pink.

The **outlined text field** has no outer ring: focus thickens its own outline to 2px in `primary-ink`, while an invalid outlined field retains the `error` outline and label. A control whose visual boundary *is* a 1px outline cannot wear a ring around it without reading as two nested boxes. The *filled* field has no outline to nest inside, so it takes the ordinary `focus` ring, which takes precedence over its error ring. This is the field family's own boundary treatment; it does not change the focus roles of other controls.

**`outline` is a boundary role, not an ink role.** It is built for a rule or a text-field border and is specified to 3:1 — the bar for a *non-text* element. Against this app's light surface it lands at 4.3:1, under the 4.5:1 WCAG AA asks of normal-size text; the dark scheme reaches 5.8:1 and passes. That asymmetry is why `text-outline` spread to 57 pieces of supporting text unnoticed: it is only wrong in one scheme, the one people ship from less often. Supporting text is `on-surface-variant` (8.5–9:1 light, 10:1 dark). `text-outline` on a *glyph* is fine — 4.3:1 clears the 3:1 non-text bar.

**A state is a container, not a rule down the side.** The reflex for an unread notification, a selected row, a quoted reply is a 3–4px coloured bar at the leading edge; M3 has no such element — a list item says "unread"/"selected" by wearing a container pair, and a block of text says "quoted" by `<blockquote>`'s own treatment. So an unread system message is `secondary-container` / `on-secondary-container` across the whole row, and the bar is gone. The ink half is not optional: the row's title and body must *inherit* the on-container colour, so write `text-on-surface`/`text-on-surface-variant` into the read branch only — a hard `text-on-surface` on the heading survives the container change and leaves a coloured row whose text still belongs to the old one.

The reply quote keeps its rule, because there the bar is not a state — it is what distinguishes quoted text from the reply around it, and what `<blockquote>` has looked like for thirty years. It is 4px `primary` (the same colour as the `<cite>` under it, so quote and attribution read as one object) over a `surface-container-high` fill — the same tone step as everything else here meaning "a distinct block inside this one".

**A filter control that is not selected is a tone step, not a keyline.** `Select`, the filled text field and `Button variant="surface"` share `surface-container-highest` without a border or decorative shadow. An unselected `Chip` remains `surface-container-high`, a different control role. Use the field toolbar family when those neighbours need one material, rather than adding a chip just because the control expands something.

Deliberate divergences from the spec, all commented where they live. Do not "fix" them:

- `primary`, `*-fill`, `media-stage`, `on-media*` and `media-plate` retain their identity between schemes. The brand primary is `#fdcad3 / #fdaebe` (T86/79, direction A like the nine); its `on-primary` is `#65032a`, measuring 9.10/7.48:1. Built-in `primary-ink` is separate text-safe foreground on raised/selected surfaces. Fixed media/status roles remain unchanged; exact custom themes preserve their prior role policy and known limits. Body text uses surface roles, navigable text uses link.

## Shape

The step is decided by the role, never by eye:

| Role                                            | Class          | Value |
| ----------------------------------------------- | -------------- | ----- |
| Button, FAB, avatar, circular icon button       | `rounded-full` | —     |
| Unread count pill, list row in a nav            | `rounded-full` | —     |
| Search bar (`Input size="lg"`)                  | `rounded-full` | —     |
| Search suggestions (`Popover variant="search"`) | `rounded-2xl` | 28dp |
| Card, section surface, palette tile             | `rounded-md`   | 12dp  |
| Square icon button (the back affordance)        | `rounded-md`   | 12dp  |
| Chip, small tag                                 | `rounded-sm`   | 8dp   |
| Outlined text field, colour swatch, one-time-code box | `rounded-xs` | 4dp |
| Filled text field, `Select shape="field"`       | `rounded-sm`   | 8dp   |
| Menu / listbox row, ordinary autocomplete row   | `rounded-sm`   | 8dp   |
| Menu, popover, ordinary autocomplete panel      | `rounded-lg`   | 16dp  |
| Badge, inline code, seam in a grouped list      | `rounded-xs`   | 4dp   |
| Snackbar / toast                                | `rounded-xs`   | 4dp   |
| Plain tooltip                                   | `rounded-xs`   | 4dp   |
| Navigation drawer, trailing corners only        | `rounded-r-lg` | 16dp  |
| Dialog, Sheet, large media                      | `rounded-2xl`  | 28dp  |
| Gallery thumbnail / grid tile, chat row         | `rounded-lg`   | 16dp  |
| Profile hero banner, `sm` and up                | `rounded-3xl`  | 32dp  |

The scale is M3's: 0 / 4 / 8 / 12 / 16 / 20 / 28 / 32 / 48, nothing between steps. `rounded-3xl` was 36dp — not one of them — the token moved rather than the two call sites, since 36 was only ever the distance between 28 and 48 split in half.

A **snackbar is 4dp** (`corner-extra-small`): a transient message, distinct from the rounder menu surface you act inside. The **navigation drawer rounds only its trailing corners**, and only while modal — a docked drawer is flush with the edge it docks to, so the rounding is `rounded-r-lg md:rounded-none`.

**A box inside another box does not take its own row of this table.** Nested corners are concentric when `inner = outer - gap`, and the eye reads a violation immediately: an inner corner rounder than `outer - gap` bulges toward the frame, a squarer one leaves a crescent of dead space. Look up the enclosure's radius, subtract the gap, and use that — the table gives the *outermost* box's step, not every box's. The rule only bites while the gap is small (treat roughly 8px as the line): two corners 16px apart are neighbours, not a ring inside a ring, and forcing the arithmetic produces a 0dp corner on something that should not have one — what `DetailBack` documents about itself.

Shortcut worth knowing: **a centred pill inside a pill is concentric for free, at every size** — `outer - gap` is always half the inner capsule's height, so the arithmetic can never be wrong. That is why /search's field is a 56dp pill rather than a 12dp box: the submit button and 以图搜图 sit in its trailing slot, and at 12dp with a 4px gap they would each need a remembered 8dp corner, whereas two ordinary pills in a pill are correct by construction. A square-cornered `Button` variant was added for the 12dp version and removed with it — `inner = outer` is the misreading of this rule, not the rule.

A **chip is 8dp, not a pill.** This table used to say `rounded-full`, contradicting `Chip.tsx` (the primitive always rendered the spec's 8dp); the cost was four filter chips in `/search` and the admin console hand-rolled as `rounded-full px-3 py-2` pills by someone reading this table rather than the component.

**Menu geometry is one nested family: 16dp panel, 8dp inset, 8dp row corner.** `Select`, `Menu` and ordinary autocomplete lists put 8dp padding on the panel; `Popover`'s content wrapper supplies the 2dp inter-row gap. Rows have a 40dp minimum under a fine pointer and 48dp under a coarse pointer. `estimateMenuHeight` includes both the insets and these gaps; search suggestions pass their own 56dp row estimate.

`Select` matches its trigger's width. Its row's 8dp inline padding plus the panel's 8dp inset align labels with the trigger's 16dp inset. The trailing mark reserves a 20dp slot, like the trigger arrow, and draws a 16dp `CheckGlyph weight="light"` inside it; selection changes opacity without adding width. A command menu may be intrinsically wider than its trigger: `matchAnchorWidth={false}` centres it on the trigger, then clamps it within the viewport. Do not widen a `Select` menu independently to hide an alignment problem.

**These corners are deliberate project refinements, not verbatim M3 component mappings.** The token sources still assign `CornerExtraSmall` to `MenuTokens.ContainerShape` and `OutlinedTextFieldTokens.ContainerShape`, and `CornerExtraSmallTop` to filled fields. The outlined field keeps 4dp; this app chooses 8dp filled fields and 16dp menus with inset rows for the rounder, coherent surface requested here. Search suggestions keep their separate 28dp / 20dp / 8dp geometry.

When citing what the specification says, **read the token file, not a summary table**, and distinguish it from these stated divergences. The generated `androidx.compose.material3.tokens` sources are fetchable —

    base=https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/refs/heads/    androidx-main/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3
    curl -s "$base/tokens/<Component>Tokens.kt?format=TEXT" | base64 -d

— every number in this file that says "M3 specifies" should be checkable that way; where a summary and a token file disagree, the token file is what generates the components.

Write the step you mean. A bare `rounded` resolves to `--radius` (4dp, same as `rounded-xs`) but says nothing about *why* — which is how 92 call sites ended up at 4dp with no role between them (icon buttons and badges that wanted `full`, skeleton bars that wanted whatever the thing they stand in for wants). `rounded-xs` is the step; use it where 4dp is the answer.

A **badge is a rounded rectangle and a count is a pill** — one row of this table split in two. M3's badge is round because M3's badge is a *number*: a dot, or a count no wider than it is tall (`CountBadge`). A badge carrying a word (Lv.13, 已核验, an earned badge's name) is a short block of text, and a full pill around text reads as a button that has lost its handler — the confusion `Chip` sits on the other side of. 4dp rather than the chip's 8dp because the box is only ~20px tall at `sm`, where an 8dp corner is 40% of the height and is a capsule again — pick the radius against the box, don't inherit it from a bigger relative.

A **chat message is a list row, not a lozenge** — 16dp, with the seams inside one turn cut to the grouped-list 4dp. It was 28dp on the argument that roundness *is* the semantics of a speech bubble; the argument lost to the thread it produced: at 28dp a two-word message is a capsule and a long one a stadium, so a column reads as a bag of lozenges, not something you can scan. What a thread keeps from the bubble is per-message width — each row only as wide as its own text, the ragged right edge carrying the rhythm of speech. The gap between rows of one turn is 2dp (`ListTokens.SegmentedGap`), which lets the 4dp seams close against each other. See `ChatBubble`.

**A direct message is plain text, and a card is not a bubble.** What was typed is what is shown: line breaks kept, links made clickable, `$emoji_<name>$` drawn as the picture — no Markdown (`app/messages/messageText.ts`; a line starting `1. ` became a list). Messages the original front end sent are BBCode and read into the same plain model. A shared picture, folder or privacy space is a card in its own surface, and one to three emoji alone draw large; both are `ChatBubble attachment`, with no bubble fill around them. Share cards are the original front end's wire format verbatim (`encodeShare` / `parseShare`, `lib/api/messages.ts`), so either front end renders the other's.

A **placeholder inherits the radius of the thing it replaces**, so `Skeleton` stands its own default down when the call site names one — `cn` is a plain join and does not resolve Tailwind conflicts, so passing a radius used to emit two and let output order decide.

`rounded-lg` is pinned to `HERO_TARGET_RADIUS_PX` in `lib/hero/constants.ts` — the shared-element flyer morphs its corner to that value on landing, so the two must stay equal.

## Elevation

`shadow-e1` … `shadow-e5` only. Never `shadow-sm/md/lg/xl` — tuned for a white page, invisible on the dark scheme's near-black surfaces. Depth comes from the `surface-container-*` tone scale first; a shadow is for things that genuinely float (dialogs, popovers, the FAB). The level is decided by the object, not by how much it should stand out:

| Object                                   | Level |
| ---------------------------------------- | ----- |
| Modal bottom sheet, search suggestions   | `e1`  |
| Menu popover / ordinary autocomplete, floating nav | `e2` |
| Dialog, FAB, snackbar                    | `e3`  |

**Ordinary controls and cards do not cast shadows, including on hover.** The user's visual direction is grounded tonal surfaces: buttons respond through their state layer and material, and `Card` variants through the surface scale. `elevated` remains a lighter surface variant for compatibility, with no rest or hover shadow. A pagination number, slider handle and table row are flat too. Shadows remain appropriate for a detached overlay whose overlap needs explaining — dialogs, sheets, menus and the floating nav — and stay on the shared elevation ladder. Do not blanket-remove box shadows: the keyboard ring and crop mask use that CSS mechanism without being elevation effects.

**The search suggestion view belongs to the search bar.** `Popover variant="search"` owns its matching width, 28dp corner, `surface-container-highest` and restrained `e1` overlap cue. Its 56dp suggestion rows sit 8dp inside the panel with 20dp corners and 2dp gaps. They appear on FastEffects (108ms at default speed) without scaling the text or staging a second child entrance; typing is a frequent action — and the list is **kept** while the next lookup is in flight (`keepPrevious` on `tagSuggestions`), replacing its rows when the answer lands rather than closing and reopening on every keystroke. With nothing typed it lists the recent searches (`LS_KEYS.searchHistory`, the original front end's key), opened by a press, a clear or ArrowDown, never by focus alone. Ordinary menu popovers use the 16dp outer shape and anchored menu transition described below.

**The ladder holds its alpha constant and grows only its geometry** — that is how M3 specifies it, and backwards is invisible one level at a time and obvious across the set: the light shadows once ramped 0.05 → 0.10 *as well as* growing their blur, so five levels spanned a factor of two in alpha and `e1` beside `e2` could not be told apart. Adding a level: copy the alpha, change the geometry.

**A surface that is not a box casts its shadow as a filter.** A box shadow follows the border box, so a speech bubble's tail — a square of the bubble's own material turned 45° — cast none, and the bubble read as a card with a sticker on its edge. `--md-sys-elevation-filter-2` is level 2 as a `filter: drop-shadow` pair, which follows alpha, so the tail shadows with the body; the mascot's bubble and the ponies' speech wear it, and the mascot's silhouette takes `--md-sys-elevation-drop-2` / `-3` for the same reason. A filter re-rasterises whenever what is under it changes, so it belongs on a small surface that changes rarely — a bubble, a figure — never on a list row or a panel that scrolls.

**The dark scheme is the one carve-out, and it is deliberate.** Its ladder does ramp — 0.30 → 0.45 on the key layer, 0.20 → 0.32 on the ambient — and starts at the spec's full 0.30 rather than the halved light value, because on a near-black ground a shadow is not reporting height, it is the only thing separating one surface from another, and the tonal steps already carry the height. Both are stated where the values live, in `globals.css`; do not "fix" either to match the paragraph above.

## Stacking order

App-level layers are the `--z-*` tokens in globals.css, used through their utilities (`z-dialog`, `z-popover`, `z-toast`, …); the order is stated once, there, with the reason for each step. Fourteen raw values were in use before, and two collided — `Toast` and `LoadingOverlay` were both at 9999, cover-order decided by DOM order — and the image detail's tag dialog carried a hand-typed 9998 meaning "above everything" that in fact put it one step *below* the lightbox.

Layering *inside* one component — a preview over its own final image, a switch handle over its own ripple — stays a small integer: those neighbours are the component's own children, and giving them tokens would imply they take part in the global order, the one thing they must not do.

## Typography

The fifteen M3 roles: `text-display-*`, `text-headline-*`, `text-title-*`, `text-body-*`, `text-label-*`. Never `text-sm`/`text-lg`/`text-[13px]`.

**The Chinese face is the platform's own — except on Windows.** Geist for Latin; a Han glyph falls to `--font-han`, the one half of the stack that depends on the platform (`--font-sans` and `--font-mono` end in it because the theme block is `inline`, so an override of those tokens would miss every font utility). Everywhere but Windows it is the system face: PingFang SC by name, then Noto Sans CJK / Source Han Sans, then `sans-serif`, which Chromium resolves per script under `lang="zh-CN"` (HarmonyOS Sans on HarmonyOS, the system face on Android), and the Noto Sans SC web font only last — so those platforms download nothing. **On Windows the web font leads** (`html[data-os='windows']`, set from the request in `app/layout.tsx` and confirmed by the pre-paint script): the one face every Windows machine has is Microsoft YaHei, and beside Geist it reads as dated — the owner's call, which amends decision 16. YaHei only fills the moment before a slice arrives, and that swap redraws glyphs without moving anything (a Han glyph is one em wide in both, every type role fixes its line height); a slice is fetched once and then comes from cache. Two names are kept out on purpose: PingFang SC from the Windows stack and HarmonyOS Sans from both. On a desktop either exists only when installed by hand, often as a single weight, and the matcher then paints every weight in that one file — a machine with only the Black file rendered all body text black.

**Every one of the fifteen roles declares its own `font-weight` token** (`TypeScaleTokens`; the seven that used to declare none — `display-*`, `headline-*`, `title-l` — were uniformly `WeightRegular`), and `@layer base` gives `h1`–`h6` only a size and a line height. Never add `font-bold`/`font-semibold` on top of a role — it overrides the token — and a role that does not declare its weight inherits one, which is the bug: `text-headline-s` once rendered 700 on `AuthModal`'s `<h1>`, 600 on `Modal`'s `<h2>` (the same dialog-title role at two weights), 400 on a `<span>`, with the 500 `-emphasized` lighter than either. Utilities sit in a later layer than `@layer base`, so declaring it is what makes a role render the same wherever it is put. **M3 headings are Regular** — the heavier line is the `-emphasized` role, a decision at the call site, not a property of the tag.

A bare `font-medium` / `font-semibold` on an element with **no** type role takes its size from whatever the parent happens to be — reach for the role's `-emphasized` twin instead, but check the weight you are actually getting, because the twins are **not** all one value: `label-*` and `title-m`/`title-s` step 500 → **700**, while `display-*`, `headline-*`, `title-l` and `body-*` step 400 → **500**. Tailwind's `font-medium` *is* 500, so on `text-label-l`, whose own token is already 500, it changes nothing at all — which is how three pieces of shared chrome (the home tab bar, `TabBar`, `Pagination`) marked their active item with no weight contrast, only colour.

Never emit two type roles on one element (`text-label-l` plus `text-label-l-emphasized`): `cn` is a plain join, the winner is decided by stylesheet order, not by which you wrote last — put the role inside each branch of the conditional so exactly one is ever applied.

## Rich-text rhythm

Two renderers put user prose on screen — `MarkdownRenderer` and `BBCodeRenderer` — and the rule is that **they emit the same document**, so one set of rules spaces both: `.bbcode-content` and `.rich-text-content` are styled together in globals.css, and neither renderer styles its own output. A direct message goes through neither: it is plain text (see the chat message note under Shape).

The forum's spacing had two causes, neither where it looked: nothing gave a paragraph a margin (Tailwind's preflight zeroes `p`; only `img`/`blockquote`/`pre` were given one back, so consecutive paragraphs touched while the space around a picture came from something else), and BBCode did not *have* paragraphs — every `\n` became a `<br>`, so a "paragraph break" was one line-height that could not agree with an image's margin beside it. `bbcodeToSafeHtml` now splits on blank lines into real `<p>`s, keeps single newlines as `<br>`, and eats the line breaks that end up adjacent to a block element.

The spacing is in `em`, so it scales with the container and a reply's rhythm is automatically tighter than an article's:

| Element      | `margin-block`             |
| ------------ | -------------------------- |
| `p`          | `0.75em`                   |
| `h1`–`h6`    | `1.25em 0.5em`             |
| `ul` / `ol`  | `0.75em` (+1.5em start pad)|
| `li`         | `0.25em`                   |
| img, video, blockquote, pre, table | `1em`    |

Which means **the container has to name a type role**, or the `em` is whatever it inherited: a forum post's body is `body-l` (it is an article) and a reply's is `body-m`. The `> :first-child` / `> :last-child` margin resets stay — they keep the last paragraph from pushing the action row down by a stray line.

**One line-break rule, in both renderers.** A newline is a line break and a blank line a paragraph — in `bbcodeToSafeHtml` and in `MarkdownRenderer` (`remarkHardBreaks`, `lib/markdownBreaks.ts`) — so a text lays out the same whichever renderer it reaches; it used to join lines through Markdown and keep them through BBCode (R6-049). `htmlToBBCode` writes a `<br>` as one newline and a paragraph as two, and a paragraph holding nothing but breaks is dropped (the original front end's `[p][br][/p]`, R6-050). Lists wear their markers (preflight removes them): disc, decimal, a circle when nested, the marker in `on-surface-variant`.

**A colour a writer chose is kept legible, and it is never an inline `color`.** `[color=]` renders `<span data-ink style="--rt-ink:…">` and the stylesheet clamps its lightness against the scheme (relative colour inside `light-dark()`, under `@supports`), so a dark ink picked on a light page stays readable on the dark one; `[bg=]` — the editor's 背景颜色 — is `data-highlight`, the colour at a 35% mix. **A picture** goes through the optimizer when it may fetch it (PicPony uploads, derpicdn's `/img/`; `getImageProps`), holds a placeholder until `complete && naturalWidth > 0` (`data-loading`, then `data-failed` after an error), loads lazily, and outside a link is a real button (`.rt-image`) that opens `PicLightbox` at that picture — inside a link the link takes the press. **A link** to this app (or to picpony.top) routes in place (`lib/richTextLinks.ts`); any other opens a new tab.

**A quote's surface is relative to its host**: `--rt-quote-surface`, defaulting to `surface-container-high`, one step up from a `surface-container-low` host — a reply row, and the thread's post card, which is `Card variant="elevated"` for that and because it is the tone of the list row it grows out of (the quote used to be a lighter hole in a `highest` card, R6-051). A host on a higher step sets the variable. The `<cite>` is `on-surface-variant`: it is text, not a mark.

**The editor writes only what the renderer draws** (`RichTextEditor`): bold, italic, underline, strike, colour, highlight, clear, two heading levels, lists, alignment (`[center]` / `[right]`), links, pictures and tables — no font size, indent, code block, picture width or full-width table, which have no BBCode form and vanished on publish (R6-043). Its row holds the tools that fit, most wanted first, and offers the rest behind 更多, whose expanded form wraps (planned from the row's own width, with a panel tool's arrow counted); `size` is the editing area — `post` 200 / 300px, `reply` 120 / 160px (phone / `sm` up), the fixed 300px without it. Import it with `dynamic()` and `EditorPlaceholder` (`components/RichTextEditorShell.tsx`) as `loading`, which is its footprint; the handle is `focus()`.

## Copy

The UI is Chinese, and the conventions below are the whole of it. Every one had drifted in both directions at once, and the wrong half is invisible to anyone reading a single file.

| | |
| --- | --- |
| Ellipsis | `…`, never `...` (44 sites used ASCII against 2 correct ones) |
| Colon after a Chinese label | `：`, never `: ` |
| Comma inside a Chinese sentence | `，`; half-width `,` only in a value the user types |
| Parentheses in Chinese prose | `（）`; keep `( )` only where the parenthetical is a Latin identifier — `(banAnthro)`, `(GIF/视频)` |
| A Latin run beside CJK | one space: `图片 ID`, `API Key` (all ten no-space sites were in `components/admin/`) |
| Toasts | no `！` — a snackbar is a status report (7 of 291 shouted) |
| Confirm body | `确定要…吗？`, with the formal 此/该 demonstrative; never 这个, never a bracketed 【…】, never `！` |
| Confirm title / button | `确认X` / `确认` (`useConfirm` and `usePrompt` both default to 确认) |
| One verb per operation | 删除 destroys a record, 移除 detaches one, 清空 empties a list (`/history` used two for one button) |
| Success | `已<verb>` (47 sites) rather than `<verb>成功` (28) |
| A finished run's line | `已<verb> N 张`, with `，M 张失败` — from the run's own counts (`runSummary`), in place of 正在…（N / N）; not a live region, since the run's toast says it |
| Failure | `{X}加载失败`, noun first; one string for one condition (`网络错误` had six wordings across 24 sites) — the line under it is the error's own `message`, from the one table in `lib/api/errors.ts` (`网络连接失败，请检查网络后再试`, `请求超时，请稍后再试`, `内容不存在或已被删除` …) |

The confirm register is the instructive one, because the copy split was the *architecture* split seen from the other side: every tab that hand-rolled its own `Modal` dropped the sentence-final 吗, and every tab that used `useConfirm` kept it. Fixing one was fixing the other.

## Motion

**The motion family follows the object.** Page transitions use an easing and a duration; handles, indicators and small state changes use the spring roles below. Anchored menus are an explicit project exception: their measured trigger-to-panel transform follows Vuetify's arrangement on `MENU_TRANSITION`, using the app's own duration and curve tokens. That exception does not change a switch handle or tab indicator into a menu transition.

### Springs — component motion

Nine responses, from M3's own generated token sets (`StandardMotionTokens` / `ExpressiveMotionTokens`). Use them through the `spring-*` utilities in CSS or `spring('name')` in GSAP — both carry the shape **and** its duration:

|            | standard        | expressive       | settle |
| ---------- | --------------- | ---------------- | ------ |
| fast spatial   | ζ0.9 k1400  | ζ0.6 k800        | 137 / 221ms |
| default spatial | ζ0.9 k700  | ζ0.8 k380        | 194 / 326ms |
| slow spatial   | ζ0.9 k300   | ζ0.8 k200        | 296 / 449ms |
| fast effects   | ζ1.0 k3800  | identical        | 108ms  |
| default effects | ζ1.0 k1600 | identical        | 166ms  |
| slow effects   | ζ1.0 k800   | identical        | 235ms  |

Pick by **what is changing**, then by how far it travels:

- **spatial** — it moves or resizes. Damping is below 1, so it may overshoot, and that overshoot is what reads as mass.
- **effects** — damping is exactly 1, critically damped, so it *cannot* overshoot. Usually that is what a fade or a recolour wants: an overshooting colour is a flash, and an overshooting opacity is **clipped** — a `linear()` value above 1 is capped, so the fade reaches full opacity early and then sits there. Not only fades: reach for it whenever overshoot would be *wrong*, position included — M3 closes its own navigation drawer on `FastEffects` because a panel that overshoots on the way out bounces back into view.

`expressive-fast-spatial` (ζ0.6) is the only visibly bouncy curve in the system — 8.4% overshoot, peaking at 63% of the run. It is for a small mark arriving in place: an unread count, a favourite filling in, a radio dot landing; anything large wearing it reads as a wobble. And never put an `opacity` on it: its table is above 1 from 45% to 97% of the run, which is why the radio's dot and the icon-swap keyframe split their scale and their opacity across the two families. **And only on arrival**: `CountBadge` and `Checkbox` pop on a change they saw (0 → n, unchecked → checked), never on mount — a checked box in a dialog that just opened, or a cached count on a screen you came back to, is not arriving.

**A component reaches for a *role*, not for a ζ and a k** — the shape of the API in AOSP (`MotionSchemeKeyTokens.DefaultSpatial`, resolved by whichever `MotionScheme` is in force). Nine utilities exposing ζ and k directly is how the drawer once picked standard's slow tier, the tab indicator expressive's default tier, and the switch a bezier. **This app's scheme is `standard`**, which is also `MaterialTheme`'s own default; the expressive trio is a documented exception for a small mark landing in place.

The assignments, with component sources and project-specific transition choices distinguished:

| Object | Motion | From |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Navigation drawer, opening (and drag release) | `default-spatial` | `NavigationDrawer.kt` |
| Navigation drawer, closing | `fast-effects` | `NavigationDrawer.kt` |
| Menu, listbox, ordinary autocomplete | `MENU_TRANSITION`, 200ms enter / 150ms exit | Anchored menu transition below |
| Search suggestions | `fast-effects`, opacity only | `Popover variant="search"` |
| Tooltip | `fast-effects` | `Tooltip.kt` |
| Tab indicator | `default-spatial` | `TabRow.kt` |
| Tab strip (the shared axis between tab panes) | critically damped, launched at twice its mean speed, 400ms — a stated divergence | `lib/tabStrip.ts`; see "Transitions between screens" |
| Tab lean (a leaning panel's blocks) | the strip's own motion, each block on screen 0–24ms late by its top, counted from the panel's own top on screen — no curve of its own | `leanDelay` / `lagSamples` in `lib/tabStrip.ts` |
| Switch handle | `fast-spatial` | `Switch.kt` |
| Bottom sheet | `default-effects` | `ModalBottomSheet.kt` |
| Slider | none *on position* — a slider reports where the input is, and a transition would put the mark behind the finger. Its handle's *width* is a state and does spring (`fast-spatial`) | `Slider.kt` |
| Button pressed corner | none — see the control-height section for why the morph came out | — |
| Shared-element flight, opening | `fastOutSlowIn` over **250ms** (M3 `medium1`) — *not* a ladder tier | a from-rest leg flies a curve, so its length is a step on M3's duration scale rather than a spring settle time; the ζ0.9 spatial spring is the *interruption* model and its `rate` is normalised, so it fits any clock |
| Shared-element flight, closing | same curve, same 250ms | Flutter's Hero rides one route animation, so a push and a pop are one duration read both ways; the direction difference lives in the thresholds |
| Shared-element flight, swipe-release | `slow-spatial`, sliding to `fast-spatial` | `NavigationDrawer.kt`'s drag release — a gesture, so it keeps the spatial ζ0.9 |
| Forum container transform, both ways | the tab strip's response — critically damped, launched at twice its mean speed — over the route cross-fade's 400ms; not `emphasized` | `lib/forumContainer.ts`; see "Transitions between screens" |

A collapsible panel a press opens or closes is the drawer's case: the two drawer springs, per direction, on every clock in the gesture.

Nine springs, **four shapes** — arithmetic, not a shortcut: normalise the timeline by the settle time and the curve depends only on the damping ratio, stiffness deciding duration alone (verified to 1e-4). So globals.css ships four `linear()` tables and nine durations, and the `spring-*` utilities pair them. **The two must never be split at a call site**: a ζ0.6 shape on a 449ms clock is not a slower bounce, it is a different object — the pairing lives in the utility and there is no `duration-spring-*` utility to reach for on its own.

Both renderers derive from one closed form in `lib/spring.ts`: CSS gets 32-point `linear()` samples, GSAP the function itself registered as an ease, so the scripted side has no interpolation error. `--ease-spring` and `eases.spring` are gone — they were `cubic-bezier(0.18, 1.36, 0.5, 1)` and `back.out(1.55)`, two independent approximations of the same physics.

### Curves — transitions

M3 ships **two** easing sets and they are not interchangeable: `emphasized` is for a large container transform or anything crossing the screen; `standard` is for a small utilitarian change. Both have a decelerate and an accelerate variant:

| Token                          | Set        | Use                      |
| ------------------------------ | ---------- | ------------------------ |
| `--ease-standard`              | standard   | begins and ends on screen |
| `--ease-standard-decelerate`   | standard   | a small thing entering    |
| `--ease-standard-accelerate`   | standard   | a small thing leaving     |
| `--ease-emphasized`            | emphasized | a large container transform |
| `--ease-emphasized-decelerate` | emphasized | a page entering           |
| `--ease-emphasized-accelerate` | emphasized | a page leaving            |
| `--ease-symmetric`             | —          | an infinite alternating loop (reach for `--ease-loop`, its role name) |

`--ease-decelerate` / `--ease-accelerate` are **aliases of the emphasized pair**, which is what every existing call site means by them — they were the only decelerate and accelerate the app had, so every small utility transition was reaching for a curve shaped for a full-window slide. Prefer the qualified name in new code.

In GSAP: `ease: 'standard' | 'standard-decelerate' | 'standard-accelerate' | 'decelerate' | 'accelerate' | 'emphasized' | 'symmetric' | 'loop'`.

**Every M3 curve is one-sided, and there is a third case neither verb covers.** The tell is not the curve's name, it is where it spends the travel — percentage of the distance covered in each tenth of the duration:

| Curve                   | per tenth of the duration        | half-travel by |
| ----------------------- | -------------------------------- | -------------- |
| `emphasized-decelerate` | 62 16 8 5 3 2 1 1 0 0            | 7%             |
| `standard`              | 16 34 19 11 8 5 3 2 1 0          | 20%            |
| `emphasized-accelerate` | 1 2 3 4 6 8 11 14 20 32          | 82%            |
| `symmetric`             | 2 7 11 14 16 16 14 11 7 2        | 50%            |

The one-sidedness is deliberate and right for the two verbs M3 models, *arrive* and *leave*: the front-loaded half of an arrival happens while the object is still mostly off-screen.

A thing **travelling in place**, with both ends of its journey on screen, is neither: it starts and stops at rest, and on `standard` its fastest tenth carries **97x** what its last tenth does — read, correctly, as a jump followed by a stall. **The answer is a spring, not a third row** (see the panel note below). What survives here is only the *loop*: `--ease-symmetric` is 7.6:1 and identical at both ends by construction, and `--ease-loop` is its role name, because a loop has no arrival and a one-sided curve makes its velocity discontinuous once per cycle. **Reach for the role name** — every non-loop use of `ease-symmetric` was a travelling-in-place case that wanted the physics; the seven determinate progress bars that had it are `ProgressBar` on `spring-slow-effects` now.

**Vuetify is the reference for the arrangement**, and it is worth copying whole: its navigation drawer uses *one* curve and *one* duration for both directions, and its scrim shares both — `$navigation-drawer-transition-duration: 0.2s`, `$navigation-drawer-transition-timing-function: settings.$standard-easing`, resolving to Material **2**'s `cubic-bezier(0.4, 0, 0.2, 1)`. One curve serves both directions because a symmetric one is the same shape read backwards, and the scrim shares the clock because it is not a component fading in place — it is the other half of the panel moving. M2's curve still tails off at 43:1, so what is borrowed is the arrangement and the shape family, not the literal value; M2 easings remain a bug wherever the tokens reach.

**If a container animates its size, check that its contents travel with it.** A box shrinking under `overflow-hidden` whose child holds a fixed width does not move that child — it guillotines it: one edge sweeping across stationary content while the neighbouring content slides at the curve's rate, two rates in one gesture, one of them zero. Prefer translating the container and closing the layout behind it with a negative margin: the contents come along because they are inside the thing that moves, the box never resizes so its subtree never re-lays-out, and the visible half of the motion is a composited `translate`. The measurement that settles it is one line in the console — sample the *inner* element's `getBoundingClientRect().left` per frame, not the container's; if it reads `0 -> 0`, no curve will help. And sample per **decile of the duration**, not "time at N% of travel": frame quantisation moves the percentage marks by a whole frame, enough to invent a velocity spike that no frame contains.

Still name the curve at every call site — a bare `transition-opacity` cannot know which kind of movement a transition is. `--default-transition-timing-function` is re-pointed at `--ease-standard` (200ms) in `globals.css`, so a forgotten one degrades to a system curve rather than to Material **2**'s, but that is a safety net, not the convention.

A raw `cubic-bezier()` is a bug wherever the tokens can reach. Two exceptions, both commented:

- Web Animations takes an `easing:` string, where a CSS `var()` is not resolved. New callers use `EASE` / `MENU_TRANSITION` from `lib/motionTokens.ts` or `springTiming()` from `lib/springTiming.ts`; `Modal`, `Popover` and the hero's chrome fades use these shared values. The top loader's `easing` prop in `app/layout.tsx` remains a literal token value; keep it in sync. `springTiming()` also applies the reduced tier's shape substitution, which a component's own cached spring string would miss.
- The theme wipe is genuinely off-scale, for the same reason `.m3-progress-arc` *was*: it animates a **radius**, and what the eye reads is the area swept, which goes as the square. Every M3 curve is one-sided and spends its travel up front, so squaring it finishes the wipe before it registers as one — measured as the fraction of screen flipped 150ms into 550: symmetric ease-in-out 3%, `emphasized` 65%, `decelerate` 87%. A radius wants a curve that is slow at both ends: `--ease-loop` is that curve and `.m3-progress-arc` uses it now; the wipe still spells it out, for the `var()` reason above.

`linear` is correct only for a spinner's rotation and for a keyframe track that has already been sampled along a curve (the hero flight, the sink).

`emphasized` is two cubic segments, so it has no `cubic-bezier()` form — the CSS token is a sampled `linear()` and GSAP takes the spec path. It hangs back, then runs through the middle at 2.5x `standard`'s peak speed, which is why a value tuned against `standard` cannot be carried over: the GSAP tab wave's lag had to drop from 0.07 to 0.032 to keep the same visual shear. (Its compositor successor is tuned in milliseconds against the strip's spring instead: 24ms at the bottom of the view is a peak lean of 16% of the window, half the 0.32 budget; the full 48ms, 31%, read as exaggerated.)

Durations: `DURATION` in `lib/motion.ts`, mirrored by Tailwind's `duration-*`.

| Situation                                     | Duration | Curve        |
| --------------------------------------------- | -------- | ------------ |
| Enters the screen                             | 400ms    | `decelerate` |
| Leaves the screen                             | 200ms    | `accelerate` |
| Begins and ends on screen (hover, colour)     | 200ms    | `standard`   |
| Large container transform                     | 500ms    | `emphasized` |
| Press down                                    | 100ms    | `standard`   |
| State layer settling in or out                | 150ms    | `standard`   |
