**Every pager needs a `[data-pagination-anchor]`, and it has to be an *ancestor* of the pager.** `Pagination` finds it with `closest()`, which fails silently: a marker on the list with the pager as its *sibling* is one the pager cannot see, and the turn falls back exactly as if there were none. Several pagers may share one enclosing anchor (a profile's four tabs use the box that holds the tab row and the panes), and a pager handed to a list component as `children` is inside its anchor by construction. A pager inside a dialog needs a *scroller* as well: the box with `overflow-y: auto` carries `data-app-scroll-container`, which `Pagination` also finds with `closest()` — `Modal`'s body and `Sheet`'s body have it. The admin feedback dialog uses that body directly, without a second nested scroller.

**A cursor pager offers only pages that can exist.** Without `totalPages` it knows the current page and whether there is a next one, so its window ends at the current page plus one — it used to draw five numbers regardless, and a two-result search offered pages 2–5 beside a disabled 下一页.

**The per-tab memory is keyed on the page and the tab group's position on it**, then on the tab name (`lib/tabScroll.ts`). Tab values are unique app-wide, but the collision is between *instances of one screen*: `posts`/`uploads`/`faves`/`comments` carried an offset from one profile to the next, and the pathname carries the profile's id. It was a `WeakMap` on the panel element, which went with every navigation because `[data-page-content]` is keyed on the pathname — the forum is a tab on `/`, so reading a thread and coming back threw the gallery's place away. Its lifetime is the route memory's rule: a page reached by traversal keeps its tabs' offsets, a push clears that pathname's, a session change clears all.

**A tab switch only moves the scroller when the panel is the page.** The memory is what makes leaving the gallery at row 20 and coming back land on row 20 — but applying it on a screen with a header above the panel drags that header, which reads as a jump. So the decision is a property of the *screen*, measured rather than assumed — `panelTop`, the shared chrome above `[data-tab-panel]`, is **24px on the home route** (the page gutter; the tab pill is fixed chrome outside the scroller), **209px on /policy** (page header and tab row) and **914px on a profile** (banner, name, level bar, badge wall, account card, tab row; 632 without a banner). Above `TAB_SHARED_CHROME_PX`, the app bar's own 64dp, the scroller is left alone and only `finalMax`'s clamp can move it. Per-screen rather than per-scroll-position on purpose: a rule that fires depending on how far you happen to have scrolled is not one a user can learn.

**And it positions under the 关闭 tier.** A scroll position is state, not decoration; the preference asks for less movement, not for less positioning. Under 关闭 the switch takes the animated path's own code with no run — `runTabTransition` lands in the task it starts — so the offset, the `panelTop` test (the half that must not be dropped: without it, turning the preference on is what makes a profile's 914px jump reachable again), the `?? 0` and the floor are one implementation, not two. Every switch records the offset it leaves, the reactive path included: the driver reads it in `getSnapshotBeforeUpdate`, before the commit can move it — the pre-commit hook the old hook-based driver lacked, so a tab reached by a sidebar link or the back button is remembered too. `applyInstantTabScroll` remains only for the moment before the engine's chunk has arrived (`lib/motionLazy.tsx`).

**A tab with no remembered offset opens at its own top — on a screen whose panel is the page.** "Stay exactly where you are" is right on a screen with shared chrome and is the same bug wearing the other face where the panel *is* the page: measured at 1440×900, leave the gallery at 1500, switch to a forum whose whole content is 1708 against a 768px scrollport, and `finalMax` is 1160 — the carried-over offset clamps to exactly the maximum and **the forum opens on its last row**. Both paths take the `?? 0` (`runTabTransition` and `applyInstantTabScroll`), and the memory is untouched: switching back still lands on 1500. `node scripts/netAuditTabHeight.mjs` asserts all three numbers. It also checks the transit: during the run the panel measures `max(H_out, H_in)`, so a tall gallery leaves ~2000px under a short forum for the run — deliberate (see `runTabTransition`), invisible because the footer is held at `opacity: 0` for the transit and the offset is clamped against the *settled* height.

## `useGSAP`

`@gsap/react`'s `useGSAP` does **not** run your cleanup on a dependency change unless you pass `revertOnUpdate: true`. Without it, listeners, `Observer`s and `ScrollTrigger`s accumulate on every dep change. Pass it, or keep the changing value in a ref and shrink the dependency list.

## Companions: the mascot and the desktop ponies

Both are shell chrome, mounted by `components/mascot/CompanionHost.tsx` after the page's useful paint and never in a route's first document. **The ponies' own chunk waits for a selection**: `CompanionRuntime` reads the account's choice itself (one small `get_my_ponies`) and mounts `PoniesRuntime` — the engine's frame, its stylesheet, the catalogue read — only once it is non-empty, latched per account so 清空 still fades the ponies on the engine's clock and its 撤销 finds the frame. It used to mount the runtime to find out, so every signed-in cold load paid both reads and the chunk for an empty lineup. Not a synced flag: the original front end edits the same selection and would leave one stale.

**What hides them is `useCompanionPresence` (`lib/mascot/visibility.ts`), and a navigation is not on the list.** `present` is the document being on screen; `away` is a modal layer, an open picture, the lightbox, the on-screen keyboard, a picture's own page or the admin console. Away, the mascot fades and the ponies pause where they stand. A route transit and a hero flight are neither: this used to be `isScreenQuiet()`, which is false for both, so the mascot blinked out and back on every navigation and the ponies' frame was torn down and rebuilt — every pony gone for a second or two and respawned somewhere else. `isScreenQuiet` is for the app's own interruptions (the announcement), which wait for the user to be between tasks; it is not a predicate for chrome.

**A floating element rises above the home pill on the pill's own legs.** The pill publishes its band's height as `--app-bottom-chrome` and the direction it went as `data-bottom-chrome` on `<html>`; the mascot's lift takes the arriving page's 80ms + 320ms `decelerate` when the band appears and the leaving page's 100ms `accelerate` when it goes, so it rises *with* the pill instead of on a spring of its own. The ponies' layer stops above the band.

**The mascot keeps one box.** The stage is the art's box in both states; collapsed, the control is a 56dp plate (M3's FAB) at the stage's bottom-right with the figure tucked into it, and the plate, the figure and the collapse control each animate inside that box — switching the box itself in one frame dropped an open bubble onto the figure and flashed a disc the art's size. The collapse control stays mounted and fades with the figure (`inert` while collapsed). The greeting on expanding opens on the figure's own `transitionend`, bounded by the wall-clock rule. Its silhouette shadows are `--md-sys-elevation-drop-2` / `-3` — a `filter: drop-shadow`, because a character is an alpha shape and a box shadow would draw its transparent margins as a slab.

**The mascot answers only on its body.** Its hit region is the character's outline, applied as `clip-path: path()` on the `.hit` box — which clips hit-testing with the paint — so the artwork's transparent margins and the gap between a wing and the tail are the page's: no hover, no press, no pointer cursor there. The outline is grown 3px under a mouse and 8px under a finger, round at a tip; an ellipse over the art stands in until it lands, or if it never does. Two consequences. **The collapse control keeps off the character**: a finger's 48px target around that 32dp button reached the ear of a small figure, and a tap there tucked the figure away. So where the head reaches the art's top-right corner, the control rises by what the outline says it must (`shapeLift`) until its target — the touch floor around it — keeps 4px off the character, and the `.hit` box sits above it anyway (it paints nothing), so wherever the two could still meet, what is drawn under the finger answers. Under a finger the control is always shown, so while the figure speaks it steps aside — the bubble has its own close — rather than push the bubble up above it, far off the head. And a pointer crossing a gap in the outline is still on the character: the lift waits 150ms (`HOVER_GRACE_MS`) before settling, and an enter that soon after a leave is the same visit — without both, every gap bobbed the figure and greeted again.

**The outline is read on the server** (`app/mascot-shape/route.ts`; the model, run-length rows on a 96-cell grid, is the import-free `lib/mascot/shapeModel.ts`), because the asset host sends no CORS headers — a canvas holding the picture is tainted — and the optimizer, which would give a same-origin copy, is off in development. **It is not a proxy, and that is the security of the endpoint**: only the runs leave the handler; the target is pinned to the asset host, an image path with no query, credentials or redirect; the body is bounded in bytes and pixels. Answers are memoised per address, failures are not. Its exposure is the optimizer's — an unauthenticated read of an image on a host `remotePatterns` already allows. The same outline says where the head is (`shapeHead`), which is where the bubble points.

**Each motion owns a block box.** `.pose` (the hover lift and the silhouette shadow), `.squash` (the press) and `.react` (the WAAPI hop) are spans inside the button and are `display: block` — inline, none of their transforms drew (the fourth trap). The press squashes the figure about its feet, `scale(1.05, 0.93)` on FastSpatial, released on the expressive fast spring, whose overshoot is the spring in it, and it grounds the hover lift for the squash to push against — under a mouse the pointer is always over what it presses. Press comes from pointer events (the `ToggleSwitch` reasoning); a finger's press waits for the tap timeout, and a tap too quick for it still squashes for `MIN_PRESS_MS` (120ms, scaled). A tip's hop waits for the squash to let go, so the release springs into it. There is no idle loop: a breath at the feet would re-run the silhouette's blur every frame for as long as a page is open, for two pixels of movement.

**One utterance at a time, standing over the head.** What is on screen leaves on FastEffects before the next one rises — a tap's tip after a hover's, the hold's countdown after a tip — so the bubble never changes its words and its size in one frame. A hover greets once per visit and never replaces an open tip; a tap asks for another. The bubble is measured whenever it is given something to say: centred over the head where the scroller allows, never past the art's right edge or within 16px of the scroller's left, its tail on the head and its foot 14px above it. Under a mouse, where it would pass over the collapse control, it steps left of the control while its tail can still reach the head, and stands above it only otherwise — standing above a risen control put a small figure's tail 23px off its head. Its shadow is the filter level in the Elevation section, so the tail casts one. Holding for three seconds (or Space) opens 彩彩 AI; the countdown appears after 350ms as its own bubble, its meter the hold's own linear clock, which every tier shows. **Letting go after the countdown has shown is cancelling**, not a tap: no tip and no hop answer it, and the meter stays where the hold left it while the bubble fades.

**Collapsed, the plate is a FAB**: 56dp on `surface-container-high` at `e3`, the control's ripple painting into it (`data-ripple="inner"`, the plate its host) and its `::before` the state layer for hover, focus and press. The collapse control is a tonal `IconButton` with the chevron a docked player collapses with, revealed with the figure's hover and held 240ms after the pointer leaves it (a tooltip's grace — the way to it crosses the art's empty corner), always shown under a finger.

**The ponies run the original Browser Ponies engine in a sandboxed, opaque-origin frame** (`public/companions/ponies-frame.*`), with one invisible control per pony laid over its sprite only while it matters (`PoniesRuntime`). Five rules hold it up:

- **`color-scheme: light` on the `<iframe>` is load-bearing.** A frame whose document's scheme differs from its element's gets an opaque canvas — under the dark scheme the transparent frame painted the content area white.
- **The layer is the content area's box, and the frame is the window's.** The layer sits beside the scroller in the box the scroller fills (`absolute`, its bottom raised by `--app-bottom-chrome`), so a docked drawer moves and resizes it in the page's own layout — no script, never a frame behind — and it is `z-companions`: above the route cross-fade's still frame, below the page chrome (the back affordance, the home pill, the mascot), which a pony walks behind. The frame inside it is window-sized, and no change of the content area resizes it: a sandboxed frame is its own process, and resized on every frame of a drawer run it held each of the page's frames on its new surface (one pony: 18 dropped frames in six runs, none without). The engine walks a *stage* instead — the layer's box, sent once it has held still for two frames, which `innerWidth` / `innerHeight` answer with inside the frame — and a pony a smaller stage leaves outside walks back in at once, first put at the edge unseen if it is out of sight. Through a run the ponies ride the content area's leading edge, as the page does: the trailing edge would sweep the drawer over them.
- **A change of selection is a diff, applied in place** (`configs`): a pony that left fades on the engine's clock, one that joined walks in, the rest carry on. Configurations are read only for ponies not yet loaded.
- **Covered, the engine pauses; it is not unmounted.** `pause` / `resume` reach the frame as messages; the frame keeps its reasons (`away`, and `focus` while the keyboard holds a pony) so one ending does not wake the ponies under another, defers the engine's start while held, queues a selection change for the release (the engine draws a spawn only into a running clock) and stops its own rect loop. The layer fades on FastEffects meanwhile. Unmounted only when the document hides, the tier leaves 标准 or the account changes.
- **An emptied frame is kept for ten seconds** (`EMPTY_GRACE_MS`) and **a pony back within a minute walks in where it left** — so 清空 fades each pony out instead of cutting the frame, and its 撤销 puts them back where they stood.

**A pony's speech is the mascot's bubble, standing over its speaker.** The engine places a bubble once — centred on the sprite with its own padding, hanging below it — and leaves it there while the pony walks on. The frame wraps the engine's one `speak` method (once, on the prototype) to adopt the bubble it appends, and re-places every speaking bubble each frame after all the rects are read, so following costs no forced layout: centred over the sprite, above it (below only where the top of the window is in the way, and kept on its side while it still fits, so a pony walking along an edge does not flip it), its tail on the sprite, 8px inside the window. It wears the mascot's material — container, ink, the large corner, `body-m` read off a probe, the filter shadow — sent in the `theme` message, since the frame's document is outside the tokens' reach.

/settings' 桌面小马 saves a run of choices as one debounced write and **keeps showing its draft until the resource shows the saved selection** — `write` publishes on a paint boundary, and clearing the draft as the save landed reverted the lineup for a frame and replayed its motion. In the lineup each place is pinned to its own cell, so a pony that leaves keeps its cell while it shrinks and fades under the one sliding in over it.

## Offline, and the service worker

`public/sw.js` is hand-written; there is no Workbox. Its job is narrow on purpose: make the static assets instant on a repeat visit, and give an offline hard refresh somewhere to land. It is **not** an offline-first shell — `lib/resource.ts` already holds a TTL'd cache with SWR, revalidation on tab return and real cancellation, and a second cache underneath it, one that cannot see request identity or staleness or the route policy, would fight it.

Rule zero is `if (request.method !== 'GET') return;` — a bare `return`, not `respondWith`, so every mutation goes through the browser's own stack untouched.

| | |
| --- | --- |
| Documents / navigations | network only; the `catch` serves `/offline.html` |
| `/_next/static/*` | cache-first, **network fallback** |
| `/_next/image*` | stale-while-revalidate, LRU 120, cache name **unversioned** |
| `/api.php`, `/relay`, `/search-api/*`, RSC payloads | never touched |
| `derpicdn.net`, `wsrv.nl`, `147052.xyz` | never touched |

Each "never" is a bug avoided rather than a preference:

- **Documents** carry three request-scoped things since the SSR pass — the first page of the feed on a 2-minute TTL, the inlined request-line policy (whose whole purpose is to be in force *before* the session's first request), and the `<html>` attributes from the appearance cookies. A cached document paints the previous visitor's theme, which is exactly the flash `COOKIE_KEYS` exists to prevent. It is also the update mechanism: the document always names the current build's chunk URLs, and `/_next/static/*`'s network fallback is what lets a client still running an old document fetch a chunk this cache has never seen.
- **`/api.php`** carries the PHP session, and its route handler exists solely to rewrite `Set-Cookie` so that session survives plain HTTP. A cached body plus a cached `Set-Cookie` on a shared device hands one user's session to the next.
- **`/relay`** validates protocol, host, port, credentials **and path**, and this file states that the path check *is* the security of the endpoint. A cache serves an old validated response for a URL the current policy may no longer allow, and it echoes the upstream `content-type`.
- **The three image proxies** are cross-origin with no `crossorigin` on the `<img>`, so their responses are **opaque** — a 500 is indistinguishable from a 200, the same blindness the image *probes* hit. One cached failure pins a dead line for the session and defeats `lib/imageLoader.ts`'s degrade ladder. Opaque entries also bill ~7 MB each against the origin quota and a gallery page holds fifty. `/_next/image` already covers the gallery.

**No `skipWaiting()` and no `clients.claim()`**, and that is this app rather than caution in general: it is a long-lived document with a module-scope resource store and a live hero session, and it now lazy-loads chunks. Swapping the worker mid-session means the page's loaded chunks are build N while the ones it is about to `import()` come from build N+1's cache — a `ChunkLoadError`. There is no `message` listener either: the one reserved for a future "new version ready" prompt called `skipWaiting()` for any same-origin script that asked, which re-enabled exactly that swap. The handler and its prompt land in one change.

**`/offline.html` is a static file, not a route.** The worker serves the cached response at the URL the user asked for, so a fallback served under a foreign URL cannot be a routed page — the `app/offline/page.tsx` version was hydrated against `location.pathname === '/tasks'` and re-rendered as an app shell full of 加载失败. It carries no script, no font and no request, and its three colours are the default palette's spelled out — a knowing divergence from the token rule, because this document is outside the design system's reach by construction.

`experimental.useOffline` is the other half and they do not overlap: it keeps a soft navigation, prefetch, RSC fetch or Server Action **pending and retrying** through a drop, which the worker cannot do; the worker covers the one case no retry reaches, a hard refresh with nothing on the wire. `<OfflineBanner>` is what tells the user why something is taking a while — without it, "pending" and "broken" look the same. It is a row of the shell's chrome under the app bar (so it counts towards `--app-chrome-bottom` and covers neither the home pill nor a composer), announced through a separate status region.

`app/manifest.ts` omits **`theme_color`** deliberately: the app has eleven palettes, so any single value is wrong for nine, and `app/layout.tsx` already renders that tag from the palette cookie — the same reason Next's own `viewport.themeColor` export was removed. `background_color` paints only the installed app's splash. `npm run icons` produces 192px, 512px and maskable 512px icons from the existing square mark; the maskable artwork fits wholly inside the 80%-diameter safe circle. Keep the source mark and generator together when changing the installed-app artwork.

Registration is `components/ServiceWorker.tsx`, on an idle callback, with a `?v=<buildId>` query (a file in `public/` cannot read a build-time variable, and a changing script URL is what makes the browser find a new worker) and `updateViaCache: 'none'` (without it the browser may satisfy its own update check from the HTTP cache and never see one; `next.config.ts`'s `headers()` covers the other half of that failure). It opts out under `navigator.webdriver`, which covers the Playwright suites — and **not** the probes that drive Edge over plain CDP (`net:*`, `perf:*`), which launch without the automation flag: there the worker registered during a session's warm-up and answered `/_next/image` from its own thread, where `Fetch` interception never sees a request, so the stubs were bypassed and a cold `/` measured 235 image requests instead of 30. Every such probe sends `Network.setBypassServiceWorker` before it installs its stubs; `perf:sw` is the one that must not.

## The React Compiler

On, through **Babel**, with `experimental.turbopackRustReactCompiler` deliberately off.

**The Rust port emits nothing here.** The built client chunks contain **0** memo-cache call sites against **356** with the Babel transform; the only `useMemoCache` references left are React's own runtime definitions. A compiler that silently optimises no files is worse than one that is off, because the flag says otherwise. The count is one grep over `.next/static/chunks/*.js` for the compiler's slot idiom, and it belongs in any Next upgrade; `next.config.ts` spells the command out beside the flag.

**What it buys** (`npm run perf:metrics`, `Performance.getMetrics` deltas, median of 9): a tab switch's `ScriptDuration` 0.076s ? 0.062s, **?18%**; a cold load of `/` 0.211s ? 0.223s, +6%. A later tab-switch measurement (median of 7) recorded `ScriptDuration` 0.038s beside `RecalcStyleDuration` 0.244s across 229 style recalculations; that style figure is total interaction cost, not an isolated compiler delta, and belongs beside the script figure when this trade is reviewed. The trade is taken because a cold load happens once while the interactions happen all session. (A 5-sample run reported the cold regression at +33%; it is noise at that size.)

**What it costs is bytes**, and the current F5 production snapshot puts `/policy` at **309,498 brotli** (`fix/F5-integration/perf_weight.log`). The older 270,655-on / 254,873-off pair is historical and is not a same-build baseline for the integrated F5 tree, so it must not be quoted as the current compiler delta. Keep a same-build on/off weight comparison before assigning that byte cost to the compiler; if a first visit on a slow link becomes the priority, `reactCompiler: false` is still the first flag to reconsider.

It was enabled **last** on purpose, so anything it broke would be attributable to it. The risk surface here is not the usual one:

- **Render-phase writes** exist on purpose and each is guarded by an *identity* comparison — `lib/resource.ts`'s `setRetained` against `snapshot.data`, and `AppLayout`'s drawer and background-location state. A memoised snapshot that changed identity for an unchanged value loops rather than merely re-renders, so this is the first place to look.
- **`useGSAP`'s `dependencies`** is a runtime argument the compiler does not model while still memoising the values fed into it. How often those identities change is how often the GSAP context is disposed, which is the accumulated-`Observer` bug this file already records. `lib/motion.ts` and `components/Sheet.tsx` pass hand-tuned lists and two of those call sites omit `revertOnUpdate` on purpose, so both carry **`'use no memo'`** for the first release. Lift them one file at a time with `npm run net:tabs` and `npm run hero:path` as guardrails.
- `RouteCrossFade` is **not** a risk: it is a class component, which the compiler does not touch.

A bailout is information, not noise — it says that file was not optimised. **Check the components that render most, not only the chunk count**: `ImageCard` (a conditional with a fallback inside its consequent, a shape the compiler cannot lower), `FadeInImage` (a suppressed `exhaustive-deps`) and `MasonryGrid` (a ref captured by an object built during render) were all skipped, so every card re-render rebuilt its whole subtree — and a router update reaches every mounted card through `useHeroLink`'s `useRouter()`, which in this Next reads `LayoutRouterContext`: one `?page=` write was a 1.2s task of fifty cards at 4× CPU. Running `babel-plugin-react-compiler` over a file with a `logger` names the line of each `CompileError`.

## Measuring it

Seven probes beside the four checks, all driving Edge over CDP against the same stubbed upstream as `net:audit`. None asserts; they print, and the numbers in this file come from them.

**Read focus after the frame, not at its rAF.** The overlay returns focus in the first rAF after a close, ahead of that frame's style and paint. A sampler whose rAF runs first reads the document for a frame no one sees (NEW-6, N4). Post a task from the rAF (`MessageChannel`) and read focus there: that is what the frame showed.

| | |
| --- | --- |
| `npm run perf:weight [routes…]` | every `<script src>` a document pulls, raw and brotli |
| `npm run perf:metrics [runs]` | `Performance.getMetrics` deltas for a cold load and a tab switch |
| `npm run perf:hydration [routes…]` | content in the first byte, and console warnings |
| `npm run perf:motion` | the lazy chunks warmed; the indicator glides; the cross-fade clone exists |
| `npm run perf:sw` | the worker controls; what is in its caches; a dead-origin navigation |
| `npm run perf:ripple` | a press wave's computed opacity per frame — peak must be the token, 0.1 |
| `npm run net:tabnav [w] [back] [gap]` | tab tap → open a thread → back; where it lands and which pane shows |
| `npm run perf:pageturn` | a gallery page turn per frame: scroll offset, and what each in-view card shows |
| `npm run perf:sharpness [w]` | the flight's bitmap vs the box it is painted into, on real images |
| `npm run perf:glass [w] [h]` | the /about plate, rendered to a PNG on the CPU — a design instrument, not a check. Every field of its four config objects is sweepable by name (`--refraction=`, `--momentum=`, `--detail=`, `--flank=`, …), plus `--colorA= --colorB= --sheen= --hue=`; `--stroke` walks a cursor through all four ink directions. One documented exception: two config objects declare `speed`, so the reeds' creep is `--fluteSpeed` and the flow's clock is `--swirlSpeed`. Note it renders on the CPU and so never compiles the GLSL — a shader change needs a browser as well |

`perf:sw` stops the **server** rather than emulating offline, and that is not a shortcut: `Network.emulateNetworkConditions` is scoped to the page target and does not reach the service worker's own thread, so the worker's `fetch()` kept succeeding and the first version of that probe reported the app rendering normally "offline".

**The /about glass rests.** `FlutedGlass` creates its programs, buffers and texture objects once per WebGL context. Resizing updates the canvas, resolution uniforms and the backdrop texture’s storage; theme changes update colour uniforms. In 标准 it wakes on input or visibility, waits eight seconds after the last wake, then eases to rest and stops requesting frames. The ink field skips its step and texture upload once empty. 减弱, 关闭 and data saver draw a still frame. Context loss stops drawing until restoration rebuilds the objects. The material, shader formulas and colour roles are unchanged.

## Module boundaries

### GSAP is not in any route's first document

**Measured**: 42–45 KB brotli (126 KB raw) off every one of the twenty-odd routes, verified by listing each document's `<script src>` set and grepping the files for `CustomEase`. Zero routes reach `lib/motion.ts` through a static import; the check is a graph walk over `from '@/…'` / `from './…'` edges with `import type` and `import()` excluded, and it must keep answering **0**. The engine used to arrive even on `/policy` because `lib/motion.ts` registers GSAP and five plugins at module scope, so *any* static import of it pulls the lot.

**Turbopack decides the chunks, not the import graph.** Cutting two shell edges moved the module graph and moved **nothing else**: a lazy import only buys bytes once the *last* static importer is gone. Do not measure this by reading the graph — measure the documents.

Four kinds of fix, and which one applies is decided by what the code actually wanted:

- **It never wanted GSAP.** `getAppScroller`/`heroOwnsScreen` (`lib/appScroller.ts`), `DURATION` and the curve literals (`lib/motionTokens.ts`), `beginPageTransit` and the theme-wipe guard (`lib/pageTransit.ts`), `setTabIntent` (`lib/tabIntent.ts`), the per-tab scroll memory and `applyInstantTabScroll` (`lib/tabScroll.ts`), the reduced-tier spring substitution (`lib/springTiming.ts`). Each is a few lines; between them they were most of the reach — a `querySelector`, a reference count and a one-line setter were putting an animation engine into the shell of every screen.
- **It wanted an animation, but not this engine.** `useSlidingIndicator` (the tab indicator's glide) is `lib/slidingIndicator.ts` on Web Animations; `Reveal`'s cascade is WAAPI; `scrollAppToTop`/`scrollAppToElement` are `lib/scrollTo.ts` on rAF. The tab indicator is the instructive one: four lines of GSAP inside `components/Tabs.tsx`, which `AppLayout` mounts and eight route pages also import. The tab strip and its lean are Web Animations now too (`playSharedAxis`), though they stay in the engine's chunk with the rest of the tab machinery.
- **It is genuinely rare.** `CaptchaModal` and `Sheet` are `dynamic()`. Both needed a *latch* as well: each was rendered unconditionally with an `isOpen={false}`, so `dynamic()` alone would have fetched the chunk at mount. A one-way "has ever opened" flag fixes that and keeps the exit animation, which conditional rendering on `isOpen` would have taken away.
- **It is common but deferrable.** `lib/motionLazy.tsx` — see below.

### Neither is the hero engine

The shell renders on every route and imports **`@/lib/hero/runtime`** only: the runtime store it renders from (phase, direction, image, stage, background) and the calls it forwards (history initialisation, route observation, the Stage's registration, an interrupt). The engine — controller, flight, container transform, geometry solver, history ladder — binds itself to that module when its own module evaluates (`bindImageHeroEngine`), which happens wherever a picture can be opened: a gallery card's `useHeroLink`, the detail route. Until then every forwarded call is the no-flight answer. Checked on the dev server's chunks: /about, /policy and /settings carry the runtime and none of the engine; /, /search and /pic/… carry both (R12-005; confirm on a build with `npm run perf:weight`). Anything the shell reaches — `lib/scrollMemory.ts`, `lib/routeCrossFade.ts`, `lib/useIntentPrefetch.ts`, the editor — imports the runtime (or `lib/hero/input`), never `@/lib/hero`. The engine's module scope runs during a server render too, so the binding and `destroy` are guarded on `window`.

### Nor is Markdown

`react-markdown` and its plugins are in no route's first document (R12-005): `MarkdownRenderer` is a `React.lazy` boundary over `MarkdownDocument`, whose placeholder lays the words out unseen so the formatted text lands without moving what is under it, and `RichTextRenderer` reaches it only for Derpibooru's texts — `format="markdown"`, or `auto` on a text with no BBCode tag in it. PicPony's own texts (posts, replies, site comments) pass `format="bbcode"`: plain text there is a sentence, and a reply opening 「1. 首先」 read as Markdown became a list. The rich-text editor is the same kind of chunk: every caller imports it with `dynamic()` and `EditorPlaceholder`, and the package's stylesheet travels with it.

**A card takes no router of its own.** `useRouter()` reads `LayoutRouterContext` as well as the router, and that context is a new object on every router change, so a component calling it re-renders on every URL write — and `useHeroLink` runs in every gallery card: each `?page=`, `?view=` or `?tab=` write, and every navigation, re-rendered the whole grid (18–31 card renders per `replaceState` on a 50-card home, counted on React's dev Components track; none now). The shell hands its router over once, with the history initialisation, and `useHeroLink` reads it from the runtime at the moment a press or the intent ladder uses it (`getImageHeroNavigation`). Anything rendered once per card follows the same rule: read navigation when it is used, never subscribe to it in render.

### `lib/motionLazy.tsx`, and the rule that makes it safe

Eight entry points behind one dynamic import: `changeScheme`, `changePalette`, `changeCustomPalette`, `changePaletteHues`, `startTabTransition`, `<DrawerSwipe>`, `<TabPanesMotion>`, `<StaggerGrid>`. `warmMotion()` and `warmRouteCrossFade()` are called from the shell inside `runWhenIdle`, so in practice the engine is resident long before any of them is reached.

**Nothing in it awaits inside an event handler**, and that is the whole rule. An `await` before `preventDefault()` loses the gesture; an `await` before a state write puts a frame of nothing on screen. So every entry point does what the **关闭 tier** already does — a real, shipped, documented path — and starts the load in the background:

| Entry point | Fallback before the chunk lands |
| --- | --- |
| `changeScheme` / `changePalette` / `changeCustomPalette` / `changePaletteHues` | the matching `commit*` — the preference is written, it just does not wipe |
| `startTabTransition` | record the outgoing offset and return, which is its own `off` branch line for line |
| `<TabPanesMotion>` | `applyInstantTabScroll`; the panes still swap, on React's `data-tab-pane-active` |
| `<StaggerGrid>` | nothing — an entrance's absent form is the cards being there, i.e. 入场动画 off |
| `<DrawerSwipe>` | nothing — one swipe; the button, the scrim and Escape are not this hook's |
| route cross-fade | `captureRouteSnapshot` returns `null`, so the navigation is a cut |

A user who beats the chunk loses **one** interaction's animation, never the interaction.

**The hooks had to change shape, because a hook cannot live behind an `import()`.** Each is mounted by a child that exists only once the module does, so its hook call is unconditional for its whole life — and that child is the engine's own component, read off the module (`DrawerSwipeHost`, `StaggerGridHost`, the `TabPanesDriver` class), never a hook handed across as a value: the React Compiler refuses a component that calls a hook it received as a prop, and all three of `lib/motionLazy.tsx`'s did (the file compiles whole now). The caller keeps the `useRef` — the ref must exist in its first render, before the engine does — which is why the grid's hook is `useStaggerGridOn(ref, …)`; the tab switch has no hook left. The old two-argument forms are gone rather than kept as wrappers: two ways to call one hook is how their defaults came to disagree.

**And `lib/routeCrossFade.ts` is split rather than lazy-per-call.** The GSAP half is `lib/routeCrossFadePlay.ts`; the front half keeps the snapshot, the layer and the cell map. The gate is in `captureRouteSnapshot`, not in `playRouteCrossFade`, and that ordering is the point: cloning a page that nothing can animate leaves a stale frame frozen over the new one, which is worse than no transition. `lib/forumTransition.ts` is split the same way, and its warm is driven by the gesture — `rememberForumOrigin` *is* a press on a post, and the detail route is a network round trip away, so the chunk is fetched exactly when it is about to be needed.

**`lib/appearance.ts` owns the six appearance preferences** — colour scheme, palette, 配色方案, motion tier, motion speed, entrance animations — and it is the only module that reads or writes them. Four describe the device and stay on it; the palette follows the account, as the original front end's `theme` did, and 配色方案 (`themeHues`) and the custom palette's 副色相 (`themeCustomAccent`) travel with it, being part of what "my theme" means. The settings sync (`lib/settingsSync.ts`) goes through this module both ways — it notices a new stored choice through the subscription and installs the account's with `commitPalette` / `commitCustomPalette` / `commitPaletteHues`. They are one concern with one shape: a stored setting that may say "follow the system", a resolved value on `<html>`, a cookie so the server can put it there before first paint, and one subscription so the app bar's glyph and /settings' dropdowns cannot disagree. Read the **root**, not the store: `motionTier()`, `currentPalette()` and `entranceMotion()` read the attribute, because that is what the CSS is keyed on and therefore what is in force — a stored setting can say `system`, an attribute never does.

It is deliberately GSAP-free. `lib/motion` registers a callback through `setMotionScaleListener` that sets `gsap.globalTimeline.timeScale` — a seam rather than an import, for the same reason `setHeroBusyCheck` is one: this module is reached by anything that reads a preference. The `off` tier is clamped there rather than here, because a `timeScale` of 0 stops the clock instead of collapsing the duration. `changeScheme` / `changePalette` live in `lib/motion` beside `circularReveal`, because what they are is a *wipe* with a one-line preference write inside it.

**`lib/generated/` is script output.** Nothing in it is hand-edited and `npm run colors` fails if it is stale.

**`lib/api.ts`'s `api` is a runtime spread, so it cannot be tree-shaken.** Every importer of it pulls every member, and 39 files import it — including `app/page.tsx`, `ImageCard`, `MasonryGrid` and `FeaturedBanner`. So the admin surface is **not** in it and is **not** re-exported from it: the panels in `components/admin/registry.tsx` import `* as adminApi from '@/lib/api/admin'`, and every panel is already a `dynamic(…, { ssr: false })` chunk. Catalogue and site-tool panels use the same lazy registry; the manual rail reads only a panel once it has been visited, then keeps it mounted for its draft and resource state. (While those 48 functions were spread into `api`, the home page shipped the entire admin console's API layer.) Splitting the rest of the object into named re-exports is still the end state, and the forum is the first part of it: its calls are named exports of `lib/api/forum.ts` (its pure model is `lib/forumModel.ts`, which the server seed reads too), no longer members of `api`. The same reasoning moved `getTeamMembers` out of the admin module: it is a public, tokenless read that `/about` renders.

**`PICPONY_API_BASE` is relative on purpose** — the browser's request has to go through `app/api.php/[[...path]]/route.ts`, which rewrites the backend's `Secure` session cookie so it survives plain HTTP. Node's `fetch` rejects a relative URL, so anything running on the server uses `PICPONY_API_ORIGIN` with it. A server component reaching for the relative form throws `Failed to parse URL`.

**`LS_KEYS` is complete and has to stay that way.** It covered 9 of the 27 keys the app writes, so 32 call sites restated a literal that *was* in the table — 25 of them in the old `app/settings/page.tsx` (66 literals went; the keys /settings shows now sit in one registry, `SYNCED_SETTINGS` in `lib/settingsSync.ts`, each entry naming its `LS_KEYS` key beside the account's), and `AppLayout` goes through `lib/appearance` rather than writing `darkMode` and `followSystemPrefersColorScheme` by hand.

**`COOKIE_KEYS` is its sibling, and it exists because the two sets are not the same.** Six preferences are mirrored into a cookie so `app/layout.tsx` can put them on `<html>` at SSR; without that the first paint is the default theme and the pre-paint script corrects it, which is a visible flash of the wrong brand on every cold load. `darkMode`'s cookie name matches its storage key and `sidebarCollapsed`'s does not, which is precisely why the mapping is a table rather than a derivation. A server-drawn placeholder that depends on a synced setting gets the same treatment from the settings sync: `storedBoolean`'s `cookie` mirrors the value on every install (a change, the account's copy adopted, the reset on signing out) and once when the sync starts, and the screen reads the server's value until hydration has run — /favorites' 隐私空间 tab (`useShowPrivacyTab`), whose placeholder row was drawn with two tabs and hydrated into three (FX-F9).

**`MEDIA` holds every `matchMedia` string, including the two preference queries.** `(prefers-color-scheme: dark)` was hand-typed at three sites and `(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` at four — seven copies of two strings, in the two places where a typo fails silently by never matching.

**Favourites and privacy also use named API exports.** Their adapters are in `lib/api/favorites.ts`; mutations and acknowledged cache changes belong to `lib/favoritesActions.ts`. An acknowledged favourite or share expires that account's task read; never guess its progress locally, and never let a stale session response refresh another account.

## Data requests

**Private favourites have a shorter lifetime than ordinary cached data.** The own list stays in memory and is dropped on lock, expiry and session change, including its image sequence. Presence runs every five seconds only while the space is visible; leaving sends inactive with keepalive. Shared private answers are concealed on hide/leave and rechecked on return. Passwords are never persisted.

**A screen does not fetch. It reads a resource, and the resource decides whether that costs a request.** `lib/resource.ts` is the primitive, `lib/resources.ts` is the catalogue, and `useResource(forumThread, { id })` is the whole of the call site. `npm run net:audit` is the request ledger that argues for the layer: before it, the shell asked twice and re-asked on every navigation, screens re-read what they had just read on a remount, screens read in series when they could read in parallel, and screens read things nobody asked for.

### The two stores, and why they are two

| What | Where | Keyed on |
| --- | --- | --- |
| What the server said | `lib/resource.ts` | the arguments of the read |
| What this screen was showing | `lib/screenState.ts` | a string the screen picks |

`lib/pageCache.ts` was both at once, and using it meant lifting your whole render into one snapshot object — which is why only three components ever adopted it; it is gone, /messages having been the last. Split apart, a remount reads its page number from `useScreenState` and its rows from the resource cache, and paints in the first frame with neither a skeleton nor a request.

`useScreenStateFor` is the same thing scoped to a record. Two profiles are two screens sharing a component, and a flat key carries page 4 of one into the other. Unique is not the same as sufficient.

### Reading a snapshot

`useResource` returns `{ data, error, isLoading, isStale, isPrevious, refresh }`, and **`data` and `isLoading` are independent on purpose**. A cached screen refreshing underneath has both; that is the entire point of the layer. So:

- the *placeholder* branches on `data === undefined`
- a *dim* may branch on `isLoading`
- branching the placeholder on `isLoading` puts a skeleton over content that is already correct, which undoes the reason for having a cache at all

Pass `SKIP` for a read that should not happen yet — an unselected tab, a signed-out visitor, an id that has not resolved. That is the mechanism that stops a screen paying for content nobody asked for.

**`error` is an `ApiError` (`lib/api/errors.ts`), and its `message` is already the sentence to print** under the screen's `{X}加载失败` title — every adapter throws one, a status and a kind on it, the technical detail in `cause`. `isNotFound(error)` picks a not-found state over an error; `isRetryable(error)` decides whether 重试 is offered (a 404, a 400, a query Derpibooru cannot parse and an owner's privacy refusal are not). `apiErrorMessage(anything)` reads any thrown value the same way and never lets an engine error (`TypeError: Failed to fetch`) reach the UI.

**A dead session ends once, in `lib/api/http.ts`, not per screen.** A 401 on a request that carried the token signs out through the token-checked `clearUserInfo` — so an older request can never sign out a newer login — with one 登录已过期，请重新登录. Only `get_user` is taken at its word; any other action's 401 is confirmed by one `get_user` per token first, because an action may answer 401 about a password rather than the session. A PicPony read has a 15s deadline (`timeoutMs` overrides it); a write has none, since aborting one does not stop the server accepting it.

**An answer somebody else changes is re-read, not waited for.** `useResource(r, args, { refetchInterval })` re-reads the key underneath what is on screen while it is mounted — quietly (no loading state; a failure leaves the shown value), jittered ±10%, skipped while the tab is hidden or the device is offline, with one read on return if a tick was missed. A TTL alone never moved a badge that stays mounted. /messages reads this way throughout (decision 13): an open conversation's newest page every 10s, the contact list every 30s while its tab is on screen, the badge the shell's 60s — and reading a list or a conversation writes the lowered count into `unreadCounts` at once, before the re-read confirms it, so the badge does not lag the screen by a minute.

**A paged list must pass `keepPrevious`, and forgetting it is a scroll bug rather than a data one.** Changing the page changes the key, and a key with nothing cached reports `data === undefined` — the rows unmount for one round trip, the scroll container collapses, the browser clamps `scrollTop` to the new tiny maximum, and the page snaps to the very top with the pager's own scroll-to-the-list undone. `node scripts/netAuditScroll.mjs` samples the card count every frame across a page turn and fails on a single empty frame, because one commit is enough. It is off by default because for an unpaged screen it is wrong: showing the *previous* profile while the next one loads is worse than a skeleton. **`isPrevious` says the rows on screen belong to another key** — with `error` set, the page turn *failed*, and the screen must say so rather than leave a pager reading 2 over page 1's pictures.

**Pass a scope string when a paged list changes owner or content filter.** `keepPrevious: true`
retains pages within the resource; a string made from `userId` and `contentFilter` retains only
within that scope. A new profile or filter drops the old answer immediately, including when the
new read fails. Disabling a read with `SKIP` also clears its retained answer. The Derpi uploader
resource puts the filter snapshot in both its key and its actual request, so a preference change
while waiting for route policy cannot store an unfiltered answer under a safe key. A list whose
query carries the viewer's exclusions (the home feed, the uploader grids) scopes retention to the
browsing fingerprint: rows the user just asked not to see must never be held across the change.

### Arriving with the answer: `seed` and `initial`

Five screens render their first read on the server and hand it down: `/` (the gallery page in the address, `?page=` up to 20, and the anonymous 近日推荐 picture beside it), `/?tab=forum` (the forum's first page — the default list, every visitor's landing; `lib/forum.server.ts`, keyed by `lib/forumKeys.ts` — while `/` alone leaves the hidden forum pane to its idle read), `/about` (the team roster), `/user/[id]` (the profile header, not the four tabs — and a user the backend says does not exist is the route's `notFound()`, before anything renders) and a direct `/pic/[id]` (the picture's record, with its tags' glossary names when they land in time). Each has a `.server.ts` module beside it, a server shell that awaits it, and an island that takes it as a prop and passes it to `useResource` as `initial` — except the picture, whose record lives outside the catalogue (below): `PicDetail` installs it with `seedImageDetail`, the same idempotent, browser-only, render-time seam. The overlay presentation reads nothing on the server: it has the list's row, and a server read would sit inside the flight's window.

**The home feed has one server cache: its 120-second process memo.** Its inner fetch is `no-store`. Next's persistent Data Cache can return an expired response while refreshing in the background; stamping that response with a new `generatedAt` made days-old pictures appear fresh to both the memo and the browser. Less-used developer-filter queries made the defect particularly visible. The memo still coalesces concurrent reads and serves prefetches, but an expired entry now waits for a real upstream response within the existing timeout. After a 429 or a 5xx the server leaves Derpibooru alone for 15s (`Retry-After` honoured, 60s cap), for every fingerprint — all SSR reads share one IP. Two feeds get no seed at all: a fingerprint the device could not mirror into its cookie (`browsing=unmirrorable`, which the server once answered with the *default* feed, hidden pictures included) and the random sort; nor does 本站讨论 (`?view=discussed`) or a deep link to the forum tab, whose hidden gallery reads for itself once the browser is idle. The seed's rows are raw `derpicdn.net` URLs; the page puts them on the document's image line with `seedOnImageLine` (the featured row with `featuredOnImageLine`). Both seeds' keys come from `lib/feedKeys.ts`, which the resources use too.

**The seam is `resource.seed(args, value, fetchedAt)`.** Both `seed` and `write` build a resolved `Entry` directly: a cold write must not call `create`, because `enqueue` → `pump` → `job.run()` starts a fetch synchronously. `seed` additionally preserves the server's timestamp and publishes its first snapshot synchronously; ordinary writes retain the existing entry's age and publish on a paint boundary. A write replaces the old entry and cancels its work, so an older response cannot undo a successful mutation. Imperative `read()` returns the current value, including after a write or background refresh; an already-settled first-read promise is not a mutable cache.

Four details of it are load-bearing:

- **The snapshot is built synchronously, not through `publish()`.** `publish` is rAF-bound and hydration renders before the next frame — a seed that went through it would arrive one frame late, which is exactly the skeleton flash it exists to remove.
- **`args` is set.** It is what `expire()` and `bindResourceRefresh` re-read from; a seed without it is invisible to "the tab came back after a minute".
- **`fetchedAt` is `Math.min(generatedAt, Date.now())`.** An RSC payload can be minutes old, and stamping it "just fetched" would pin stale HTML for a whole TTL. The clamp only ever errs toward *stale*, never toward *fresh*.
- **It is a no-op on the server** (`typeof window === 'undefined'`). `lib/resource.ts` is a `'use client'` module, and such a module is still *evaluated in Node* during SSR — so its module-scope `store` is shared across concurrent requests. Seeding there would hand one visitor's feed to another's render.

**The keys have to agree on both sides, and key functions must be pure.** `homeFeed` used to read the browsing fingerprint out of `localStorage` *inside* its key function — a hydration mismatch the moment content comes from the key. It takes `{page, sort, fp}` now, and `COOKIE_KEYS.browsing` mirrors `browsingFingerprint()`'s own output so the server can compute the same string. The island renders its first frame with the **server's** fingerprint and switches to the device's after mount: identical, and there is no mismatch; different, and the key changes once, costing one request — over a skeleton rather than the server's rows, which were filtered for other settings (the feed's retention is scoped to the fingerprint). `DEFAULT_BROWSING_FINGERPRINT` is what an *absent* cookie means — without it the server keyed on `''` while the browser keyed on `safe|-|d|-|`, and the seed never applied.

**The site's public blacklist is part of every Derpibooru image search**, in every content mode including 开发者: `buildSearchQuery` appends `-id:N` for the newest hundred ids and `searchImagesByIds` drops them, so a listed picture reaches no feed, search result, profile tab or favourites list. The featured banner and a picture opened by its own link are not searches and the site's list is not applied to them (the banner does apply the device's own content settings, after the read — `lib/imageFilters.ts` — and wears the grid's spoiler cover). The server reads it (`readPublicBlacklist`, anonymous, five minutes, invalidated by `/api.php` when an administrator edits the list) and inlines it with the route policy; the browsing fingerprint includes it, so the server seed and the first client key still agree.

**What does not move, and why.** The *keyed* `featuredImage` read stays on the client: `getFeatured` puts the user's Derpibooru API key in the URL, so a shared server cache would leak it. (The anonymous one is seeded — `readFeatured` — and a visitor with a key re-reads over it, the seeded picture held meanwhile.) Personal reads for `/favorites`, `/history`, `/tasks` (including `/tasks/coins`), `/block-groups`, `/tag-groups` and /messages' account-only panes are token-gated and the token is in `localStorage`, which the server cannot read — moving them means moving auth into a cookie, which is a security decision rather than a performance one. `/search` reads `searchParams` and would explode the cache key. On `/` the forum pane is already `api: 0` on tap, mounted on an idle callback, so a seed there would only add bytes for the majority who never open it — only a deep link to `/?tab=forum` is seeded. **A thread is never read on the server**, not even for its title: `get_forum_post_detail` counts a view per read (measured), so a server read would count every visit twice; the thread names the document itself once its read lands (`useDocumentTitle`, which holds the title against the metadata Next resolves after the page mounts).

### Speculation may move a request earlier. It may never add one.

That is the rule, and `npm run net:audit` asserts it: every journey's request count may fall and may not rise. Three consequences:

- **Prefetch is intent-driven, never idle.** `useIntentPrefetch` is `useHeroLink`'s ladder with the gallery card taken out of it — hover at 70ms, focus at 120ms, a mouse or pen press immediately and a finger's press 100ms later (the tap timeout: a flick that becomes a scroll fires `pointercancel` and warms nothing), cancel on leave, gated by `isScrollLikelyActive()`. `prefetchRoute` maps a path to the reads that path starts, in one table rather than scattered across the components that link there. **A forum thread is the exception to the ladder**: its read counts a view on the server, so it is warmed on a press only (`ForumPostList`), never on a hover or a focus, `prefetchRoute` does not warm `/forum/<id>`, and the thread's pager prefetches nothing. The 本站讨论 tab warms its own read on the same ladder (two round trips — the discussed ids, then the pictures — so the tap usually lands on rows); `Tabs` takes no handlers per tab, so they are delegated from a wrapper with no box. The idle reads are a stated exception: the home route's hidden pane mounts on an idle callback — the forum behind the gallery, so a tap on 论坛 costs nothing (the `get_forum_posts` in the ledger's cold home journey), and the gallery behind a deep link to the forum, which used to read with the visible forum rather than after it.
- **The obvious pagination win is not taken.** Warming page *n+1* as soon as page *n* settles was written and removed: it is a request for a page that may never be looked at, on every paged screen. What survives is the intent ladder on the controls, which buys the same head start and costs nothing until somebody reaches for them. The viewer is the one place it is taken, and only at the end of the run: a step that lands within four pictures of the loaded list's end, in the direction it is going, reads the list's next page (`PAGE_READ_AHEAD` in `lib/hero/useDetailStep.ts`, behind the same `saveData` switch). Somebody stepping through pictures one at a time is walking toward that page — the step past the last one would read it anyway — and without the head start every page boundary stopped a run of flicks for a round trip on the Derpibooru lane.
- **Guessing cannot starve a real read.** Each upstream has its own four slots — `lane: 'picpony' | 'derpi'` on `defineResource`, and the opened picture (`lib/detail.ts`) shares Derpibooru's — so a slow or rate-limited Derpibooru cannot leave the forum or the badge with nothing in flight; background work is capped at two of a lane's four, so a prefetch can never take the last one. A first read the screen has moved off (four page turns in a row, an unmount) is abandoned so the page now on screen gets the slot. `saveData` and the two slowest `effectiveType`s switch speculation off entirely — the concurrency cap protects latency, and nothing but that switch protects *bytes*.

**A `<Link>` prefetch is not a data prefetch.** Next warms the RSC payload and the chunk when the link is in the viewport, which is real and is not the expensive half: every screen here is a client component that starts its reads in its first effect, so a warm chunk still arrives at an empty page.

### Coming back

`bindResourceRefresh`, mounted once in the shell, re-reads what is on screen when the tab returns after a minute away and whenever the network reconnects. It **expires rather than invalidates**, and that is the whole difference between a refresh and a reload: every mounted screen keeps what it is showing and re-reads underneath, with no loading state. Dropping the entries instead would empty every screen in the app in one frame.

`expire` also has to *start* the re-read rather than merely mark: `useResource`'s effect is keyed on the resource and the key, and neither changes when a value goes stale. The entry holds its own args for this.

`invalidate` preserves mounted subscribers and re-reads their keys with the answer still on screen, starting a microtask later — so a mutation that invalidates and then `write`s its own authoritative answer in the same tick sends nothing it would only abort; deleting the listener slot would leave an unchanged key permanently empty. A background refresh moves `fetchedAt` only when it lands: one the queue dropped or that failed leaves the answer stale, so the next read tries again. `clearAllResources` is separate: signing out aborts and drops every entry without issuing requests for the old account. `node scripts/testResources.mjs` checks these distinctions and the races between reads, refreshes and writes.

### Two mistakes this layer made, both worth not repeating

**A subscriber arrives before the reader.** `useSyncExternalStore` subscribes during render and the read happens in the effect a tick later, so every mounted component creates a listener slot for a key nothing has requested; it must not be confused with a real queued entry (a resolved promise that carries nothing — every migrated screen went silent at once). `Entry.placeholder` is the distinction.

**And the ledger called it a triumph.** The home page "improved" from five requests to one, with the gallery empty behind it, because the only assertion was an upper bound. An upper bound cannot tell an optimisation from a breakage. `net:audit` now also holds a floor — a step that read something before must still read something, and a step whose own content request was identified before must still identify it.

### `npm run net:audit`

Drives Edge over CDP through a fixed set of journeys and counts requests per step. It asserts **counts** and the **round** of each step's own content request; wall-clock timings are printed and asserted nowhere, which is the same split `palette.mjs` draws between a `floor` pair and a `report` pair.

The upstream is **stubbed** (`netAuditFixtures.mjs`), and not because it is down: `proxyFetch`'s retry ladder turns one logical read into one *or* three depending on which line is reachable, so the count stops being a property of the code. `--live` runs against the real thing for a payload-shape check and refuses to write a baseline. A second fixture server is handed to `next start` through `PICPONY_UPSTREAM_ORIGIN`, because CDP intercepts the *browser* and reaches nothing the server does — without it the SSR-side policy read could not be measured at all.

Two numbers it reports and does not assert: the maximum `rounds` over a step (an idle-scheduled request inherits depth it does not owe), and chrome reads — `get_user`, `get_unread_counts`, `get_announcement`, and signed in `get_tag_subscriptions` (the drawer's 标签订阅 count) — are excluded from the rounds chain entirely, because they fire on every screen in parallel and were making `contentRound` race. They are still counted.

One request is not counted at all, on either layer: `track_visitor`, the visit count a production build sends (`components/VisitorTracker.tsx`) — a statistics POST on an idle callback that nothing waits on, whose landing inside a step's window is timing rather than code. The tracker itself stands down where the probes run: never in development (the dev server proxies to the real backend), never under `navigator.webdriver`, and never in a headless browser — `net:*` and `perf:*` drive headless Edge over plain CDP, where `webdriver` is false (the service worker's blind spot, in its section above) but the user agent says `HeadlessChrome`. A signed-in visitor is never counted either, as in the original front end.

**A third column, `srv`, and a third round value, `r0`.** Once a read moves to the server, CDP cannot see it — from the ledger's point of view the request vanished, which is indistinguishable from the screen having stopped loading, precisely what the floor exists to catch. So the fixture server that `PICPONY_UPSTREAM_ORIGIN` points at now tallies what the Next server asks it for. `srv` is that count, `r0` means "this screen's own read was already in the first byte", and the two assertions split by what is reproducible:

- **The ceiling is applied twice** — to browser requests, which is what a user waits on, and to `api + srv`, because "speculation may move a request earlier, never add one" is a rule about requests and not about which process sends them.
- **The floor counts both layers**, and asks only "did this step read anything, anywhere". A step that reads nothing on either side is a screen that has stopped loading.

`get_maintenance_status` is excluded from `srv` for the same reason `get_user` is excluded from the rounds chain: the server does it on every route, including ones that read nothing. `get_public_blacklist`, read beside it for the same inline policy, is excluded with it.

Two harness facts make `srv` assertable, both load-bearing: the tally is cleared before each step (the `r0` classification tests that list — a stale tally would mark a later screen's content as "already in the first byte", the one failure the floor exists to catch), and Next's Data Cache persists to disk (`.next/cache/fetch-cache`) — and, contrary to what this file once said, an explicit `next: { revalidate }` is **not** vetoed by an upstream `Cache-Control: no-store`; the veto applies to the default heuristic, not to an explicit instruction. `PICPONY_SERVER_MEMO_TTL_MS=0` is what the harness sets, and it reaches **both** caches through `cacheSeconds` (`lib/serverMemo.ts`) — the memo and the `revalidate` argument share one number. It has to be both or it is neither: either cache alone serves the second journey to open `/` from memory, and from outside a cache hit and a screen that has stopped loading are the same observation.

**In-flight coalescing is not caching and survives the switch.** `/user/[id]` has two callers per request — `generateMetadata` and the page — and Next renders them concurrently, so a slot that is only written on resolution has both miss and both fetch. The memo joins an **unsettled** slot whatever the TTL says, which is de-duplication rather than staleness.

**A server-side read is reached by `<Link>` prefetching, not only by a visit.** The footer links to /about from every route, Next prefetches the RSC payload of every visible link, and rendering that payload runs the read — so without a cache in front of it, one page view *anywhere* becomes one upstream fetch. All three `.server.ts` reads go through `createServerMemo`, and any future one should too.

### What is not in the catalogue

**The opened picture.** `lib/detail.ts` holds that one, and it is where this layer's machinery came from: TTL, LRU, priority queue, concurrency cap, real cancellation and a paint-bound notification tuned so a response can never land inside a hero flight's geometry frame. It stays separate because its publication is gated on `imageHeroController.isDetailDataPublishable`; `ResourceOptions.publishGate` is the seam that would let it fold in, and folding it in has not been done. Its queue is no longer its own: it schedules on the resource layer's Derpibooru lane (`scheduleLaneJob`), so an opened picture and a gallery page compete for one set of slots instead of each assuming it has four. A failed read's error is kept for `peekImageDetailError`: an open overlay subscribed to a record that failed used to see exactly what it saw while loading, and kept its skeletons forever.

**Persistence.** The store is a `Map` and a reload genuinely reloads, which is `lib/screenState.ts`'s rule too. `lib/tagCounts.ts` and `lib/tagTranslations.ts` keep their own localStorage caches on week-long TTLs; that is a different problem, and keeping it separate is what stops this from growing a quota policy.

### Settings follow the account

**One module, on every screen.** `lib/settingsSync.ts` holds the registry, `SYNCED_SETTINGS`: each entry names its storage key, its key or keys in the account's object (the original front end's names, which are the contract), a fallback and a scope. `components/SettingsSync.tsx` starts the engine from the root layout once the app has mounted, not while a module is evaluated, because the Node suites import `lib/resources.ts` under stubbed globals. From then on every `get_user` answer adopts the account's copy through `sessionUser`'s fetch: at sign-in, on each load and on each return to the tab. A change made here (`changeSyncedSetting`, or `noteSettingChanged` for a value its own module wrote) is written after a 600ms pause, so a run of toggles costs one request. Four rules hold it up, and each one is a bug this app shipped:

- **The write is the whole object.** `update_settings` replaces what is stored; it does not merge. The old /settings sent `{ [key]: value }` per toggle, and the accounts it reached were left holding that one key. The engine sends the last cloud copy with this device's changes laid over it, so keys it does not manage ride through untouched. A copy older than a minute is read again first.
- **A newer local change is never overwritten by an older cloud copy.** Pending changes are persisted per account (`LS_KEYS.settingsSyncPending`), and a read that started before a change cannot speak for it.
- **A failed write keeps the local value and says so once:** a toast, plus 有设置尚未同步到账号 with 重试 on /settings. It retries on reconnect, on return to the tab and on the next change, backing off to five minutes.
- **Scope is part of the entry.** A `device` entry describes how this device browses (filter, sorts, lines, palette). It survives signing out, and fills in a key the account lacks. An `account` entry is a fact about the account: what the profile shows, 隐藏 IP 属地 (the backend itself answers the profile's `ip_location` with 该用户已隐藏) and the email notifications. It resets on sign-out and is never filled in from the device.

The scheme, the motion tier, the speed and entrance motion stay on the device; the palette syncs as `theme` (plus `themeCustomSeed` and `themeCustomAccent`, written out as `null` for 自动 so an older hue cannot ride through), and 配色方案 beside it as `themeHues` (`node --test scripts/testPalette.mjs` covers the two). 中等限制 needs a saved birthday of 16 or more on the Beijing calendar, and 开发者模式 needs developer mode. That gate is enforced on sign-out, on every account read and after a profile save, so a birthday typed and cancelled unlocks nothing. To add a setting, add one registry entry; nothing in /settings, the shell or the resource layer changes. `node --test scripts/testSettingsSync.mjs` covers these rules.

## Request lines

**A line is not a user preference — it is a policy the server pushes.** `api.php?action=get_maintenance_status` carries four fields besides the maintenance ones, and an administrator uses them to pin every visitor to one Derpibooru API line and one image line. `lib/route.ts` owns all of it. When the policy is anything but `auto`, /settings says so instead of offering the user's own toggles. Its 当前线路 row names the pinned lines, badged 全站强制, and the switches of a pinned axis are not shown: four switches that could never be used were noise on every visit (R7-027). So "the line switches are gone" is this feature *working*, not a bug.

**Maintenance is the same document's other half, and it is rendered by the server.** When `maintenance_mode` is on, `app/layout.tsx` renders `MaintenanceScreen` (with `maintenance_message`) in place of the shell, so a visitor's first byte is the notice and no route starts a read. Staff keep the app, with a row in the chrome saying so. Who is staff is the backend's `is_admin` for the session's token, which the server cannot send — so it goes by `COOKIE_KEYS.maintenanceStaff`, a hint the client writes from that answer and corrects in both directions (`components/MaintenanceScreen.tsx`). `lib/maintenance.server.ts` reads the document at the route policy read's URL and options, so the two share one Data Cache entry, and fails open. In development a `devMaintenancePreview` cookie shows it on one browser without touching the shared backend's switch.

**Two axes, and they are independent.** The API policy rewrites Derpibooru `/api/` calls; the image policy rewrites derpicdn images. PicPony's own `/api.php`, its avatars and its banners never change host, so `PICPONY_API_BASE`, `getAssetUrl`'s host and the proxy route's upstream are not part of this at all.

| API line | URL |
| --- | --- |
| `direct` | `trixiebooru.org/api/v1/json/…` unchanged |
| `api_accel` | `PROXY_API_BASE` + the encoded `derpibooru.org` URL — a Worker, `GET`/`HEAD`/`OPTIONS` only |
| `picpony_api` | our own `/relay?url=…&xp_user=…` |
| `third_party` | an admin-supplied origin, with the Derpibooru path and search copied onto it |

| Image line | URL |
| --- | --- |
| `direct` | `derpicdn.net/…` unchanged |
| `cdn` | `IMAGE_CDN_BASE` + encoded |
| `picpony` | `IMAGE_WORKER_BASE` + encoded, plus a thumbnail marker |

**Every line is applied per request, over the canonical URL.** `DERPIBOORU_API_BASE` stays what it is and `buildApiLineUrl` rewrites at call time. The alternative — making the base constants mutable — cannot work: `export const '…'` is a string literal that a bundler is free to fold into all 140 call sites, so reassigning the module binding changes nothing.

**`proxyFetch` awaits `ensureRoutePolicy()` on every call**, and that await is the whole guarantee. Resolved, it costs a microtask; unresolved, it is what stops the first request of a cold load from going out on the default host while the site is pinned somewhere else. It is the old frontend's `window._maintenanceReady` moved somewhere it cannot be got wrong — a React boundary would depend on mount order. It **never rejects**: any failure leaves the `auto` defaults in place, because a rejection there would lock every Derpibooru request in the app behind one dead fetch. And it **never holds a request past 2s** for a policy the document did not carry: past that the request goes out on the defaults and the policy re-routes everything after it.

**A forced line is not ours to leave, and neither is the relay.** Under a forced policy — or on `picpony_api` under `auto` — a failure is retried in place, three times, and then thrown. Falling quietly back to direct is what would make 全站强制 meaningless, and the relay in particular exists for the visitor whose direct connection does not work. The same rule holds for images: a forced image policy has no ladder, so `resolveNextAttempt` converges on the named line — including *snapping to* it, since an `<img>` built before the policy landed starts on the wrong tier and retrying that tier in place would never reach the forced one.

**What that costs, stated because it is the sharp edge of the design.** `useHongKongRelay` defaults on, so `auto` resolves to the relay for anyone who has not touched the switch — and the relay does not fail over. If it is down, those visitors get an error even where direct works. The cascade that is actually reachable is therefore **direct → accel → direct-with-cooldown**, and only for users who turned the relay off; cooldown is 30s, or 10 minutes on a 403/503. `auto` leaves the backup line only once a one-tag probe proves direct answers again — first after 10s, the wait doubling on every failed probe up to 10 minutes — and each API line notice is shown once per session; the blind timed revert it replaced put a blocked visitor back on failing requests, and a snackbar pair, every forty seconds. `stepApiFailover`'s relay step is unreachable for the same reason and says so where it sits.

**Only a network throw and the proxy statuses retry**, and three exclusions are load-bearing. A **cancellation** is not a failure of anything: retrying re-`fetch`es an aborted signal, and on the `auto` cascade it would announce two line switches because the pointer left a gallery card. **429** is left out of the failover list that the old frontend includes — a rate limit is counted against the caller, so moving to a shared worker spreads one visitor's limit onto every visitor of that line, and it hid the limit behind a line-switch notice; a 429 now surfaces as itself, 请求过于频繁，请稍后再试. A **403 on a request that carried a key** is about the key: no other line answers it differently, so failing over spends six requests and two snackbars on a revoked credential. Note that `readJson` turns a dead line into `{ success: false }` rather than an exception, which is why the decision is made on `res.ok` and the status inside `proxyFetch`. **A GET resolves only with an OK response**; everything else is an `ApiError` carrying the last status, including a ladder that ran out. Each attempt has a 12s deadline and the whole ladder a 25s budget, pauses included — there were none, and the relay's three in-place attempts could hold a skeleton, and a scheduler slot, for ~91s. A 400 on a search the user typed is `kind: 'syntax'` — the query's fault, so no 重试.

**The image line is applied in the data layer, once.** `applyImageLine` runs inside `lib/api/derpi.ts` for every image-bearing response, not at the screens — the featured banner, the opened picture and both profile grids render their URLs directly, so a policy applied only in the two gallery maps never reached them. It is idempotent (strip the wrapper, then apply the current one), which is what makes the surviving call sites harmless and what lets a forced `direct` policy unwrap a URL that arrived pre-wrapped.

**`/relay` exists because the relay checks `Origin`.** `cdn.picpony.top/relay` allows `picpony.top` and `www.picpony.top` and 403s everything else, so the browser cannot use that line from this app's origin at all. `app/relay/route.ts` makes the hop server-side, where the `Origin` is ours to set. It forwards `GET`/`HEAD` only and validates protocol, host, port, credentials **and path** — **that check is the security of the endpoint**, not a tidiness check: without the path restriction a bare `?url=https://derpibooru.org/` serves third-party HTML *from this origin* on an unauthenticated GET, in the origin that holds the session token, because the upstream `content-type` is echoed through. A 3xx is rejected rather than passed on: the browser would follow it back to the host whose `Origin` check this route exists to satisfy. `xp_user` is bounded and charset-checked because it is unauthenticated — it is the relay's per-user accounting label and anyone can claim any value for it.

**`/admin/import-tools/*` admits a body only from an administrator.** It cannot stream — it rebuilds the PHP's form field by field — so it holds one body at a time, and that slot is taken only after one `get_user` with the caller's own bearer says administrator (`verifyAdmin`: per token, a pass for 60s, a refusal for 5s, an unknown answer never; an 8s deadline; fails closed with 503). A body with no chunk for 20s, or averaging under 32 KB/s after 30s, is abandoned (408), and a disconnect releases the slot at once.

**Every document refuses to be framed by another origin.** `next.config.ts`'s `headers()` sends `frame-ancestors 'self'` (with `X-Frame-Options` for older engines), `nosniff` and a `strict-origin-when-cross-origin` referrer on everything except `/api.php`, `/relay` and `/share.php`, which set their own sandboxing policy on the response and must not be given a second one — the config's policy does not add to a handler's own, it *replaces* it (measured on `/share.php` before it was excluded: only `frame-ancestors 'self'` survived), so a proxy left inside the rule loses its sandbox; `X-Powered-By` is off, and the proxies drop the upstream's `server` and `x-powered-by`. `/share.php` (the short links) proxies only `?action=create`, whose answer is JSON, and sends a visited `?id=` on to PicPony's own page rather than serving that HTML — built from a title anybody can store — from the origin that holds the session token. None of it touches an image line: the rules govern who may frame *this* origin and what it leaks, not what it may load.

**`proxy.ts` exists for one legacy address and must not grow into middleware.** The original front end's thread share link was `/?post=N`; the proxy's matcher is `/` *with a `post` query* and nothing else, so no other request runs it, and it answers `/forum/N`. It is a proxy rather than a `next.config.ts` redirect because a config redirect passes the request's query on — that answered `/forum/5?post=5`. Links to that form inside a post already route in the client (`inAppHref`).

**Three things were quietly wrong before and are worth not reintroducing.** `usePicponyProxy` is the *image* worker's toggle, and it was gating the API proxy as well, so one switch steered both pipelines. The fourth line — `picpony_hk_relay`, the relay, and the old frontend's *default* — was missing entirely, so this app's out-of-the-box line was the accel Worker while the old one had always been the relay. And the image degrade constants had drifted from the live ones (a 30s window against 10s, one 60s recovery against 30s and 15s) with no CDN-versus-direct race at all, so the `raceWinner` rung in `resolveImageLine` had nothing to read.

**The image probes use an `Image()`, not a `fetch`.** These hosts are image proxies, so a decoded bitmap is the only evidence that means "this line works". A no-cors `HEAD` yields an opaque response that resolves on a 500 as readily as on a 200, so a dead line probed as healthy undid its own degrade. The race is lazy: it runs when an image has actually failed and after a recovery probe comes back down, never at boot. Both hosts take the same three-distinct-URLs-in-10s threshold — the CDN used to be dropped on its first failure, and one deleted picture 404s on every line.

**Two things to know rather than fix.** When the server sets `global_api_third_party_pass_api_key`, the user's Derpibooru API key is forwarded in the query string to the third-party origin. That is the old frontend's behaviour and the flag is the server's to set, so this app follows it — but it is a credential leaving for a host neither end controls, and the `false` branch (which strips the key) has to keep working. And the key transits *our* server too: `getFeatured` and identity detection put `key=` in the URL, and on the relay line that whole URL becomes a query parameter of `/relay`, so it lands in this server's access log as well as the relay's.
